<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological timing (Muhurta) from the previous section, specifically focusing on the restrictions imposed by the position of Jupiter (Guru) in the zodiac.</meta>

# Muhurta Chintamani (The Jewel of Auspicious Timing)

Whatever activities are forbidden during the combustion <note>astronomical disappearance due to proximity to the Sun</note> of Jupiter are also forbidden when Jupiter is in the signs of Leo (Simha) and Capricorn (Makara). However, according to the opinions of other teachers, there is no fault in undertaking pilgrimages to Gaya or the Godavari River during these times. Some teachers hold that the aforementioned activities are also forbidden during the retrograde <note>vakra</note> or accelerated <note>atichara</note> motion of Jupiter, but only for a period of 28 days. Furthermore, another school of thought suggests that if Jupiter is positioned in the 5th, 9th, 7th, 2nd, or 11th house from one's birth sign in the current transit, then the faults of retrograde or accelerated motion do not apply. 

Additionally, these activities are forbidden during "Guru-Aditya" <note>Jupiter-Sun conjunction</note>, which occurs when Jupiter and the Sun occupy the same sign. Some define "Guru-Aditya" as the Sun being in Jupiter's sign or Jupiter being in the Sun's sign, but the primary view is the former. Furthermore, in a "Vishvaghasra Paksha"—a fortnight where two lunar dates (tithis) are omitted, resulting in a thirteen-day fortnight—the aforementioned activities are forbidden. One should also avoid wearing ornaments made of ivory or precious gems during these faulty periods (such as Jupiter in Leo). ॥ 48 ॥

->Marriage is not desired when Jupiter is in Leo or in the Leo navamsha <note>a specific 3°20' subdivision of the sign</note>. This fault applies specifically to the region between the north of the Godavari and the south bank of the Ganges (Bhagirathi); it does not apply to other regions, nor when the Sun is in Aries. ॥ 49 ॥<-

The remedies for the fault of Jupiter in Leo are described in three ways: First, the fault of Jupiter in Leo applies only to marriage and (according to some) the thread ceremony (Vratabandha), but not to other activities. Second, the fault does not apply to the entire duration of Jupiter in Leo, but specifically when it is in the Leo subdivision (from 13° 20' to 16° 40' of the sign). Third, the fault is restricted geographically to the lands between the Godavari and the Ganges; it does not exist in other regions. Furthermore, when the Sun is in Aries (during the solar month of Vaishakha), this fault is not considered anywhere. ॥ 49 ॥

->Jupiter is considered censurable everywhere when it is in the first five quarters <note>padas</note> starting from the constellation Magha. ॥ 50 ॥<-
->Except for the region between the Ganges and Godavari, it causes no fault in the remaining quarters. ॥ 50 ॥<-
->When the Sun is in Aries, marriage and thread ceremonies are auspicious even between the Ganges and Godavari. ॥ 51 ॥<-
->Jupiter in any part of Leo is to be avoided in the regions of Kalinga, Gauda, and Gurjara. ॥ 51 ॥<-

To reinforce the previous opinion: as long as Jupiter remains in the first five quarters—the four quarters of Magha and the first quarter of Purva Phalguni—it is considered inauspicious in all countries. In the remaining quarters (the last three of Purva Phalguni and the first of Uttara Phalguni), it is forbidden only in the lands situated between the Ganges and the Godavari, and not elsewhere. ॥ 50 ॥ Furthermore, even if Jupiter is in Leo, if the Sun is in Aries, then marriage and thread ceremonies are auspicious even in the lands between the Ganges and Godavari. However, Jupiter in Leo is strictly avoided in the regions of Kalinga <note>modern Odisha</note>, Gauda <note>modern Bengal</note>, and Gurjara <note>modern Gujarat</note>, regardless of other factors. ॥ 51 ॥

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological timing and geographical exceptions for Jupiter's transit through Capricorn. It follows a section likely detailing the general prohibitions of a "debilitated" Jupiter.</meta>

# Chapter 1: Including the Language Commentary

->(Shalini Meter) East of the Reva, west of the Gandaki, north and south of the Shon—here the debilitated Jupiter is acceptable.<-
->He is not to be avoided in Konkan or Magadha; however, in Gauda and Sindh, he must be avoided for auspicious rites. || 52 ||<-

The remedy for the defects of Jupiter in Capricorn <note>original: "Makar"</note> is described in two ways. In the lands to the east of the **Reva** <note>The Narmada River, flowing from Amarkantak in the south, through Jabalpur, the Vindhya slopes, Hoshangabad, Omkareshwar, Mandleshwar, and Maheshwar, meeting the Gulf of Khambhat near Bharuch and the western sea near Dwarka</note>; and to the west of the **Gandaki** <note>Flowing from Muktinath in the Nepal Himalayas to meet the Ganges at Harihar Kshetra near Patna; the "West of Gandaki" extends from here to Mount Man or the Saraswat region, i.e., the western sea north of Dwarka</note>; and regarding the **Shon River** <note>Flowing from Amarkantak through the Vindhyas to meet the Ganges between Arrah and Maner</note>—in the lands to its south including Udela, Surguja, Lohardaga, Rohtas, Sagad, Bihar, etc., and to its north including Baghelkhand, Prayagraj (Allahabad), Awadh, Rohilkhand, Delhi (Indraprastha), Agra, Mathura, Nadinath, Jwalamukhi, up to the northern Himalayas—in these regions, the defect of Jupiter being in Capricorn does not apply. 

Furthermore, in **Konkan** <note>140 miles south of Mumbai along the seacoast</note>, **Gauda** <note>Gauda-Bengal, Malda, Purnia, Lakshmanavati, Jannatabad</note>, **Magadha** <note>Gaya and Patna districts</note>, and **Sindh** <note>The region between Attock and Jhelum, called Sindh Sagar</note>—in these lands, auspicious works are forbidden. According to other texts, in lands other than these two categories, the prohibition lasts for 60 days. Additionally, some hold that only the specific Capricorn portion <note>Navamsha</note> is forbidden, not the entire transit through Capricorn, nor is it forbidden for all countries. || 52 || 

Regarding this subject, in the year 1890 AD (Samvat 1946), prompted by the provocation of certain envious persons, I published a critique of this matter in the newspapers. In response, I received a formal "Letter of Victory" <note>Vijaypatra</note> regarding the decision from 64 learned scholars <note>Shastris</note> of Kashi (Varanasi), in which the aforementioned meaning was established through numerous proofs. || 52 ||

->(Vanshastha Meter) When Jupiter, moving with <term>excessive speed</term> <note>Atichara: an accelerated astrological movement</note> through signs other than Taurus, Aries, Pisces, or Aquarius, enters the next sign and does not return to the previous sign via retrograde motion, then a "Lost Year" occurs. This is highly condemned for auspicious rites in the region between the Reva and the Ganges. || 53 ||<-

Except for the signs of Taurus, Aries, Pisces, and Aquarius, if Jupiter moves with <term>excessive speed</term> (completing a sign in ten or eleven months) into the next sign and, after becoming <term>retrograde</term> <note>Vakra: appearing to move backward</note> for some days, does not return to the previous sign, that year is called a <term>Lost Year</term> <note>Lupta Samvatsara</note>. This is considered extremely inauspicious for performing sacred rites. However, if this accelerated movement occurs in signs 1, 2, 11, or 12 <note>Aries, Taurus, Aquarius, or Pisces</note>, the defect of the <term>Lost Year</term>...

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological timing and geographical calculations from the Muhurta Chintamani, specifically focusing on regional exceptions for planetary defects and the calculation of the planetary hour (Hora).</meta>

<page-num>22</page-num>
-># Muhurta Chintamani <-
<note>The "Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments," a classic 16th-century Sanskrit text on electional astrology.</note>

...does not occur. There is an exemption based on regional differences: the defect of the "lost year" <note>original: "lupta samvatsara"; a year omitted in certain calendrical calculations</note> applies only to the lands between the Reva (Narmada) and the Bhagirathi (Ganges) rivers, and not elsewhere. According to the opinions of other teachers, even when Jupiter and Venus are in the same seventh sign <note>directly opposite each other</note>, it is considered as defective as a planetary combustion in those specific regions. || 53 ||

->### (Upajati Meter) <-
The entry of the day-lord <note>the planet ruling the day</note> occurs earlier or later than midday based on the distance in *yojanas* <term>Yojana: a traditional measure of distance, roughly 8-9 miles</term> east or west of the prime meridian, adjusted by the calculated minutes. || 54 ||

Imagine a straight line tied from Lanka <note>the traditional zero-point for Indian longitude</note> to Mount Meru <note>the North Pole</note>; the countries falling directly beneath it lie on the prime meridian. The distance of any place from this line is measured in *yojanas*, known as the longitudinal distance <note>original: "deshantara yojana"</note>. To find the start of the astrological day: subtract one-fourth from these *yojanas*. If the location is west of the meridian, add this to 15; if east, subtract it from 15. On the day you wish to find the "day-entry" <note>the exact moment the planetary ruler of the day takes over</note>, compare this adjusted longitude to the midday value. If the result is greater than 15, subtract 15; if less, subtract it from 15. This determines the progression. It should be remembered: if the calculated figure is less than 15 after the midday adjustment, the day-entry occurs after sunrise by those minutes; if the calculated figure is less than 15, the entry is before sunrise. 

**Example:** The city of Kashi (Varanasi) is 63 *yojanas* east of the prime meridian (Kurukshetra). Subtracting a quarter leaves 47. Since it is east, we subtract 47 from 15 <note>using sexagesimal/traditional units</note>, resulting in 14;13. Since the midday value is 17;2, we subtract 14;13, leaving 2;49. Because the calculated figure is less than the midday value, the day-entry will occur 2 *ghatis* and 49 *palas* <note>approx. 67 minutes</note> after sunrise. || 54 ||

->### (Anushtubh Meter) <-
Multiply the desired *ghatis* <term>Ghati: a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term> from the start of the day by two. Divide this by five, discard the quotient, and subtract the remainder from the original doubled value. Add one to the result. If it exceeds seven, divide by seven. The remainder indicates the Lord of the Hour <note>original: "Kala Hora"</note>, counted sequentially from the Lord of the Day. || 55 ||

To know the *Hora* <note>planetary hour</note> of all planets in a day: one *Hora* follows another in the order of the sixth planet from the previous one. **Example:** If the entry of Sunday occurred at 6 *ghatis*, multiply by two (12). Place this in two spots. In one spot, divide by 5...

--- Page 40 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of planetary hours (Horas) and introduces the specific dates for Manvadi and Yugadi festivals, concluding the first chapter of the Muhurta Chintamani.</meta>

<page-num>23</page-num>
<header>With Vernacular Commentary—Chapter 1.</header>

2 was subtracted from 12 (the second position), leaving 10. Dividing this by 7, the remainder is 3. Adding one gives 4. We must look for the Hora of the day for Sunday; therefore, the fourth from the Sun is Mercury's Hora. <note>The author is demonstrating a mathematical formula to determine which planet rules a specific hour of the day.</note> Here, the change of the day is considered only for the purpose of the Time-Hora (<term>Kala-hora</term>; the planetary hour); for all other ritual acts, the day is considered to begin only from sunrise. This is written in the *Vasistha Siddhanta* <note>An ancient and authoritative treatise on Indian astronomy</note>. || 55 ||

->(Shalini Meter)<-

> The effects prescribed for a specific weekday may be performed during the Time-Hora of that day's lord. Similarly, an act prescribed for a specific lunar mansion (<term>Nakshatra</term>) may be done during the Hora of that mansion's lord. One must still consider the "Directional Spear" (<term>Dikshula</term>) and the "Iron Bar" (<term>Parigha-danda</term>) periods, which should never be ignored. || 56 ||

The purpose of the Time-Hora is this: an action prescribed for a specific day can be performed on *any* day during the corresponding Time-Hora. For example, while entering a new house is generally forbidden on a Sunday, if it is necessary, one may do so on a Sunday during the Horas of the Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus. Similarly, if a task is forbidden under a certain star, but is urgent, one may perform it during the Time-Hora of that star's presiding deity. The lords of the auspicious moments (<term>Muhurtas</term>) are described in the section on Marriage. In these matters, one must remember to check the Directional Spear (<term>Dikshula</term>) and the Iron Bar (<term>Parigha-danda</term>) <note>Astrological prohibitions regarding travel in certain directions at certain times</note>; these are discussed in the section on Travel. || 56 ||

->(Shardulavikridita Meter)<-

> The Manvadi dates occur on: Chaitra Bright 3 and 15; Kartika Bright 15 and 12; Ashadha Bright 10 and 15; Jyeshtha and Phalguna Bright 15; Ashvina Bright 9; Magha Bright 7; Pausha Bright 11; Bhadrapada Bright 3; Shravana Dark 30 and 8. The Yugadi dates are: Kartika Bright 9; Vaishakha Bright 3; Bhadrapada Dark 13; and Magha Dark 30. || 57 ||

->Thus ends the first chapter on Auspicious and Inauspicious Times in the *Muhurta Chintamani*. || 1 ||<-

The <term>Manvadi</term> <note>Dates marking the beginning of the eras of the various Manus, the progenitors of humanity</note> dates are: the 3rd and 15th of the bright fortnight of Chaitra; the 15th and 12th of Kartika; the 10th and 15th of Ashadha; the 15th of Jyeshtha and Phalguna; the 9th of Ashvina; the 7th of Magha; the 11th of Pausha; the 3rd of Bhadrapada; and the 30th (New Moon) and 8th of the dark fortnight of Shravana. The <term>Yugadi</term> <note>Dates marking the start of the four cosmic ages or Yugas</note> dates are: the 9th of the bright fortnight of Kartika; the 3rd of Vaishakha; the 13th of the dark fortnight of Bhadrapada; and the 30th (New Moon) of Magha. These dates are holy festivals. On these days, one should observe a break from studies (<term>Anadhyaya</term>) for the initiation of students, the beginning of education, and the conclusion of vows. They are also breaks for daily recitation. Other types of temporary breaks in study occur during twilight thunder, lightning strikes, earthquakes, or the falling of meteors; in such cases, study is paused immediately. After finishing the study of the *Aranyaka* <note>Forest texts of the Vedas</note>, one should pause for a day and night. Breaks also occur on the Full Moon and the fourteenth day...

<summary>This page concludes the first chapter of the Muhurta Chintamani, explaining how planetary hours (Horas) can mitigate unfavorable days and listing the sacred Manvadi and Yugadi dates when Vedic study is prohibited.</summary>
<keywords>Kala-hora, planetary hours, Vasistha Siddhanta, Dikshula, Manvadi, Yugadi, Anadhyaya, Muhurta Chintamani, Hindu calendar, astrological timing</keywords>

--- Page 41 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological timing and prohibitions from the previous section, transitioning from general forbidden times for study to the specific deities governing the lunar mansions.</meta>

<page-num>24</page-num>
<header>The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments</header> <note>original: "Muhurta Chintamani"</note>

On the eighth day of the lunar fortnight, during an eclipse period <note>original: "Rahu-sutaka"</note>, or at the transition of the seasons; after eating at a funeral feast or accepting gifts at a shraddha ceremony; if an animal—such as a frog, mongoose, dog, snake, cat, or rat—passes between a teacher and disciple, study is forbidden for one day and night. Immediate cessation of study <term>Anadhyaya: specific times when the Vedas should not be recited</term> also occurs during a lightning strike, the appearance of a rainbow, or when donkeys, camels, vultures, owls, or crows make distressed, loud noises. Furthermore, being near a corpse, a person of low caste, a cemetery, or an outcast; until the hands are dry after a meal; during a violent wind at midnight; during a rain of dust or a "burning of the quarters" <note>an atmospheric phenomenon where the horizon appears red</note>; at twilight, in heavy frost, in a place of fear, while running, or in the presence of a foul smell; when a distinguished guest arrives at the house; or while riding a donkey, camel, elephant, or horse, or climbing a tree—all these are times when study must stop. There are other forbidden times mentioned in the Law Books <note>Dharmashastras</note>, such as periods of birth or death impurity.

Thus ends the first chapter on Auspicious and Inauspicious Matters in Mahidhara's commentary on the *Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments*.

## Now, the Chapter on Lunar Mansions <note>Nakshatras</note>.

-> The Twin Physicians, Death, Fire, the Creator, the Moon, the Howler, the Mother of Gods, the Preceptor, and the Serpents; <-
-> The Ancestors, the Apportioner, the Sun, the Celestial Architect, and the Wind, in order; <-
-> Indra-Agni, the Friend, the King of Gods, the Goddess of Destruction, the Waters, the Universal Gods, the Creator; <-
-> The Pervader, the Elemental Gods, the Lord of Waters, the One-Footed Goat, the Serpent of the Deep, and the Nourisher are the names of the lords. <-

The lords of the lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras: the 27 (or 28) sectors of the sky along the moon's path</term> are as follows: The Ashvini Kumaras for Ashvini. Yama for Bharani. Similarly, Agni for Krittika. Brahma for Rohini. The Moon for Mrigashira. Shiva for Ardra. Aditi for Punarvasu. Brihaspati for Pushya. The Serpents for Ashlesha. The Ancestors for Magha. Bhaga for Purva Phalguni. Aryaman for Uttara Phalguni. The Sun for Hasta. Vishvakarma for Chitra. Vayu for Swati. Indra and Agni for Vishakha. Mitra (the Sun) for Anuradha. Indra for Jyeshtha. Nirriti for Mula. The Waters for Purvashadha. The Vishvedevas for Uttarashadha. Vidhi <note>Brahma</note> for Abhijit. Vishnu for Shravana. The Vasus for Dhanishtha. Varuna for Shatabhisha. Ajacharana for Purva Bhadrapada. Ahirbudhnya for Uttara Bhadrapada. Pushan for Revati. These are the lords of the mansions. In various texts, the mansions are often referred to by the names of these lords; for example, where the word "Kara" <note>Hand</note> appears in relation to a mansion, it should be understood as Hasta. The specific activities suitable for each mansion are described in other texts: In Ashvini, one should undertake clothing, initiation ceremonies, shaving, the parting of a pregnant woman's hair, wearing ornaments, installations, elephant-related tasks, matters concerning women, agriculture, and learning. In Bharani, activities include building step-wells, wells, and ponds, as well as fierce or dreadful acts involving poison or weapons, entering cavities, mathematics, and depositing wealth...

->**The Qualities of the Stars**<-

...storing items. In **Krittika** <note>The Pleiades; associated with fire</note>: establishing the sacred fire, handling missiles and weapons, fierce deeds, meetings, creating divisions, harsh actions, battle, medicine, and musical performances. In **Rohini**: the hair-parting ceremony <note>Simanta; a traditional pregnancy rite</note>, marriage, garments, jewelry, permanent works, activities involving elephants and horses, consecration, and installation of idols. In **Ardra**: flags, gateways, battle, walls, missiles, weaponry, treaties, creating divisions, enmity, and chemical or liquid-based works. In **Punarvasu**: installation, riding vehicles, hair-parting, garments, architecture, the sacred thread ceremony <note>Upanayana; initiation into Vedic study</note>, grain, eating, and tonsure. In **Pushya**: all auspicious deeds except for marriage. In **Ashlesha**: falsehoods, vices, gambling, alchemy <note>Dhatuvada; the science of minerals and metals</note>, medicine, battle, disputes, liquid-based works, and trade. In **Magha**: agriculture, trade, cattle, grain, deeds useful for war, marriage, dance, and song. In the three **Purvas** <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>: quarrels, poison, weapons, fire, harshness, ferocity, battle, and the sale of meat. In the three **Uttaras** <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>: installation, marriage, hair-parting, consecration, vows, entering a new home, establishing idols, and architectural work. In **Hasta**: installation, marriage, hair-parting, riding vehicles, the sacred thread ceremony, garments, tonsure, architecture, consecration, and jewelry. In **Chitra**: tonsure, entering a home, garments, hair-parting, installation, vows, architectural science, and jewelry. In **Swati**: installation, the sacred thread ceremony, marriage, garments, hair-parting, jewelry, disputes, activities involving elephants, agriculture, and tonsure. In **Vishakha**: garments and jewelry, trade, collecting liquids and grains, dance, song, crafts, and writing. In **Anuradha**: entering a home, establishing idols, marriage, vows, the eight types of auspicious things, garments, jewelry, architecture, treaties, and creating divisions. In **Jyeshtha**: cruel deeds, fierce deeds, weapons, trade, activities involving cows and buffaloes, water-based works, dance, musical instruments, crafts, ironwork, stonework, and writing. In **Mula**: marriage, agriculture, commerce, fierce and harsh deeds, battle, medicine, dance, crafts, treaties, creating divisions, and writing. In **Shravana**: installation, tonsure, hair-parting, travel, the sacred thread ceremony, medicine, beginning the construction of towns, villages, or houses, and coronations. In **Dhanishtha**: weapons, the sacred thread ceremony, tonsure, installation, riding vehicles, jewelry, architecture, hair-parting, entering a home, and weaponry. In **Shatabhisha**: entering a home, establishing idols, tonsure, the sacred thread ceremony, medicine, activities involving horses, hair-parting, and architectural work. In **Revati**: marriage, vows, activities involving horses, installation, riding vehicles, jewelry, entering a home, garments, hair-parting, tonsure, and medicinal works. || 1 ||

The three **Purvas**, **Bharani**, and **Magha**, along with **Tuesday**, are **Fierce** and **Cruel**. In these, acts of striking, fire, deceit, poison, and weapons are successful. || 4 ||

# Muhurtachintamani

**...Visakha and Krittika are known as Mixed and Ordinary. In these, fire rituals, mixed activities, and the release of a bull <note>Vrishotsarga: a ritual involving the release of a bull for religious merit</note> are accomplished. || 5 || Hasta, Ashwini, Pushya, and Abhijit are Swift and Light. In these, incantations, killing, creating divisions, and the taming of animals are performed. || 8 ||**

The classification of the lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras</term> and their associated actions are described: The three Uttaras <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note> and Rohini, associated with Sunday, are called **Fixed and Constant**; in these, stable works such as sowing seeds, starting a house, peace rituals <term>Shanti-karma</term>, gardening, and activities prescribed for "Soft" mansions are accomplished. || 2 || Swati, Punarvasu, Shravana, Dhanistha, Shatabhisha, and Monday are called **Movable and Changeable**; in these, riding elephants or horses, building step-wells, travel, and activities prescribed for "Light" mansions are accomplished. || 3 || The three Purvas <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, Bharani, Magha, and Tuesday are called **Fierce and Cruel**; in these, acts of killing, fire rituals, sensual matters, weapon-work, other harmful acts, and activities prescribed for "Dreadful" mansions are accomplished. || 4 || Vishakha, Krittika, and Wednesday are called **Mixed and Ordinary**; in these, fire sacrifices <term>Agnihotra</term>, the ritual release of a bull, and activities prescribed for "Fierce" mansions are accomplished. || 5 || Hasta, Ashwini, Pushya, Abhijit, and Thursday are called **Swift and Light**; in these, shopkeeping, sexual intercourse, beginning the study of scriptures, jewelry, craftsmanship, the 64 arts including dance, and activities prescribed for "Movable" mansions are accomplished. || 6 || Mrigashira, Revati, Chitra, Anuradha, and Friday are called **Soft and Friendly**; in these, musical performances, clothing, sporting with women, acts related to friends, jewelry, and activities prescribed for "Fixed" mansions are accomplished. || 7 || Mula, Jyeshtha, Ardra, Ashlesha, and Saturday are called **Sharp and Dreadful**; in these, incantations <note>Abhichara: magic used for malevolent purposes</note>, killing (terrible deeds), creating hatred, the taming or training of animals like elephants and horses, or binding them, or castrating them, and activities prescribed for "Fierce" mansions are accomplished. || 8 ||

# Chapter 2: Including the Commentary in Common Language

The constellations that are "upward-facing" are auspicious for activities such as <term>Rajyabhisheka</term> <note>royal consecration or coronation</note>, <term>Pattabandhana</term> <note>the binding of a royal turban or crowning</note>, and construction of buildings. The "soft" constellations—namely Hasta, Swati, Punarvasu, Jyeshtha, and Ashwini (which is <term>Tiryangmukha</term> <note>sideways-facing</note>)—are known as having "even sight." In these, tasks involving wheels, chariots, plows, seeds, and animal husbandry are successfully accomplished. || 9 ||

> <note>original Sanskrit: Vasantatilaka meter</note>
> *Coral, ivory, conch-shells, gold, and new garments should be worn during Revati, the fixed constellations, Ashwini, and the five stars from Hasta to Anuradha, as well as Pushya and Punarvasu. One should avoid doing so on "empty" lunar days, or on Saturdays, Mondays, and Tuesdays. However, a fortunate married woman should not wear these items on a Tuesday even if it coincides with a fixed constellation or Punarvasu.* || 10 ||

Revati, the <term>Dhruva Nakshatras</term> <note>the fixed constellations: Rohini, Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Ashwini, and the sequence from Hasta to Anuradha, as well as Pushya and Punarvasu, are the times to wear ornaments made of coral, pearls, ivory, conch-shells (such as bangles), gold, and new clothes. However, this should not be done on a <term>Rikta Tithi</term> <note>the "empty" 4th, 9th, or 14th lunar days</note>, or on Saturday, Monday, or Tuesday. Note that on Tuesdays, there is no fault in wearing red-colored clothes or gold; however, a <term>Saubhagyavati</term> <note>a fortunate woman whose husband is alive</note> must not wear the aforementioned items on a Tuesday even if it falls under a fixed constellation, Punarvasu, or Pushya. || 10 ||

> <note>original Sanskrit: Shardulavikridita meter</note>
> *If a new garment—or by extension, a bed, sandals, umbrella, or flag—is burned by fire, torn, or stained with soot or mud, one should divide it into nine equal parts. The four corners belong to the gods, the three middle vertical parts to humans, and the two side parts to demons. If the damage is in the demon section, it is inauspicious; if in the human or divine sections, it is auspicious.* || 11 ||

If a new garment—and by extension, items like bedding, sandals, umbrellas, or flags—is burned by fire at any spot, or is torn, or becomes smeared with soot or mud, one should divide that item into nine equal parts. The four corners are the places of the Deities; the three middle parts (from top to bottom) are the places of Humans; and the two side parts are the places of the <term>Rakshasas</term> <note>demons or malevolent spirits</note>. If the burned or damaged portion falls in the Rakshasa section, it yields evil results; one should discard that garment and donate gold or similar items in charity. If the damage is in the Human or Divine sections, it is considered auspicious. There is an alternative opinion: if the damaged area contains auspicious signs like the <term>Shrivatsa</term> <note>an ancient mystical mark meaning "beloved of Sri"</note> or <term>Sarvatobhadra</term> <note>a sacred geometric grid</note>, it is auspicious even in the Rakshasa section. Conversely, if evil signs like a serpent appear in the auspicious sections, it remains inauspicious. || 11 ||

> <note>original Sanskrit: Anushtubh meter</note>
> *By the command of a Brahmin, at a wedding, by the order of a King, or when something is gifted out of affection, the wise say a garment may be worn even during an inauspicious constellation or day.* || 12 ||

By the instruction of a Brahmin, during a wedding, or when a King gives a garment as a mark of pleasure, one may wear it even without waiting for the aforementioned <term>Muhurta</term> <note>astrologically calculated auspicious moment</note> or even during a censured constellation or day. || 12 ||

# The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments
## (Muhurtachintamani)

-> (Written in the **Shardulavikridita** meter) <-

For planting vines and trees, the constellations of Anuradha, Mula, the "Fixed" stars, the "Soft" stars, Shatabhisha, and the "Swift" stars are best. For a first meeting with a king <note>In a modern context, this refers to high-ranking officials or employers</note>, use the "Fixed," "Soft," and "Swift" stars, as well as Shravana and Dhanishtha. For the preparation of intoxicants or medicines involving fermentation, the "Sharp," "Fierce," and Shatabhisha stars are considered moderate. For the buying and selling of cattle and other animals, the "Swift" stars, Revati, Krittika, Jyeshtha, Punarvasu, Shatabhisha, and Dhanishtha are auspicious. || 13 ||

<note>The "Fixed" (Dhruva) stars are Rohini, Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada. The "Soft" (Mridu) stars are Mrigashira, Chitra, Anuradha, and Revati. The "Swift" (Kshipra) stars are Ashwini, Pushya, and Hasta.</note>

---

-> (Written in the **Indravajra** meter) <-

When the rising sign (Ascendant) is auspicious and the eighth house is "pure" <note>meaning no planets are occupying the eighth house from the rising sign</note>, one should perform protective rituals for animals during their specific "Yoni" constellations or "Movable" stars. However, one should not stable or bring animals into a new enclosure on "Empty" lunar days (the 4th, 9th, and 14th), the 8th day, the New Moon, Tuesdays, or during the Shravana, Chitra, or "Fixed" constellations. || 14 ||

---

-> (Written in the **Mandakranta** meter) <-

For taking medicine, the "Swift," "Soft," and "Movable" stars, as well as Mula, are best, provided the rising sign is a "Dual-natured" sign (Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, or Pisces) and the 12th, 7th, and 8th houses are empty. The best days are Friday, Monday, Thursday, Wednesday, and Sunday, occurring on auspicious lunar dates (excluding the "Empty" days and the New Moon). However, one should not begin treatment on the day of their own birth-star. For needlework <note>original: "Suchikarma" - includes sewing and embroidery</note>, use Punarvasu, Dhanishtha, Chitra, Anuradha, Ashwini, or Pushya. || 15 ||

---

-> (Written in the **Anustubh** meter) <-

Constellations recommended for buying should not be used for starting a sale; likewise, those recommended for selling should not be used for buying. This rule applies specifically to the *commencement* of a new business venture, not to every daily transaction. If this rule were applied to every single moment, trade would become impossible. For buying, the following are auspicious: Revati, Shatabhisha, Ashwini, Swati, Shravana, and Chitra. || 16 ||

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological timing (Muhurta) for commercial activities, specifically addressing the practicalities of buying and selling, the inauguration of shops, and the management of livestock like horses and elephants.</meta>

<page-num>29</page-num>
<header>Chapter 2: Including the Vernacular Commentary</header>

...should not be [a cause for concern]. For example, if someone comes to buy on a day with a favorable lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra; one of the 27 sectors of the sky the moon passes through</term> for purchasing, but the seller refuses because it is not a favorable mansion for selling, how can the buyer complete the purchase at that specific moment? Similarly, if someone wishes to sell at a favorable moment but no buyer is available, to whom shall they sell? In response to such doubts, this rule applies only to the *initial commencement* of a business venture. For instance, when large-scale merchants <term>Mahajans; influential bankers or wholesale merchants</term> buy vast quantities of goods at a specific time to stock their warehouses and later sell them at the appropriate time—this is when the specific timing <note>Muhurta</note> matters. It is not intended for every single daily transaction. The lunar mansions Revati, Shatabhisha, Ashwini, Swati, and Shravan are auspicious for purchasing. || 16 ||

->(Verse in Shardulavikridita meter)<-
> The three Purvas, Vishakha, Krittika, Ashlesha, and Bharani; with auspicious planets in the centers (1, 4, 7, 10) or trines (9, 5); and inauspicious planets in the 3rd, 6th, and 11th houses; excluding the sign of Aquarius—selling is auspicious on favorable lunar days. || Excluding the "Empty" days <note>Rikta Tithis: the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight, considered unlucky for new starts</note>, Tuesdays, and the sign of Aquarius; for opening a shop, use the Friendly, Constant, or Swift lunar mansions; with the Moon and Venus in the ascendant, malefic planets absent from the 8th and 12th houses, and benefic planets in the 2nd, 11th, and 10th houses. || 17 ||

The three Purvas <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, Vishakha, Krittika, Ashlesha, and Bharani are the mansions for selling. If auspicious planets are in the 1st, 4, 7, or 10th houses or the 9th and 5th houses from the ascendant <note>Lagna</note>, and inauspicious planets are in the 3rd, 6th, and 11th houses—provided the ascendant is not Aquarius—one should begin selling on auspicious lunar days. For the opening of a shop <term>Panyarambha; the ceremonial start of trade</term>, excluding the "Empty" lunar days, Tuesdays, and the sign of Aquarius, one should choose the Anuradha, Constant, or Swift mansions. The Moon and Venus should be in the ascendant, malefic planets should not be in the 8th or 12th houses, and auspicious planets should be in the 2nd, 11th, or 10th houses. In such a moment, one should begin trade. Usually, the Moon in the ascendant is avoided for all tasks, but because the Moon is the lord of the merchant class <insert>Vaishyas</insert> and when placed with Venus, a Moon in the ascendant is considered virtuous. || 17 ||

->(Verse in Indravajra meter)<-
> In the Swift, Final, Vasu, Moon, Wind, Water, and Sun mansions; on days other than the "Empty" ones; it is praised. The work of horses and elephants should be done by the wise in the Soft, Swift, and Mobile mansions. || 18 ||

In the Swift mansions, Revati, Mrigashira, Swati, Shatabhisha, and Punarvasu—excluding the "Empty" lunar days and Tuesdays—one should perform deeds such as the buying and selling of horses. For riding a horse, other texts describe a "Horse Chart": draw the shape of a horse and count from the Sun's mansion to the day's mansion. 5 mansions in the shoulder signify Fortune; 10 in the back signify Success; 2 in the tail signify Loss of Wife; 4 in the legs signify Defeat in Battle; 5 in the belly signify Loss of the Horse; 2 in the mouth signify Financial Gain. Similarly, the wise should handle elephants in the Soft, Constant, Swift, and Mobile mansions. The first use of the goad <term>Ankusharambha; the ceremonial first training or piercing of an elephant</term> should be done under a favorable ascendant, division, and star, or on a Saturday with Saturn in the ascendant. || 18 ||

# The Jewel of Muhurta (Muhurta-Chintamani)

<page-num>30</page-num>

->(Meter: Shardulavikridita) The crafting of ornaments should occur under the <term>Tripushkara</term> <note>A specific astrological combination where a tithi, day, and nakshatra coincide to triple the effect of an action</note>, Moveable, Swift, and Fixed stars. Avoid Sharp and Fierce stars. It is best on Sundays and Tuesdays, with Aries, Scorpio, or Leo in the ascendant. If the jewelry is set with pearls, use Moveable, Fixed, Soft, or Swift stars on Mondays or Fridays, with the 4th, 2nd, or 7th sign in the ascendant. Forged weapons are auspicious under Sharp, Fierce, Ashvini, Mrigashira, Vishakha, and Krittika stars. <-

The crafting of ornaments—specifically those set with gemstones—should be done under <term>Tripushkara</term> (stars whose three quarters fall across different signs), Moveable, Swift, and Fixed <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>Lunar mansions or star clusters used in Vedic astrology</note>. One must avoid Sharp and Fierce stars. The preferred days are Sunday and Tuesday, with the ascendant (Lagna) being Aries, Scorpio, or Leo. If the ornament includes pearls, use Moveable, Fixed, Soft, or Swift stars on Mondays or Fridays with the 4th, 2nd, or 7th sign as the ascendant; the same applies to silver jewelry. Forging weapons is auspicious under Sharp or Fierce stars, as well as Ashvini, Mrigashira, Vishakha, and Krittika. || 19 ||

->(Meter: Sragdhara) The minting of coins is auspicious under Fixed, Soft, Moveable, and Swift stars, excluding Mondays and Saturdays. It should be done on Purna and Jaya dates, provided Jupiter and Venus are not combust, and with auspicious planets in the ascendant. The washing of clothes is good under Dhanishtha, Ashvini, and the five stars starting from Hasta, as well as Punarvasu and Pushya. Never wash on Empty dates, the 6th, festivals, or Saturdays and Wednesdays. <-

Under Fixed, Soft, Moveable, and Swift stars—excluding Mondays and Saturdays—on the <term>Purna</term> and <term>Jaya</term> lunar days (5th, 10th, 15th, 3rd, 8th, 13th), and during a time free from the "combust" defects of Jupiter and Venus, one should perform <term>Mudrapatana</term> <note>The minting or striking of coins/seals</note>. Regarding <term>Vastrakshalana</term> (washing clothes or giving them to a washerman), it should be done under Dhanishtha, Ashvini, the five stars from Hasta, Punarvasu, or Pushya. However, one must never wash clothes on <term>Rikta</term> (Empty) dates, the 6th lunar day, festival days, the New Moon, or on Saturdays and Wednesdays. || 20 ||

->(Meter: Sragdhara) Spears, armor, bows, arrows, swords, and small daggers should be donned on days other than Empty dates, specifically on Fridays, Thursdays, and Sundays. Use Friendly, Fixed, and Light stars, along with Punarvasu, Jyeshtha, and Vishakha. This should occur in a Fixed ascendant with the Moon aspected by benefits. The enjoyment of beds and seats is desired under Fixed, Soft, and Light stars, as well as Shravana, Bharani, and Punarvasu. <-

Excluding the Empty lunar dates, on Friday, Thursday, or Sunday, under Friendly, Fixed, or Light stars—as well as Punarvasu, Jyeshtha, and Vishakha—one should equip themselves with spears, armor, bows, arrows, swords, or daggers. This should be done when a Fixed sign is in the ascendant and the Moon is influenced by auspicious planets.

# Chapter 2: Including the Vernacular Commentary

The aspect of <term>benefic planets</term><note>Planets considered naturally auspicious, such as Jupiter, Venus, Mercury, and a waxing Moon</note> and their presence in the <term>Kendras</term><note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a horoscope, considered the pillars of a chart</note> are essential. In the Fixed, Soft, and Light <term>Nakshatras</term><note>Lunar mansions or star clusters used in Vedic astrology</note>—specifically Shravana, Bharani, and Punarvasu—one should begin using beds, couches, deerskin mats, sandals, and other items intended for sitting and sleeping. || 21 ||

->(Shardulavikridita Meter)<-
> The Blind-eye mansions are Rohini, Purvashadha, Dhanishta, Pushya, Vishakha, Uttaraphalguni, and Revati.
> The Dull-eye mansions are Hasta, Uttarashadha, Anuradha, Shatabhisha, Ashlesha, Ashwini, and Mrigashira.
> The Medium-eye mansions are Ardra, Magha, Purvabhadrapada, Chitra, Jyeshtha, Abhijit, and Bharani.
> The Good-eye mansions are Swati, Punarvasu, Shravana, Krittika, Uttarabhadrapada, Mula, and Purvaphalguni. || 22 ||

<note>The Sanskrit verse uses deity names to represent the stars: e.g., 'Dhatri' for Rohini, 'Jala' for Purvashadha.</note>

Rohini, Purvashadha, Dhanishta, Pushya, Vishakha, Uttaraphalguni, and Revati are designated as **Blind-eye** (<term>Andhaksha</term>). Hasta, Uttarashadha, Anuradha, Shatabhisha, Ashlesha, Ashwini, and Mrigashira are designated as **Dull-eye** (<term>Mandaksha</term>). Ardra, Magha, Purvabhadrapada, Chitra, Jyeshtha, Abhijit, and Bharani are designated as **Medium-eye** (<term>Madhyaksha</term>). Uttarabhadrapada, Mula, Purvaphalguni, Swati, Punarvasu, Shravana, and Krittika are designated as **Good-eye** (<term>Sulochan</term>). An easy way to calculate these is to count in groups of four starting from Rohini in the order of Blind, Dull, Medium, and Good. For example: Rohini (Blind), Mrigashira (Dull), Ardra (Medium), Punarvasu (Good); then Pushya (Blind), Ashlesha (Dull), and so on. || 22 ||

->(Anushtubh Meter)<-
> Wealth lost during a Blind-eye mansion is recovered quickly; in a Dull-eye, with great effort.
> In a Medium-eye, one only hears news of it from afar; in a Good-eye, it is never found. || 23 ||

The purpose of these classifications is as follows: if an object is lost when the Moon is in a **Blind-eye** mansion, it will be found quickly. If lost in a **Dull-eye** mansion, it may be recovered with significant effort. In a **Medium-eye** mansion, one might only hear rumors of its location from a distance, but the object will not be physically recovered. In a **Good-eye** mansion, forget about finding it—one will not even hear news of where it might be. If the day or mansion of the loss is unknown, the prediction should be made based on the mansion active at the time the question is asked. || 23 ||

->(Anushtubh Meter)<-
> Wealth given, invested, or used during Sharp, Mixed, Fixed, or Fierce mansions, 
> or during Vishti or Vyatipata, is lost and never regained. || 24 ||

Money or goods given to someone with the intent of taking them back, or stolen, or lost, or given as a loan during the **Sharp, Mixed, Fixed, or Fierce** mansions, or during the inauspicious times of <term>Bhadra</term><note>A specific inauspicious period in the lunar day</note> or <term>Vyatipata</term><note>A specific astronomical yoga considered very unfavorable</note>, will not be recovered. || 24 ||

->(Shardulavikridita Meter)<-
> In the mansions of Anuradha, Hasta, Rohini, Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, Magha, Purvashadha, Revati, Pushya, and Mrigashira...
> When the malefic planets are weak, and Jupiter, Mercury, or Venus are in the ascendant or the tenth house...

<summary>This page details the classification of Nakshatras into four "eye" categories to predict the recovery of lost or stolen property and lists inauspicious times for financial transactions.</summary>

# ( 32 )
-> **The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments** <-
<note>original: "Muhurtachintamani," a classic 16th-century Sanskrit text on electional astrology used to determine the best time for various activities.</note>

> In the constellation of Purvashadha, the digging of all types of water reservoirs should be done; with the same stars (excluding Purvashadha and Magha, but including Jyeshtha), dancing is auspicious when the fourth house is occupied by benefics, and Mercury or the Moon are in the ascendant or in a sign ruled by Mercury. || 25 ||

In the constellations of Anuradha, Hasta, Shravana, Dhanishtha, Shatabhisha, Magha, Purvashadha, Revati, Pushya, and Mrigashira, provided the malefic planets are weak and the ascendant <term>Lagna; the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> is auspicious—containing either Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus—and the Moon is in the tenth house in a water-sign: at such a time, one should dig or construct reservoirs such as step-wells, wells, or ponds. 

Furthermore, for the **"Commencement of Dance"** (the beginning of dancing, acting, or theatrical performances), one should use the aforementioned constellations (excluding Purvashadha and Magha, but including Jyeshtha), provided there is a benefic planet in the fourth house from the ascendant, and Mercury is in the ascendant, or the Moon is in a sign of Mercury (Gemini or Virgo). || 25 ||

**(Shalini Meter)**
> In the swift and friendly constellations, on the days of Mercury, Venus, the Sun, or Jupiter, with a benefic in the ascendant and the Sun or Mars in the tenth or eleventh house; service to a master should be undertaken by a servant, provided there is compatibility between their animal types and zodiac signs. || 26 ||

In the "Swift" <note>Kshipra: Ashwini, Pushya, Hasta</note> and "Friendly" <note>Maitra: Anuradha, Mrigashira, Revati, Chitra</note> constellations, on Wednesday, Friday, Sunday, or Thursday, and in an ascendant joined by benefic planets where the Sun or Mars is in the tenth or eleventh house: in such a favorable moment, a servant (employee) should begin their service to a master. However, the compatibility of the "Yonis" <term>animal types assigned to birth stars</term> and the compatibility of the zodiac signs between the master and servant are the primary considerations. If the Yonis and signs are mutually friendly, the service will be auspicious. || 26 ||

**(Shardulavikridita Meter)**
> In Swati, Punarvasu, the soft stars, Vishakha, Pushya, the three ears, Ashwini, and in movable ascendants, with the ninth, fifth, and eighth houses purified, the lending of money is auspicious. Not on Tuesdays, nor on the day of a solar transit, nor on a Sunday with Hasta; debt taken then may not be cleared even by one's descendants. And on Wednesday, wealth should never be given as a loan. || 27 ||

In Swati, Punarvasu, the "Soft" constellations <note>Mridu: Mrigashira, Chitra, Anuradha, Revati</note>, Vishakha, Pushya, Shravana, Dhanishtha, Shatabhisha, and Ashwini, and in "Movable" <term>Chara: Aries, Cancer, Libra, Capricorn</term> ascendants, with benefic planets in the 9th and 5th houses, no malefic planets present, and the 8th house empty: in such a moment, **(Lending Money)** for the sake of increasing wealth is auspicious. 

However, one should not take a loan on a Tuesday, on the day of a Solar Transit <term>Sankranti; when the sun moves into a new sign</term>, or on a Sunday falling in the Hasta constellation. If one takes a loan then, the debt may not be cleared even by one's descendants. Furthermore, one should never give money as a loan on a Wednesday. || 27 ||

**(Shardulavikridita Meter)**
> Mula, the two-lorded star, Magha, the movable, fixed, soft, and swift stars—excepting Sundays and Saturdays—with malefics weak, the Moon in a water sign, and Venus and the Moon being strong; in an ascendant with Jupiter, the "Driving of the Plough" is praised. It is not auspicious in Leo or Aquarius, and in Cancer, Aries, Capricorn, or Libra, it causes loss; likewise on the "Empty" dates and the sixth lunar day. || 28 ||

<meta>This page continues the discussion of agricultural astrology, specifically focusing on the auspicious timing for plowing and sowing seeds based on planetary positions and lunar mansions.</meta>

<page-num>33</page-num>
<header>With Commentary - Chapter 2</header>
<sig>3</sig>

In the lunar mansions of <term>Mula</term> <note>the root</note>, <term>Vishakha</term>, <term>Magha</term>, and the groups known as <term>Chara</term> <note>Movable: Shravana, Dhanishtha, Shatabhisha, Punarvasu, Swati</note>, <term>Dhruva</term> <note>Fixed: Rohini, Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, <term>Mridu</term> <note>Soft: Chitra, Anuradha, Mrigashira, Revati</note>, and <term>Kshipra</term> <note>Swift: Ashwini, Pushya, Hasta</note>, on days other than Sunday and Saturday, when the Moon is weak and devoid of malefic influence and is positioned in the sign or division of a water-bearing zodiac sign <note>Cancer, Scorpio, or Pisces</note>, and when Venus and the Moon are risen (strong), and Jupiter is in the Ascendant—specifically in the Ascendants of Leo, Aquarius, Cancer, Aries, Capricorn, or Sagittarius—and not on the <term>Rikta</term> <note>Empty/Inauspicious lunar days: 4th, 9th, 14th</note> or the sixth lunar day; in such a <term>Muhurta</term> <note>auspicious moment</note>, one should begin agricultural work such as plowing. If done during the forbidden <term>Rikta</term> or sixth lunar days, the harvest will perish. || 28 ||

-> (Verse in Shardulavikridita meter) <-
Except for Shravana, Shatabhisha, Punarvasu, and Vishakha, and excluding Tuesdays, the sowing of seeds is declared auspicious in the lunar mansions previously mentioned for plowing. From the day the Sun enters the first quarter of <term>Ardra</term>, the Earth is said to be in her menses for three days. During these days, one must not sow seeds in the earth. In the matter of sowing, special consideration is given to the <term>Phani Chakra</term> <note>The Serpent Diagram</note>: counting from the lunar mansion occupied by Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note>, 8 mansions are inauspicious, 3 are auspicious, 1 is inauspicious, 3 are auspicious, 1 is inauspicious, 3 are auspicious, 1 is inauspicious, 3 are auspicious, and 4 are inauspicious. One should calculate up to the mansion of the day to know the result. Similarly, for plowing, there is the <term>Hala Chakra</term> <note>The Plow Diagram</note>: from the mansion occupied by the Sun, 3 are inauspicious, 8 are auspicious, 9 are inauspicious, and 8 are auspicious; this includes the 28th mansion, <term>Abhijit</term>. In these diagrams, if the aforementioned mansions fall in auspicious places, they should be chosen; if in inauspicious places, they should be avoided. Even if a mansion not mentioned before falls in an auspicious place in the diagram, it should not be taken. According to other texts, the diagrams are as follows. || 29 ||

### The Seed-Sowing Diagram (Bijopti Chakra)

> From another text: There are three mansions in the head [of the serpent] starting from Rahu's mansion, which lead to the destruction of grain. Three in the throat lead to "black soot" <note>crop disease or blight</note>. Twelve in the belly lead to growth/increase.
> Four in the tail are said to lead to "grainlessness" <note>empty husks</note>.
> Five in the navel lead to various agricultural calamities (pests/drought) in sequence during sowing.

### ->The Diagram of the Plough<-

From another text—In the calculation of the Plough-Diagram, place three units each at the two ends of the pole and the yoke-peg. Place five units at the two yokes, and one in the center of the plough.
If the moon's constellation falls on the pole, there is loss of cattle; if on the yoke-peg, there is fear for the owner. Prosperity resides in the plough and the yokes on the day of commencing field work. <note>This "Hala Chakra" or Plough Diagram is a mnemonic tool where the day's lunar mansion (Nakshatra) is mapped onto parts of a plough to predict the harvest's success.</note>

---

(In the *Shardulavikridita* meter) Bloodletting <note>original: "shira-mokshanam"</note> should be performed when the moon is in Chitra, Swati, Anuradha, Jyeshtha, Shatabhisha, Shravana, or the "light" constellations, on Tuesdays, Sundays, or Thursdays. Purgatives and emetics <note>original: "vireka-vamana"</note> should be taken on any day except Wednesday or Saturday. Religious ceremonies <note>original: "dharma-kriya"</note> such as the Great Fire Sacrifice or Rudra-recitations should be done under "friendly," "swift," "movable," or "fixed" constellations on the days of the Sun, Moon, Mercury, or Jupiter, provided the ascendant is in a division of Mercury or Jupiter and the planet Jupiter is transitionally strong for the performer.

Chitra, Swati, Anuradha, Jyeshtha, Rohini, Mrigashira, Shatabhisha, Shravana, and the light constellations <note>The "light" or "swift" constellations include Ashwini, Pushya, and Hasta.</note> on Tuesday, Thursday, and Sunday are for <term>Bloodletting</term> (extracting blood through the veins). In the same constellations, on days other than Wednesday and Saturday, one should take <term>Emetics and Purgatives</term> (cleansing the body through induced vomiting or bowel movements with medicine). Under the friendly, swift, movable, and fixed constellations on Sunday, Monday, Wednesday, and Thursday, in an auspicious planetary division of Mercury or Jupiter, and when Jupiter is personally auspicious, one should perform <term>Religious Acts</term> such as the Kotihoma or Rudra-Anusthana. ३०

(In the *Vasantatilaka* meter) The harvesting of grain is highly recommended under the "sharp" constellations, Purva Bhadrapada, Hasta, Krittika, Dhanishtha, Shravana, Mrigashira, Swati, Magha, the three "Uttara" constellations, Purva Ashadha, Bharani, Chitra, and Pushya, excluding Saturdays, Tuesdays, and the "Empty" lunar days, provided the ascendant is a "fixed" sign. ३१

Under the sharp constellations, Purva Bhadrapada, Hasta, Krittika, Dhanishtha, Shravana, Mrigashira, Swati, Magha, the three Uttaras (Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada), Purva Ashadha, Bharani, Chitra, and Pushya, and avoiding Saturday, Tuesday, and the <term>Rikta</term> (Empty) dates <note>The 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight, considered unlucky for new ventures.</note>, and in fixed-sign ascendants (Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, Aquarius), one should perform <term>Grain-Cutting</term> (harvesting the ripe crop). ३१

(In the *Vasantatilaka* meter) Threshing is said to be good under Rohini, Uttaraphalguni, Shravana, Magha, Jyeshtha, Rohini <insert>repeated</insert>, Mula, Anuradha, and Revati. The planting of crops is auspicious under the two-footed constellations, Purva Bhadrapada, the three Uttaras, Rohini, Shatabhisha, and Uttaraphalguni, excluding Saturday and <term>Mars</term> (Tuesday). ३२

# Chapter 2

Page 35

Under the constellations of Purva Phalguni, Uttara Phalguni, Shravana, Magha, Jyeshtha, Rohini, Mula, Anuradha, and Revati, on auspicious lunar days and weekdays, one should perform **Threshing** <term>Annamardana: the process of crushing grain like chickpeas or wheat to separate it from the husk</term>. Under the constellations of Vishakha, Purva Bhadrapada, Mula, Rohini, Shatabhisha, and Uttara Phalguni—excluding Saturdays and Tuesdays—one should perform the transplanting of crops from one field to another or into irrigated fields. || 32 ||

-> (*Vasantatilaka meter*) In constellations other than the Mixed, Fierce, Sharp, or Serpent groups, and when the Ascendant is not Cancer, Aries, or Libra, on an auspicious day, the storage of grain is declared beneficial. In the Fixed, Nourishing, Indra, or Movable constellations, the lending of grain leads to its increase. || 33 || <-

In constellations excluding the Mixed <note>Mishra</note>, Fierce <note>Ugra</note>, Ardra, Ashlesha, and Jyeshtha, and when the Ascendant <note>Lagna</note> is not Cancer, Aries, or Libra, on an auspicious day, one should perform **Grain Storage** <term>Annasthiti: the act of placing harvested crops into a granary or storage pit</term>. In the Fixed <note>Dhruva</note>, Pushya, Vishakha, Jyeshtha, and Movable <note>Chara</note> constellations, one should perform **Grain Increase** <term>Dhanyavriddhi: lending grain on interest, such as receiving 1.25 or 1.5 times the amount back after a few months</term>. || 33 ||

---

-> (*Vasantatilaka meter*) In the Swift, Fixed, Final, Movable, and Friendly constellations, as well as Magha, peace-making rituals and nourishing rites are praised. When the Sun is in the 10th house, the Moon in the 4th, and Jupiter in the Ascendant, even during "combust" or corrupted times, these rituals bring good results if prompted by an omen. || 34 || <-

In the Swift <note>Kshipra</note>, Fixed <note>Dhruva</note>, Revati, Movable <note>Chara</note>, and Friendly <note>Maitra</note> constellations, and when the Sun is in the 10th house from the Ascendant, the Moon in the 4th, and Jupiter is in the Ascendant, one should perform **Occasional Peace Rites** <term>Naimittika-shanti: rituals performed to counteract bad omens like comets or eclipses</term>. These rites, along with nourishing ceremonies, are auspicious even during "corrupted" times such as the planetary setting of Jupiter or Venus, or the "childhood/old age" phases of planets. || 34 ||

---

-> (*Anushtubh meter*) Counting in groups of three from the Sun's constellation to the Moon's: Sun, Mercury, Venus, Saturn, Moon, Mars, Jupiter, Rahu, and Ketu. Offerings are not desired for the cruel planets. || 35 || <-

An offering into the fire is called an oblation <note>Ahuti</note>. One should perform the fire sacrifice during the influence of a benefic planet and avoid it during a malefic one. To determine this, count from the Sun's current constellation to the Moon's constellation in groups of 3. The first 3 belong to the Sun, the next 3 to Mercury, followed by Venus, Saturn, Moon, Mars, Jupiter, Rahu, and Ketu in that order. This determines the **Oblation** <term>Ahuti: the planetary energy currently "consuming" the sacrifice</term>. || 35 ||

---

-> (*Indravajra meter*) Add one to the lunar day and the weekday, then divide by four. If the remainder is zero or three, the Fire resides on Earth. For happiness, perform the sacrifice then; if the remainder is one or two, life and wealth are destroyed as Fire is in the heavens or the underworld. || 36 || <-

Add 1 to the current lunar day <note>Tithi</note> and then add the number of the weekday. Divide the total by 4. If the remainder is 0 or 3, it is known as **The Abode of Fire** <term>Agni Vasa: the symbolic location of the fire god</term> being on Earth. Performing a fire sacrifice at this time brings happiness. If the remainder is 1 or 2, Fire is not on Earth; performing a sacrifice then leads to the destruction of life and wealth. || 36 ||

-> (Anuṣṭubh meter) Eating the first fruits of the harvest is auspicious during the Moveable, Swift, and Gentle constellations, in a good Ascendant. <-
-> Excluding the Nanda dates, the Poisonous Hours, the months of Madhu and Pausha, and the days of Saturn and Mars. || 37 || <-

Excluding the months of Pausha <note>approx. December-January</note> and Chaitra <note>approx. March-April</note>, the days of Saturday and Tuesday, the Nanda <term>Tithis</term> <note>the 1st, 6th, and 11th lunar days</note>, and the "Poisonous Hours" (Visha-ghati) mentioned in the chapter on marriage—one should perform the <term>Navatra</term> <note>the ritual of eating the first grains of a new harvest</note> during a favorable <term>Lagna</term> <note>Ascendant/Zodiac sign rising on the horizon</note> and under Moveable, Swift, or Gentle <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>Lunar mansions/star clusters</note>. || 37 ||

-> (Anuṣṭubh meter) Excluding the three beginning with Bharani, Vishakha, Jyeshtha, Ashlesha, Magha, and Ardra; <-
-> On the days of Venus, Jupiter, and the Sun, the construction of a boat is auspicious in a good Ascendant. || 38 || <-

In <term>Nakshatras</term> other than Bharani, Krittika, Rohini, Vishakha, Jyeshtha, Ashlesha, Magha, and Ardra, and on the days of Friday, Thursday, or Sunday, during an auspicious <term>Lagna</term>, one should undertake the construction of a boat, ship, or dugout. || 38 ||

-> (Anuṣṭubh meter) In Mula, Ardra, Bharani, Magha, or Mrigashira; with Mercury in Aquarius, <-
-> Or Venus in the fourth house, and the eighth house being pure; success is achieved in Heroic rites and Incantations. || 39 || <-

Under the <term>Nakshatras</term> of Mula, Ardra, Bharani, Magha, or Mrigashira, and in the Ascendant of Aquarius with Mercury present, or when Venus is in the fourth house and the eighth house is empty/pure—in such a <term>Muhurta</term> <note>calculated auspicious time</note>, one should perform "Hero-attainment" (Vira-sadhana) and <term>Abhichara</term> <note>incantations or rituals intended to harm or kill an enemy</note>. Note: Since it is impossible for Mercury to be in the Ascendant while Venus is in the fourth, the word "or" is used here to denote separate conditions. || 39 ||

-> (Vasantatilaka meter) In Revati, Punarvasu, the Constant stars, Magha, Swati, and Ashlesha; on the "Empty" dates, in a Moveable <-
-> Ascendant, excluding the days of Venus and the Moon; the bath of a person freed from illness is praised, provided the Moon is weak <-
-> and malefic planets are in the 11th, the Centers, or the Trines. || 40 || <-

When a patient is cured of a disease, the <term>Muhurta</term> for their recovery bath is as follows: under Revati, Punarvasu, the Constant (Dhruva) constellations, Magha, Swati, or Ashlesha; on "Empty" (Rikta) <term>Tithis</term> <note>the 4th, 9th, or 14th lunar days</note>; in a Moveable Ascendant; on days other than Friday or Monday. Malefic planets should be in the 11th house or in the Centers (1, 4, 7, 10) or Trines (1, 5, 9) from the Ascendant, and the Moon should be "weak" (situated in the 4th, 8th, or 12th position from the birth sign). || 40 ||

-> (Anuṣṭubh meter) In the Gentle, Constant, Swift, or Moveable stars; with Jupiter or Mercury in the Ascendant or the 10th house; <-
-> While the Moon is in the divisions of Mercury or Jupiter; the commencement of craftsmanship attains success. || 41 || <-

Under Gentle, Constant, Swift, or Moveable <term>Nakshatras</term>, if Jupiter or Mercury is in the 10th house or the Ascendant, and the Moon is in any of the six-fold divisions (Shadvarga) belonging to Mercury or Jupiter, one should begin <term>Shilpa-vidya</term> <note>the study or practice of arts, crafts, or engineering</note>. || 41 ||

->**<term>Anuṣṭubh</term> <note>A poetic meter consisting of four quarters of eight syllables each.</note> When the Moon is in the constellations of Anuradha, Puṣya, or Pūrvāphalguṇī, on the eighth or twelfth lunar day, or during the Taitila Karana <note>A 'Karana' is half of a lunar day; Taitila is one of the seven moving Karanas.</note>, and when Venus is in the ascendant or aspects the ascendant on a favorable weekday, one should initiate a treaty or friendship. || 42 ||**<-

In the constellations of Puṣya, Anuradha, or Pūrvāphalguṇī, on the eighth or twelfth lunar dates, or in the Taitila Karana, if Venus is in the ascendant <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the event.</note> or if the ascendant is aspected by Venus on an auspicious weekday, one should begin (Prīti) alliances, friendships, or diplomatic relations. || 42 ||

---

->**<term>Vasantatilakā</term> <note>A lyrical meter of fourteen syllables, often used for more complex descriptions.</note> Avoiding the eighth and fourteenth lunar days, Saturday, Tuesday, the period of Bhadrā <note>An inauspicious time period in the Hindu calendar.</note>, the birth constellation, the birth month, and the eighth house from the Sun and Moon in transit, as well as the 'Nadi' constellations; in dual or movable signs and Navamsas <note>A one-ninth division of a zodiac sign.</note>, when the Moon, Jupiter, and the birth star are pure, one should conduct trials or examinations in the constellations of Hasta, Punarvasu, Śravaṇa, Jyeṣṭhā, or Śatabhiṣā. || 43 ||**<-

Avoiding the eighth and fourteenth lunar dates, Saturday, Tuesday, Bhadrā, the birth constellation, and the birth month; avoiding the eighth house from the Sun and Moon in transit, and the Nadi constellations (which are the 10th, 16th, 18th, 23rd, and 25th from the birth constellation). Excluding these, in dual-natured or movable ascendants, when the Moon, Jupiter, and the strength of the stars are purified, one should perform (Parīkṣā) trials or divine ordeals in the constellations of Hasta, Punarvasu, Śravaṇa, Jyeṣṭhā, and Śatabhiṣā. || 43 ||

---

->**<term>Anuṣṭubh</term> When the 12th and 8th houses are pure, and the ascendant is in an Upacaya house <note>Houses 3, 6, 10, and 11, which represent growth and improvement.</note> associated with or aspected by benefics, and the Moon is in the 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th house, every undertaking becomes successful. || 44 ||**<-

When the 12th and 8th houses from the ascendant are pure (free of planets), and the current ascendant is in an Upacaya house (3, 6, 10, 11) or the 1st house from the birth sign, and the Moon is in the 3rd, 6th, 11th, or 10th house—such purification of the ascendant ensures success in the commencement of all auspicious works. || 44 ||

---

->**<term>Upajāti</term> <note>A combination of Indra-vajra and Upendra-vajra meters.</note> In Svātī, Jyeṣṭhā, the three Pūrvās, Ārdrā, and Āśleṣā, if a fever or illness begins, it leads to death. In Revatī and Anurādhā, the illness becomes stable (long-lasting). In Bharaṇī, Śravaṇa, Śatabhiṣā, and Citrā, it lasts 11 days. In Viśākhā, Hasta, and Dhaniṣṭhā, it lasts 15 days. || 45 ||**<-

---

->**<term>Upendravajrā</term> In Mūla, Kṛttikā, and Aśvinī, it lasts 9 days. In Maghā, 20 days. In the three Uttarās, Puṣya, Punarvasu, and Rohiṇī, it lasts 7 days. In Mṛgaśīrṣa, Citrā, and Śatabhiṣā, if combined with the birth star or certain planetary positions, or in the case of a snake bite, it leads to death. || 46 ||**<-

If fever or other diseases arise in Svātī, Jyeṣṭhā, the three Pūrvās (Pūrvāphalguṇī, Pūrvāṣāḍhā, Pūrvābhādrapadā), Ārdrā, or Āśleṣā, death may occur. In Revatī and Anurādhā, the disease remains (stable) for many days. In Bharaṇī, Śravaṇa, Śatabhiṣā, and Citrā, it lasts for 11 days; in Viśākhā, Hasta, and Dhaniṣṭhā, for 15 days; in Mūla, Kṛttikā, and Aśvinī, for 9 days; in Maghā, for 20 days; and in the three Uttarās (Uttarāphalguṇī, Uttarāṣāḍhā, Uttarābhādrapadā), Puṣya, Punarvasu, and Rohiṇī, for 7 days.

# The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Timing

<page-num>38</page-num>
<header>Muhurta Chintamani <note>The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Timing</note></header>

In the constellations of Mrigashira and Uttarashadha, an illness lasts for 30 days. If a snake bites during Bharani, Ashlesha, Mula, Mishra <note>The "mixed" constellations, usually referring to Vishakha and Anuradha</note>, Magha, Krittika, Vishakha, or Ardra, death occurs. || 45 || 46 ||

->(Upajati Meter) If a person falls ill during the constellations of Ardra, Ashlesha, Jyeshtha, Shatabhisha, Bharani, the three Purvas, Vishakha, Dhanishta, or Krittika; or on the days of malefic planets; or on the "Empty" lunar days (4th, 9th, 14th), the 12th, or the 6th lunar day, the patient shall meet death quickly. || 47 ||<-

If a person becomes ill during the constellations of Ardra, Ashlesha, Jyeshtha, Shatabhisha, Bharani, the three Purvas <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, Vishakha, Dhanishta, or Krittika, as well as on malefic weekdays <note>Tuesday and Saturday</note>, or on the "Empty" <term>Rikta: lunar days that are considered "empty" or hollow for new beginnings</term> dates (4th, 9th, 14th), or the 12th or 6th lunar days, they will face death soon. This is especially certain if the Moon is transit through the 4th, 8th, or 12th house. || 47 ||

->(Indravajra Meter) One should avoid performing funeral rites, cremating the dead, traveling toward the southern direction, preparing beds, or repairing the house when the Moon is in Capricorn or Aquarius. || 48 ||<-

One should not perform <insert>(Funeral Rites)</insert> the last rites for the deceased during the constellations of Ashwini, Pushya, Hasta, Ashlesha, Mula, Mrigashira, Jyeshtha, Shravana, Ardra, or Swati. Furthermore, when the <insert>Moon is in Capricorn or Aquarius</insert>, the period known as <term>Panchak: a five-day period considered inauspicious for certain activities</term> occurs. During these times, one must avoid cremating the dead, traveling toward the South <note>The direction of Yama, the God of Death</note>, <insert>(Bedding)</insert> making beds, <insert>(Canopy)</insert> installing awnings, or plastering and repairing the house. By extension <note>original: "upalakshana"</note>, one should not collect grass or wood. If cremation is absolutely necessary during Panchak, one must make five effigies of the deceased out of Kusha grass and cotton and burn them alongside the body; a "Panchak Peace" ritual must also be performed. || 48 ||

->(Vasantatilaka Meter) The Tri-Pushkar yoga occurs when the Bhadra lunar days (2nd, 7th, 12th) coincide with Saturday, Tuesday, or Sunday, and specific constellations. This yoga triples the result of death, loss, or gain. || 49 ||<-

When the Bhadra lunar days (2nd, 7th, 12th) coincide with Saturday, Tuesday, or Sunday, and the constellations of Vishakha, Uttara Phalguni, or Purva Bhadrapada, the <term>Tri-Pushkar Yoga: the "Triple-Lotus" combination</term> is formed. If someone dies during this time, two more deaths will occur in that family. If an object is lost, two more will be lost. If something is gained or increased, it will be gained two more times. If the constellations are Dhanishta, Chitra, or Mrigashira instead, the results are doubled; this is called <term>Dvi-Pushkar: the "Double-Lotus" combination</term>. || 49 ||

->(Shardulavikridita Meter) [The verse continues regarding Venus, Saturn, and the lunar months...]<-

<summary>This page details astrological warnings regarding the timing of illnesses and the strict prohibitions for funeral rites during the "Panchak" and "Tri-Pushkar" periods. It explains that certain celestial alignments can cause a single event, like a death or a loss, to repeat two or three times.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of funeral rites and astrological timing from the previous section, specifically focusing on the ritual of burning an effigy when a body is missing.</meta>

<page-num>39</page-num>
<header>With Commentary—Chapter 2.</header>

If more than a year has passed since a person's death, or during the Sun's southern course <note>original: "Dakshinayana"</note>, or during the astrological combinations of Vyatipata or Parigha, or when Jupiter or Venus are set <note>invisible in the sky</note>, or during the periods of Bhadra or Vaidhriti, or during the dark fortnight of the lunar month—one should not perform the cremation of an effigy <note>original: "Parnashara" or "Pratikriti"; a ritual substitute made of leaves used when the physical body is unavailable</note>. || 50 ||

The person performing the ritual should ensure it is not their birth star, the "Pratyari" <note>the 5th star from one's birth star, considered hostile</note>, or when the Moon is in the 4th, 8th, or 12th house from their birth sign. The ritual is considered of "medium" quality if performed on a Wednesday or under the constellations of Anuradha, Purva Phalguni, Punarvasu, Shravana, Vishakha, Mrigashira, Chitra, or Dhanishtha. It is considered "best" if performed on a Sunday, Thursday, or Monday under the constellations of Shravana, Hasta, Swati, Pushya, or Ashvini. (These factors are considered after the period of ritual impurity <note>original: "Ashaucha"</note> has passed). If for some reason the funeral rites were not performed during the initial impurity period, then these timings must be calculated; however, during the immediate period of impurity, these astrological restrictions do not apply. || 51 ||

(In the Upajati meter) Narada states that the "unfavorable root" <note>original: "Abhukta Mula"; a particularly inauspicious astrological junction</note> lasts for four ghatikas <note>a unit of time roughly equal to 24 minutes</note> at the end of the Jyeshtha constellation and four at the beginning of Mula. Vashistha says it is one ghatika at the end of Jyeshtha and two at the start of Mula. Brihaspati, however, says it is only one ghatika in total. || 52 ||

<margin>4</margin> The measurement of the "unfavorable root" according to Narada's view is a total of 8 ghatikas (combining 4 from the end of Jyeshtha and 4 from the start of Mula). Vashistha claims one at the end and two at the start. Brihaspati claims only one ghatika. || 52 ||

(In the Upajati meter) Other teachers say that the first 8 ghatikas of Mula and the last 5 ghatikas of Jyeshtha constitute the "unfavorable root." If a child is born during this time, one should either abandon the child or the father should not look upon the child's face for eight years. || 53 ||

Other teachers say that 8 ghatikas of the beginning of Mula and 5 ghatikas of the end of Jyeshtha are the "unfavorable root." Because there are many opinions here, the author has accepted Narada's view as the standard. In this "unfavorable root"...

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological timing (Muhurta) from the previous section, specifically focusing on the remedial measures and consequences of births under the "Mula" and "Ashlesha" lunar mansions (Nakshatras).</meta>

# Muhurta Chintamani
<page-num>40</page-num>
->Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Timing<-

If a child is born in the *Mula* <note>The 19th lunar mansion, often associated with roots and destruction</note> constellation, one should either abandon the child or not look upon the child's face for eight years. After this period, a peace ritual <term>Shanti: a propitiatory rite to avert evil</term> must be performed. By extension, the same consideration applies to the end of *Ashlesha* and the beginning of *Magha* constellations. || 53 ||

*(Upajati Meter)*
If born in the first quarter of *Mula*, it leads to the destruction of the father; in the second, the mother; in the third, wealth. The fourth quarter is auspicious after performing the peace ritual. In the constellation of the Serpent (*Ashlesha*), the results are exactly the opposite. || 54 ||

Whether a girl or a boy is born in the first quarter of *Mula*, the father is destroyed. If in the second, the mother dies. If in the third, wealth is lost. If in the fourth quarter, it is auspicious after the peace ritual and no one suffers a defect. In *Ashlesha*, this logic is reversed: for example, the father dies in the fourth quarter, the mother in the third, wealth is lost in the second, and the first quarter becomes auspicious after the peace ritual. There is another variation: it destroys the father in 1 year, the mother in 3 years, wealth in 2 years, the father-in-law in 9 years, the brother in 5 years, the brother-in-law or maternal uncle in 8 years, and other unmentioned relatives in 7 years. Therefore, a peace ritual is necessary. Another method involves a specific analysis of *Mula* and *Ashlesha* using a diagrammatic placement <term>Chakranyasa</term> in the form of a tree or vine, as described in the chart. || 54 ||

| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |

*(Indravajra Meter)*
In the months of Ashadha, Bhadrapada, Ashvin, and Magha, *Mula* resides in Heaven. In Shravana, Kartika, Chaitra, and Pausha, it resides on Earth. In Phalguna, Margashirsha, Vaishakha, and Jyeshtha, it resides in the Underworld <note>original: "Patala"</note>. It yields its fruit only where it resides during that month. In other realms, there is no significant defect. || 55 ||

# With Vernacular Commentary - Chapter 2.

(Verse in Shardulavikridita meter) Birth during the following times is considered inauspicious: Gandanta, Jyeshtha, Shula, Pata, Parigha, Vyaghata, Atiganda, the transition of the sun (Sankranti), Vyatipata, Vaidhriti, Sinivali, Kuhu, Darsha, Vajra, the fourteenth day of the dark fortnight, Yamaghanta, Dagdha Yoga, Mrityu Yoga, and Vishti (Bhadra). Similarly, birth in the birth star of one's siblings or parents is harmful unless specific peace-making rituals are performed. || 56 ||

<note>The text lists specific astrological "Yogas" (alignments) and lunar days deemed spiritually volatile for a newborn.</note>

Birth during Gandanta, Jyeshtha, Shula, Pata, Parigha, Vyaghata, Atiganda, a diminished lunar day (Kshaya Tithi), the sun's entry into a new sign (Sankranti), Vyatipata, Vaidhriti, (Sinivali) the first half of the first day of the bright fortnight, (Kuhu) the second half of the fourteenth day of the dark fortnight, (Darsha) the New Moon day, Vajra Yoga, the fourteenth day of the dark fortnight, Yamaghanta, Dagdha Yoga, Mrityu Yoga, and Bhadra is considered harmful for a son or daughter. Birth during the birth star (Nakshatra) of a sibling or parents is also inauspicious. The rituals to pacify these effects are described in other texts; by performing them, auspiciousness is restored. By extension, this also includes births during an eclipse or the "Trika" <note>A sequence where a daughter is born after three sons, or a son after three daughters</note>. || 56 ||

---

(Verse in Upajati meter) The number of stars for the Nakshatras starting from Ashwini are: three, three, five, fire (three), four, five, two, two, five, five, two, two, five, one, one, four, four, three, eleven, three, two, three, three, four, a hundred, two, two, and thirty-two. || 57 ||

The number of stars belonging to the Nakshatras starting from Ashwini are stated: Ashwini has 3, Bharani 3, Krittika 6, Rohini 5, Mrigashira 3, Ardra 1, Punarvasu 4, Pushya 3, Ashlesha 5, Magha 5, Purva Phalguni 2, Uttara Phalguni 2, Hasta 5, Chitra 1, Swati 1, Vishakha 4, Anuradha 4, Jyeshtha 3, Mula 11, Purva Ashadha 3, Uttara Ashadha 2, Abhijit 3, Shravana 3, Dhanishtha 4, Shatabhisha 100, Purva Bhadrapada 2, Uttara Bhadrapada 2, and Revati has 32. These stars are identified by these counts and their specific forms described hereafter. || 57 ||

The visual forms of the Nakshatras: Ashwini is like a horse's face, Bharani like the female organ, Krittika like a <term>razor</term> <note>original: "kshura"</note>, Rohini like a cart, Mrigashira like a deer's head, Ardra like a gem, Punarvasu like a house, Pushya like an arrow, Ashlesha like a wheel, Magha like a house, Purva Phalguni like a couch, Uttara Phalguni like a bed, Hasta like a hand, Chitra like a pearl, Swati like coral, Vishakha like an archway, Anuradha like a heap of rice, Jyeshtha like an earring, Mula like a lion's tail, Purva Ashadha like an elephant's tusk, Uttara Ashadha like a small bed, Abhijit like a triangle, Shravana like a dwarf (or three steps), Dhanishtha like a drum, Shatabhisha like a circle, Purva Bhadrapada like a small bed, Uttara Bhadrapada like a pair (twins), and Revati is in the form of a drum. || 58 || 59 ||
