# Chapter 2: Commentary in Common Language

<page-num>43</page-num>
<header>With Commentary in Common Language - Section 2.</header>

> *(Upajati Meter)* For the consecration of reservoirs, gardens, and deities, the northern transit of the sun is best, provided Jupiter, the Moon, and Venus are visible. It should occur during the bright fortnight, under "soft," "fixed," "swift," or "mobile" stars, or on the specific day and lunar date associated with the deity. || 60 ||

The <term>Muhurta</term> <note>an auspicious time period calculated through astrology</note> for establishing water sources (wells/tanks), gardens, and the installation of deities is described: It should be done during the <term>Uttarayana</term> <note>the six-month period when the sun travels north, considered spiritually auspicious</note> when Jupiter, the Moon, and Venus are rising (visible). One should choose "soft," "fixed," "swift," or "mobile" <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>lunar mansions or star clusters</note> during the bright half of the lunar month, on an auspicious date and time. Furthermore, it should be done under the specific star associated with the deity being installed—for example, Shravana for Vishnu, Ardra for Shiva, or Purvashadha and Shatabhisha for water reservoirs. One must avoid "Empty" lunar dates (<term>Rikta Tithis</term>) and Tuesdays. This explanation also encompasses the first part of the following verse. || 60 ||

> *(Upajati Meter)* Avoid "Empty" dates and Sundays; the placement of the Moon and malefic planets in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses is highly praised. Benefic planets should not be in the 8th or 12th houses. The Sun should be installed in Leo, Brahma in Aquarius, Vishnu in Virgo, Shiva in Gemini, and the Goddesses in dual signs. Minor deities should be placed in fixed signs. Planets should be installed in Pushya; Ganesha, Yakshas, and spirits at the end; and the Buddha (Jina) in Shravana. || 61-62 ||

->Thus ends the second chapter on Nakshatras in the "Jewel of Auspicious Moments" composed by Sri Rama.<-

The meaning of the first quarter was explained previously. The rest is as follows: For the consecration of reservoirs and gardens, only the auspiciousness of the <term>Lagna</term> <note>the rising sign or ascendant</note> needs to be considered, without specific planetary combinations. For the installation of deities, it is auspicious if the Moon and malefic planets are in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses, and benefic planets are absent from the 8th and 12th houses. Specifically: the Sun's image should be installed when Leo is rising; Brahma in Aquarius; Vishnu in Virgo; Shiva in Gemini; and the Goddesses in dual signs (Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, or Pisces). Dakshinamurti and similar forms should be installed in mobile signs. Minor deities like the sixty-four Yoginis and the directional guardians (Indra, etc.) should be established in fixed signs. 

Furthermore, the nine planets should be installed under the Pushya star (or the Sun under Hasta). Shiva and Brahma are suited for Pushya, Shravana, or Abhijit; Kubera and Skanda for Anuradha; Durga and others for Mula. The Seven Sages, Vyasa, and Valmiki should be installed in the stars where the Seven Sages (Big Dipper) are observed. Alternatively, Ganesha, sacrificial fires, Nagas (serpents), spirits, celestial musicians, and other supernatural beings should be installed in Pushya. The Buddha (Jina) should be installed in Revati; Indra and the world-protectors (excluding Kubera) in Shravana; and the remaining deities in the three "Uttara" stars or Rohini. || 61-62 ||

---

->Thus ends the second chapter on Nakshatras in the "Jewel of Auspicious Moments" (Muhurta Chintamani), composed by Rama, son of the astrologer Ananta, including the "Mahidhari" commentary and the common language translation by Mahidhara. || 2 ||<-

<header>The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments</header>

# Now, the Chapter on the Sun’s Transit (Sankranti)

<note>The following verse is in the Vasantatilaka meter.</note>
The transit of the Sun is called **Ghora** when it occurs on a Sunday in the "fierce" asterisms <note>original: "ugra-nakshatra"</note>; it brings joy to the Shudras. In the "light" asterisms on a Monday, it is **Dhwankshi**, favoring the Vaishyas. In the "mobile" asterisms on a Tuesday, it is **Mahodara**, favoring thieves. In the "friendly" asterisms on a Wednesday, it is **Mandakini**, bringing happiness to kings. In the "steady" asterisms on a Thursday, it is **Manda**, favoring Brahmins. In the "mixed" asterisms on a Friday, it is **Mishra**, favoring cattle. In the "sharp" asterisms on a Saturday, it is **Rakshasi**, favoring the outcasts. || 1 ||

The movement of a planet from one zodiac sign to another is called a **Sankranti** <term>Sankranti: The moment the Sun enters a new zodiacal sign, marking the start of a solar month</term>. This is of two types: (1) Mean and (2) True. Here, leaving aside the mean transit, we describe the True Transit. This is also of two types: Tropical <note>with precession</note> and Sidereal <note>without precession</note>. Regarding the transit times of other planets, we shall discuss them in the Marriage Chapter under the section "Duties regarding Divine Property." Here, we primarily describe the Sun’s transit based on the day of the week and the lunar mansion (Nakshatra).

If the Sun’s sidereal transit occurs in the "Fierce" asterisms (the three Purvas, Bharani, or Magha) on a Sunday, it is named **Ghora** and pleases the Shudra class. If it occurs in a "Light" asterism on a Monday, it is named **Dhwankshi** and gives happiness to the Vaishya <note>merchant</note> class. If in a "Mobile" asterism on a Tuesday, it is **Mahodara** and brings success to thieves. If in a "Friendly" asterism on a Wednesday, it is **Mandakini** and gives happiness to Kings. If in a "Steady" asterism on a Thursday, it is **Manda** and gives happiness to Brahmins. If in a "Mixed" asterism on a Friday, it is **Mishra** and benefits cattle. If in a "Sharp" asterism on a Saturday, it is **Rakshasi** and gives happiness to Chandalas <note>those outside the formal caste system</note>. || 1 ||

If the transit occurs in the first third of the day, it destroys kings; in the middle, Brahmins; in the last, Vaishyas; and at sunset, Shudras. || 2 || If in the watches of the night, it destroys ghosts, night-wanderers, actors, and herdsmen respectively. At sunrise, it destroys all those wearing sectarian marks. The transits into Capricorn and Cancer are called the Northern and Southern paths. || 3 ||

By dividing the duration of the day into three parts, we get the portions. If the transit occurs in the first portion of the day, it is said to bring destruction (inauspicious results) to kings; if in the second (middle) portion, to Brahmins; in the third, to Vaishyas; and at the time of sunset, to Shudras. If it occurs in the first watch <note>Prahara, a 3-hour period</note> of the night, it harms ghosts and spirits; in the second, night-stalkers; in the third, dancers/actors; in the fourth, cattle herders. At the moment of sunrise, it brings destruction to "Lingis" <note>those wearing the outward garb of ascetics or hypocrites</note>. Furthermore, the transit into Capricorn marks the **Uttarayana** <note>Northern Path/Winter Solstice</note> and the transit into Cancer marks the **Dakshinayana** <note>Southern Path/Summer Solstice</note>. According to other texts, the Aries transit in Bharani—

It also identifies the beginning of the Northern and Southern solar cycles.</summary>

--- Page 62 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological transitions (Sankranti) and the auspicious timing for rituals. It follows the previous page's focus on planetary influences on agriculture and social stability.</meta>

<page-num>45</page-num>
<header>With Commentary - Chapter 3</header>

If the Sun enters a new sign while in the 4 constellations starting from Rohini, there is an increase in grain; if in the 10 starting from Magha, there is loss; in other constellations, there is happiness. A transition occurring in one's birth constellation brings auspiciousness to kings but causes distress and loss of wealth to commoners. The results of the transition year are: if it falls in the 1st, 6th, 12th, or 4th house, there is happiness and abundance; in the 11th, 9th, 5th, or 3rd, there is disease and war; in the 2nd, 8th, 7th, or 10th, there is disease, theft, and fear of fire. || 2 || 3 ||

->(Verse: Anushtubh meter) The *Shadashitimukha* occurs in Sagittarius, Gemini, Virgo, and Pisces.<-
->The *Vishuvati* occurs in Libra and Aries; the *Vishnupada* occurs in Leo, Scorpio, Taurus, and Aquarius. || 4 ||<-

The transition of the Sun into Sagittarius, Gemini, Virgo, and Pisces is named *Shadashitimukha* <note>A specific astrological period roughly translated as "eighty-six mouths"</note>; into Libra and Aries is called *Vishuvati* <note>Equinox</note>; and into Leo, Scorpio, Taurus, and Aquarius is called *Vishnupada* <note>The "Foot of Vishnu"</note>. Their significance is that the moments at the beginning of the Southern Transit (*Dakshinayana*), the middle of the *Vishnupada*, the 7th and 8th hours of the *Shadashitimukha*, and the moments following the entry into Capricorn are extremely meritorious for performing rituals. || 4 ||

->(Verse: Upajati meter) Sixteen *ghatikas* <note>A ghatika is a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> before and sixteen after the<-
->moment of transition are considered auspicious. If the transition occurs at night,<-
->if before midnight, the latter half of the previous day is auspicious; if after midnight,<-
->the first half of the following day is the meritorious time. || 5 ||<-

From the time of the Sun's transition, 16 *ghatikas* (approx. 6.4 hours) before and 16 *ghatikas* after is the *Punya-kala* <note>meritorious or auspicious time</note>. If the transition happens at night: if it occurs before midnight, the second half of the preceding day is auspicious; if it occurs after midnight, the first half of the succeeding day is the auspicious time. || 5 ||

->(Verse: Upendravajra meter) If the transition occurs exactly at midnight, both days are<-
->auspicious. If the transition into Cancer occurs after sunset,<-
->the following day is the meritorious time. || 6 ||<-

If the transition occurs exactly at midnight, then both the preceding and succeeding days are considered auspicious times. If the transition into Cancer (*Karka Sankranti*) occurs after sunset, then the following day is the meritorious time. || 6 ||

->(Verse: Indravajra meter) The twilight period is measured as 3 *ghatikas* before sunrise and<-
->3 *ghatikas* after sunset. Based on this, the auspicious days for the Southern and<-
->Northern transits are determined respectively as the previous or following days. || 7 ||<-

The period of 3 *ghatikas* (72 minutes) before sunrise and 3 *ghatikas* after sunset is known as the twilight (*Sandhya*). For this reason, the days surrounding the transitions into Cancer and Capricorn are called auspicious: if the Southern Transit (*Dakshinayana*) occurs during the morning twilight, the previous day is suitable for bathing and charity; if the Northern Transit (*Uttarayana*) occurs during the evening twilight, the following day is suitable for these meritorious acts. || 7 ||

->(Verse: Anushtubh meter) In the Southern Transit and the *Vishnupada*, the beginning is auspicious; in the Equinoxes, the middle;<-
->and in the *Shadashitimukha* and Northern Transit, the later hours are most meritorious. || 8 ||<-

<summary>This page details the specific astrological timings (Sankranti) for the Sun's entry into various zodiac signs and defines the "Punya-kala" or auspicious windows for ritual bathing and charity. It categorizes transitions into groups like Vishnupada and Shadashitimukha, providing rules for midnight and twilight occurrences.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the Muhurta Chintamani, a 16th-century Sanskrit treatise on electional astrology. The previous sections likely established the basic definitions of solar transits (Sankrantis).</meta>

# Muhurta Chintamani
<page-num>46</page-num>

-> Precious Gem of Electional Astrology <-

The 16 ghatis <note>A ghati is a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> prior to the transits of the Southern Solstice and the *Vishnupada* <note>The fixed signs: Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, and Aquarius</note> (4, 2, 5, 8, 11), the period between the Libra and Aries transits, and the 16 ghatis following the *Shadashityanana* <note>The dual signs: Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, and Pisces</note> (3, 6, 9, 12) and Capricorn transits are considered extremely meritorious for spiritual practice. || 8 ||

**The precession of the equinoxes multiplied by sixty and divided by the daily motion of the Sun results in the time in days and ghatis.**
**These "moving transits" occurring before the Aries transit are highly auspicious for charity, chanting, and other rituals. || 9 ||**

Having described the sidereal transits (*Nirayana*), the author now describes the tropical transits (*Sayana*): Multiply the degrees of precession (*Ayanamsha*) by 60 and divide by the Sun's clear daily motion to find the result in days and ghatis. This "moving transit" occurs that many days prior to the standard Aries transit time; it is considered to grant great merit for charity and meditation. || 9 ||

**The transits occurring in Soft, Swift, Dhanishtha, Shravana, Krittika, Magha, the three Purvas, and Mula are "Even."**
**Those in Fixed, Vishakha, and Punarvasu are "Great," while those in Ashlesha, Shatabhisha, Ardra, Svati, Jyeshtha, and Bharani are "Low." || 10 ||**

The 15 lunar mansions (*Nakshatras*)—Soft, Swift, Dhanishtha, Shravana, Krittika, Magha, the three Purvas (Purva Phalguni, Purvashadha, Purva Bhadrapada), and Mula—are designated as "Even" (*Sama*). The 6 mansions—Fixed, Vishakha, and Punarvasu—are designated as "Great" (*Brihat*). The 6 mansions—Ashlesha, Shatabhisha, Ardra, Svati, Jyeshtha, and Bharani—are designated as "Low" (*Jaghanya*). || 10 ||

**In a "Low" transit, the duration is 15 muhurtas; in "Great," it is 45;**
**In "Even," it is 30. These numbers indicate high prices, low prices, or stability, respectively; the same applies to the moon's appearance. || 11 ||**

If the transit occurs in "Low" nakshatras, it lasts 15 muhurtas <note>A muhurta is 48 minutes</note>; in "Great," 45; and in "Even," 30. If the transit is 15 muhurtas, the price of grain will be high (*Mahargha*); if 45, it will be cheap and plentiful (*Sulabha*); if 30, the prices will remain stable (*Sama*). The same logic should be applied when observing the first appearance of the moon. || 11 ||

**When the Cancer transit falls on a Sunday, the year-value is ten;**
**Then twenty, eight, twelve, eighteen, eighteen, and five for the following days. || 12 ||**

If the Cancer transit (*Karka Sankranti*) occurs on a Sunday, the *Abda-vishopaka* <note>A numerical index used to predict the prosperity or "strength" of the year</note> is 10; on Monday, 20; on Tuesday, 8; on Wednesday, 12; on Thursday, 18; on Friday, 18; and on Saturday, 5. || 12 ||

**In Taitila, Naga, and Chatushpada, the Sun is "Sleeping"; in the five starting with Gara, he is "Seated."**
**In Kimstughna he is "Upright," and in Shakuni and Kaulava he is "Destroyed." These states indicate the quality of prices and rainfall. || 13 ||**

If the transit occurs during the *Karanas* <note>A Karana is half a lunar day</note> of Taitila, Naga, or Chatushpada, the Sun is considered "Sleeping," which is inauspicious for grain prices and rainfall. In the five starting with Gara (Gara, Vanija, Vishti, Balava, <insert>Kaulava</insert>)...

<summary>This page details the auspicious timing of solar transits (Sankrantis) and their classification based on lunar mansions and astrological divisions to predict economic conditions and rainfall. It provides specific numerical values for the "strength of the year" based on the weekday the Cancer transit occurs.</summary>

<page-num>47</page-num>
<header>With Commentary—Chapter 3.</header>

In the *Bava* <note>The first of the eleven Karanas or astrological divisions of a lunar day</note> Karana, the influence is moderate; above *Kinstughna*, *Shakuni* and *Kaulava* are considered superior; this will be explained clearly further on. || 13 ||

->(In the *Shardulavikridita* meter)<-
The vehicles of the Sun's transit, starting from *Bava*, are the lion, tiger, boar, donkey, elephant, buffalo, and horse; then the dog, goat, ox, and the "weapon-footed" <note>original: "charanayudha" - a poetic term for a rooster or fowl</note> bird. || 14 ||
The garments are white, bright yellow, green, pale-white, deep red, black, and dark blue; then variegated, a woolen blanket, and cloud-colored. The weapons are the *bhushundi* <note>An ancient fire-arm or a spiked mace</note>, mace, sword, staff, bow, tomara-spear, lance, noose, and goad; then arrows. The foods are grain, rice-pudding, alms-food, fried sweets, milk, curd, variegated rice, jaggery, honey-mixed food, clarified butter, and sugar. || 15 ||
The ointments are musk, saffron, red sandalwood, earth, yellow pigment <note>original: "gorochana" - a bright yellow pigment found in a cow's gall bladder, used for tilaks</note>, turmeric, barley-alkali, collyrium, black aloe-wood, and sandalwood. The classes affected are the gods, spirits, serpents, birds, and the five groups: Brahmins, Kshatriyas, Vaishyas, Shudras, and those of mixed caste. The flowers are the Punnaaga, Jasmine, Bakula, Ketaki, Bilva, Arka, Durva grass, and Lotus. || 16 ||

->(In the *Indravajra* meter)<-
There are also the Mallika, Patali, and Japa flowers. If the Sun enters a sign while the personified Transit is wearing such clothes, eating such food, or riding such a vehicle, it brings destruction to those things and to those who earn their living through them, whether they are standing, sitting, or sleeping. || 17 ||

The seven *Karana* <term>Karana: Half of a lunar day (Tithi). There are 11 types in total.</term> known as *Bava, Balava, Kaulava, Taitila, Gara, Vanija,* and *Vishti* are "movable," while *Shakuni, Kinstughna, Naga,* and *Chatushpada* are "fixed." We now describe the vehicles and other attributes based on which Karana the *Sankranti* <term>Sankranti: The moment the Sun enters a new zodiac sign.</term> occurs in:

1. **Bava:** Lion | White garment | *Bhushundi* <note>A specific type of staff or ancient weapon</note> | Grain | Musk.
2. **Balava:** Tiger | Yellow garment | Mace | Rice-pudding | Saffron.
3. **Kaulava:** Boar | Blue <note>original: "haritaka" - green/bluish-green</note> garment | Sword | Alms-food | Red sandalwood.
4. **Taitila:** Donkey | Pink garment | Staff | Fried sweets | Earth.
5. **Gara:** Elephant | Red garment | Bow | Milk | Yellow pigment.
6. **Vanija:** Buffalo | Black garment | Arrow | Curd | Turmeric.
7. **Vishti:** Horse | Dark blue garment | Spiked club | Variegated rice | Barley-alkali <insert>Yavaka</insert>.
8. **Shakuni:** Dog | Variegated garment | Lance | Jaggery.
9. **Chatushpada:** Ram | Blanket | Noose | Honey-food.
10. **Naga:** Ox | Naked <note>Representing lack of cloth/bareness</note> | Goad | Ghee.
11. **Kinstughna:** Rooster | Cloud-colored | Arrow | Sugar.

The purpose of these listed vehicles and attributes is as follows: in those specific months, these objects—or those who make a living from these objects (whether standing, sitting, or sleeping)—will face destruction.

-> (Upajati Meter) Counting from the Sun's transit constellation through the birth constellation: in groups of three, it signifies travel, then happiness, then pain, then physical comfort, then loss of wealth, and finally the arrival of wealth. <-

**Translation of the Verse:**
Count from the constellation <term>Nakshatra: lunar mansion</term> in which the Sun's transit <term>Sankranti: the entry of the Sun into a new zodiac sign</term> occurs, starting from that first constellation up to your own birth constellation. If the count is within the first 3, there will be travel in that month. If it is in the next 6, there is happiness. The next 3 signify pain/affliction. The next 6 signify gain of clothing and comforts. The next 3 signify loss of wealth. The final 6 signify the arrival of wealth. || 18 ||

--- Page 66 ---
<meta>This page concludes the chapter on Solar Transits (Sankranti) and begins the chapter on Planetary Transits (Gochara) from the Muhurta Chintamani.</meta>

<page-num>49</page-num>
<header>Commentary included - Chapter 4.</header>

->(Upajati Meter)<-
Seeking an audience with the King, all auspicious works, battle, the study of scriptures, marriage, and initiation are governed by the strength of the Sun. When the strength of the stars <note>Tarabala: the strength derived from the position of the moon relative to one's birth star</note> is favorable, the Moon becomes auspicious. When the Moon is strong, the Sun is auspicious; when the Sun is strong, Mars and the other planets become auspicious. || 19 ||

By observing the strength of the Sun or on Sundays, it is auspicious to seek an audience with the King. Similarly, all auspicious deeds are governed by the Moon, battles by Mars, the teaching or learning of scriptures by Mercury, marriage by Jupiter, journeys by Venus, and sacrificial initiations by Saturn. Furthermore, the Moon should be understood as auspicious through the strength of the stars; if the stars are favorable during a lunar transition, even an otherwise unfavorable Moon becomes auspicious. Likewise, a solar transition is auspicious if the Moon is strong. Other planetary transitions, such as those of Mars, are auspicious when the Sun is in an "Upachaya" <note>The 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th houses from the natal sign, associated with growth and increase</note> position. || 19 ||

->(Upendravajra Meter)<-
A month devoid of a clear solar transit is called an "Adhimasa" <note>Intercalary or leap month, also known as Malmasa</note>. A "Kshayamasa" <note>A decayed or lost month</note> occurs when two transits fall within a single lunar month. In that case, the two divisions of the month are designated as the "First" and "Last" months. || 20 ||
Thus ends the Chapter on Transits in the Muhurta Chintamani, composed by Rama, son of the astrologer Ananta. || 3 ||

A lunar month lasts from the bright first day <note>Shukla Pratipada</note> to the new moon <note>Amavasya</note>. If this month lacks a clear solar transit, it is called an Adhimasa, Malmasa, or Laund. Similarly, if two clear solar transits occur within said month, it is a Kshayamasa. In a Kshayamasa, for rituals concerning birth or death, if the date falls in the first half, the former month is considered; if in the latter half, the subsequent month is used for anniversary rites. || 20 ||
Thus ends the third chapter in the vernacular commentary by Mahidhara on the Muhurta Chintamani. || 3 ||

---

## Now begins the Chapter on Planetary Transits (Gochara).

->(Upajati Meter)<-
The Sun is auspicious in the 6th, 11th, 10th, and 3rd houses. Mars, Saturn, and Rahu <note>The North Node of the Moon, referred to here as "Tamas" or darkness</note> are auspicious in the 6th, 11th, and 3rd. The Moon is auspicious in the 6th, 9th, 11th, 5th, 3rd, 10th, 7th, and 1st. || 1 ||
Mercury is auspicious in the 11th, 8th, 1st, 5th, 6th, 2nd, 4th, and 3rd. Jupiter is auspicious in the 6th, 9th, 8th, 1st, 10th, 11th, 12th, 5th, and 4th. || 2 ||
->(Indravajra Meter)<-
Venus is auspicious in the 11th, 9th, 10th, 2nd, 3rd, 11th, 1st, 8th, 2nd, 7th, 3rd, 1st, 4th, 10th, 5th, 9th, 8th, and 11th <note>The text lists specific numerical codes for houses where planets provide benefits</note>. The Sun is auspicious in the 9th, 10th, 11th, 6th, and 1st. || 3 ||
->(Upendravajra Meter)<-
A planet is considered "pierced" <term>Vedha: a state where the auspicious effect of a planet is nullified by another planet in a specific opposing position</term> and loses its auspiciousness accordingly. However, they say there is no "Vedha" between a father and son <note>Specifically between the Sun and Saturn, or the Moon and Mercury</note>. Even a malefic planet becomes auspicious through "Reverse Vedha," and the Moon and Venus are auspicious in the trine positions. || 4 ||

# Page 50
## Muhurta Chintamani
<note>The "Jewel of Thoughts on Auspicious Moments," a classic 16th-century Sanskrit text on Hindu astrology.</note>

The movement of planets from the <term>Janma Rashi</term><note>The zodiac sign the moon was in at the time of birth</note> is called <term>Gochar</term><note>Transit; the current position of planets in the sky</note>. The Sun is auspicious in the 6th, 12th, 10th, 4th, 3rd, 9th, 11th, and 5th positions from the birth sign, but it can also be <term>Viddha</term><note>Pierced or obstructed; a state where the positive influence of a planet is blocked by another planet in a specific opposing position</note>. For example, if the Sun is in the 6th house and any planet is in the 12th house, an obstruction (Vedha) occurs. Similarly, the 10th is obstructed by the 4th, the 3rd by the 9th, and the 11th by the 5th. However, there is no mutual obstruction between "father and son" pairs: Saturn and the Sun, or the Moon and Mercury.

Furthermore, Mars, Saturn, and Rahu are auspicious or obstructed in the 6th, 9th, 11th, 5th, 3rd, and 12th positions. The Moon is auspicious or obstructed in the 10th, 4th, 3rd, 9th, 11th, 8th, 1st, 5th, 6th, 12th, 7th, and 2nd positions in the aforementioned order. Mercury in the 2nd, 5th, etc.; Jupiter in the 5th, 4th, 2nd, 12th, 9th, 10th, 7th, 3rd, 11th, 8th; and Venus in the 1st, 8th, 2nd, 7th, 3rd, 1, 4, 10, 5, 9, 8, 5, 9, 11, 12, 6, 11, 3—these planets are auspicious in these positions without obstruction, but become inauspicious if obstructed. In positions not mentioned, they should be known as inauspicious. This is called "Regular Obstruction" (<term>Krama Vedha</term>). Opposite to this is "Reverse Obstruction" (<term>Vama Vedha</term>). For example, the regular obstruction for the Sun in the 6th is a planet in the 12th. If the Sun is in the 12th and is obstructed by a planet in the 6th, this is Reverse Obstruction. If a planet is in a "bad" house but experiences Reverse Obstruction, it becomes auspicious. During the bright fortnight (waxing moon), the Moon in the 2nd, 9th, or 5th positions is auspicious if not obstructed by planets in the 6th, 8th, or 4th positions.

->**Obstruction Chart (Vedha Chakra)**<-

| **Sun** | | | | **Mars, Sat, Rahu** | | | | **Moon** | | | | **Mercury** | | | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 6 | 10 | 3 | 11 | 6 | 11 | 3 | 10 | 3 | 11 | 1 | 6 | 7 | 2 | 4 | 6 |
| 12 | 4 | 9 | 5 | 9 | 5 | 12 | 4 | 9 | 8 | 5 | 12 | 2 | 5 | 3 | 9 |

| **Jupiter** | | | | | | | | **Venus** | | | | | | | | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 8 | 10 | 11 | 5 | 2 | 9 | 7 | 11 | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 8 | 9 | 12 | 11 |
| 1 | 8 | 12 | 4 | 12 | 10 | 3 | 8 | 8 | 7 | 1 | 10 | 9 | 5 | 11 | 6 | 3 |

---

### (Upajati Meter) Others say obstruction is calculated from the sign occupied by the planet; Kashyapa states this obstruction applies only between the Himalayas and the Vindhya mountains, not in all lands.

According to some opinions, there are two types of obstruction: one from the birth sign and another from the sign occupied by the planet. Sages like Kashyapa have described two variations based on the birth sign itself. For instance: if the Sun in the 6th from the birth sign is not obstructed by a planet in the 12th, it is auspicious. Alternatively, if the Sun is in the 12th from the birth sign (usually unfavorable) but is obstructed by planets in the 6th from its own occupied sign (Reverse Obstruction), it becomes auspicious. These two types of obstruction are applicable in <term>Aryavarta</term><note>The "Land of the Noble," historically the northern part of the Indian subcontinent</note>, the region between the Himalayas and the Vindhya mountains, and not in all countries.

<summary>This page explains the concept of Vedha (astrological obstruction) in transit, detailing how specific planetary positions can cancel out the auspicious or inauspicious effects of others. It also notes a geographical limitation to these rules, citing the sage Kashyapa.</summary>
<keywords>Vedha, Gochar, Janma Rashi, Vama Vedha, Aryavarta, Muhurta Chintamani, Hindu Astrology, Planetary Transits</keywords>

# Chapter 4: Commentary in the Common Tongue

### Page 51

->**The Effects of Eclipses Based on Birth Signs**<-

*(Sanskrit Verse in Shardulavikridita Meter)*
The effects of an eclipse occurring in the various houses from the birth sign are: physical pain, loss, wealth, disease, distress to children, happiness, distress to the wife, death, loss of honor, comfort, gain, and destruction. To destroy these inauspicious effects, one should perform chanting, donate gold or cows, and perform peace-making rituals. Others say that one should simply avoid looking at an inauspicious eclipse. || 6 ||

<note>original: "जन्मर्क्षेनिधनंत्रयंह..."</note>

The results of an eclipse based on the **Birth Moon Sign** <term>Janmarashi: The zodiac sign the moon was in at the time of birth</term> are as follows:
1. If on the sign itself: Physical pain.
2. Second house: Loss.
3. Third: Wealth.
4. Fourth: Disease.
5. Fifth: Distress to children.
6. Sixth: Happiness.
7. Seventh: Distress to the wife.
8. Eighth: Death.
9. Ninth: Loss of honor.
10. Tenth: Comfort.
11. Eleventh: Gain.
12. Twelfth: Destruction.

These effects last for six months. To remove the inauspicious results, one should chant mantras such as the *Gayatri*, donate cows, land, or gold according to one's means, and perform the peace-making rituals <term>Shanti: Rituals performed to calm malevolent planetary influences</term> prescribed in the scriptures. Some authorities hold the opinion that the only remedy is to avoid looking at an eclipse that indicates unfavorable results. || 6 ||

*(Sanskrit Verse in Anushtubh Meter)*
Even a Moon in a favorable position becomes inauspicious if it is between malefic planets, joined with them, or in the seventh house from them. Conversely, even an inauspicious Moon becomes auspicious if it is in a favorable sub-division, a friend's sub-division, or is aspected by Jupiter. || 7 ||

Even a Moon situated in a favorable house gives inauspicious results if it is hemmed in by malefic planets, associated with them, or positioned in the seventh house from them. However, if the Moon is in a favorable <term>Navamsha: A one-ninth division of a sign used for fine-tuning predictions</term> or in the division of a great friend, and is viewed by Jupiter, then even an inauspicious Moon will grant favorable results. || 7 ||

*(Sanskrit Verse in Anushtubh Meter)*
The auspiciousness or inauspiciousness of the two fortnights depends on the Moon's transit on the first day of the bright and dark halves. If the transit is contrary, the results are contrary. This is the strength of the Moon in times of crisis. || 8 ||

If the Moon's transit <term>Gochar: The current movement of planets through the zodiac</term> is favorable on the first day of the bright fortnight <note>Shukla Paksha: The waxing phase of the moon</note>, then the entire bright fortnight is favorable. If it is unfavorable on the first day of the dark fortnight <note>Krishna Paksha: The waning phase of the moon</note>, then the entire dark fortnight is favorable. Understand the opposite to be true as well: if the Moon is unfavorable on the 1st day of the bright fortnight, that fortnight is unfavorable; if it is favorable on the 1st day of the dark fortnight, that fortnight is unfavorable. || 8 ||

->**The Arrangement of the Nine Gems (Navaratna)**<-

*(Sanskrit Verse in Shalini Meter)*
Diamond for Venus, Pearl for the Moon, Coral for Mars, Hessonite for Rahu, Blue Sapphire for Saturn, Cat's Eye for Ketu, Yellow Sapphire for Jupiter, Emerald for Mercury, and Ruby for the Sun in the center. || 9 ||

To ward off the evil effects of the planets, there is a method for wearing each planet's specific gem or the "Nine Gems" <term>Navaratna: A talismanic arrangement of nine specific gemstones</term>:
*   **Venus:** Diamond <insert>on the eastern edge of the ring or armband</insert>.
*   **Moon:** Pearl in the South-East.
*   **Mars:** Coral in the South.
*   **Rahu:** Hessonite in the South-West.
*   **Saturn:** Blue Sapphire in the West.
*   **Ketu:** Cat's Eye in the North-West.
*   **Jupiter:** Yellow Sapphire in the North.
*   **Mercury:** Emerald in the North-East.
*   **Sun:** Ruby in the center.

One should either arrange them this way or wear/donate the specific gem to please an individual planet. || 9 ||

<summary>This page details the astrological consequences of eclipses based on one's birth sign and provides a guide for the ritual use and placement of the nine sacred gemstones (Navaratna) to mitigate negative planetary influences.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, eclipse effects, birth sign, Navaratna, gemstones, remedial measures, Moon transit, Hindu calendar</keywords>

-> (In the Indravajra meter)
Ruby, Pearl, Coral, Emerald, Topaz, Diamond, Sapphire,
Hessonite, and Cat’s Eye are the gems; additionally, gold is for the pleasure of the Sun and others. <note>original: "माणिक्यमुक्ताफलविद्रुमाणि..." - This verse summarizes the primary gemstones associated with the nine celestial bodies to ensure their favor.</note> <-

It concludes with a mnemonic verse in Sanskrit regarding the primary gemstones for the nine planets.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological remedies, specifically focusing on the use of gemstones, metals, and herbal roots to mitigate planetary influences, as well as the calculation of 'Tara' (stellar influences) based on birth constellations.</meta>

<page-num>53</page-num>
<header>Chapter 4: Including the Vernacular Commentary</header>

The gemstones to be worn are as follows: for the Sun, a ruby <note>original: "Manikya" or "Chunni"</note>; for the Moon, a pearl; for Mars, coral; for Mercury, emerald; for Jupiter, topaz <note>original: "Pushparaj"</note>; for Venus, a diamond; for Saturn, a sapphire; for Rahu, chrysoberyl <note>original: "Vaidurya"</note>; for Ketu, emerald <note>original: "Markat"</note>. Additionally, it is stated that gold should be worn to please Mercury. || 10 ||

-> (Shalini Meter) Lapis Lazuli should be worn for Rahu and Ketu; silver for Venus and the Moon; a pearl for Jupiter; iron for Saturn; and coral for Mars and the Sun. The calculation of the "Tara" (stellar strength) is done by counting in three cycles from the birth star to the current day's star. || 11 || <-

If one does not have the means to wear expensive gemstones, one should wear gold for Mercury (as connected from the first verse). Furthermore, for Rahu and Ketu, wear Lapis Lazuli <note>original: "Lajavart"</note>; for the Moon and Venus, wear silver; for Jupiter, a pearl; for Saturn, iron; and for the Sun and Mars, wear coral. In other texts, wearing the roots of plants is also mentioned: for the Sun, the root of the Bael tree; for the Moon, the Dudhiya plant; for Mars, Gojihva; for Mercury, Vidhara; for Jupiter, Bharangi; for Venus, Simhapuchhi; for Saturn, Bichali; for Rahu, Sandalwood; and for Ketu, Ashwagandha. To find the "Tara," count from the birth constellation <note>term: Nakshatra; the star sign of the moon at birth</note> to the current day's constellation and divide into three cycles of 9 each. The resulting number determines the specific Tara. || 11 ||

-> (Anushtup Meter) The names of the Taras are Janma (Birth), Sampat (Wealth), Vipat (Misfortune), Kshema (Well-being), Pratyari (Opposition), Sadhaka (Achievement), Vadha (Death), Mitra (Friend), and Ati-Mitra (Great Friend). Their results correspond to their names. || 12 || <-

By counting according to the sequence mentioned in the previous verse, these Taras are identified. 1. Janma, 2. Sampat, 3. Vipat, 4. Kshema, 5. Pratyari, 6. Sadhaka, 7. Vadha, 8. Mitra, 9. Param-Mitra. Their fruits are exactly as their names suggest. Among these, the 3rd, 5th, and 7th Taras are considered inauspicious. || 12 ||

-> (Shardulavikridita Meter) In the Vadha Tara, one should donate gold and sesame seeds; in Vipat, jaggery; in the three Janma Taras, vegetables; and in the Pratyari Tara, salt. In the first cycle, Vipat, Pratyari, and Vadha are entirely inauspicious. In the second cycle, only the first 20 Ghatis of Vipat, the middle 20 of Pratyari, and the final 20 of Vadha are to be avoided. In the third cycle, all are considered auspicious. || 13 || <-

When necessary, there are remedies <note>term: Parihar; an act of atonement or neutralization</note> for the malefic Taras: in the 7th Tara (<term>Vadha</term>), donate sesame and gold; in the 3rd (<term>Vipat</term>), donate jaggery or sugar; in the <term>Janma</term> (1st) Tara, donate vegetables; and in the 5th (<term>Pratyari</term>), donate salt. Another method of neutralization is based on time: in the first cycle of nine stars, the 3rd, 5th, and 7th Taras are entirely bad for the full 60 Ghatis <note>term: Ghati; a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>. In the second cycle, one must only avoid the first 20 Ghatis of the 3rd, the middle 20 of the 5th, and the last 20 of the 7th. In the third cycle, they are all auspicious and cause no harm. || 13 ||

## (Anustup Meter) Multiply the elapsed lunar mansions by sixty, add the passed hours of the current mansion, and multiply by four.
## Divide by forty-five; the quotient indicates the state, starting from Aries. || 14 ||
<note>original: "षष्टिघ्नं गतभं भुक्तघटीयुक्तं युगाहतम् ॥ शराब्धिहृल्लब्धतोरर्कशेषवस्थाः क्रियादिघोः ॥" This Sanskrit verse provides the mathematical formula for the calculation described below.</note>

There are 12 states <term>Avasthas; specific conditions or 'moods' of a planet</term> of the Moon in each zodiac sign. These provide results consistent with their names at the start of all endeavors. Count the number of lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras</term> starting from Ashwini, multiply that number by 60, and add the passed hours <term>Bhukta-ghati; a ghati is a traditional unit of 24 minutes</term> of the current mansion. Multiply this total by 4 and divide by 45. The resulting quotient represents the completed states, and the remainder indicates the current state. If the quotient exceeds 12 after dividing by 45, divide by 12 again to find the elapsed state; the remainder determines the current state. For the Moon in Aries, the sequence begins with "Travel" and so on; in Taurus, it begins with "Destruction"; in Gemini, with "Death." The sequence for counting these states is detailed in the chart below. || 14 ||

### Chart of the Moon's States

| Ardra | 3.75 Death | 15 Victory | 26.25 Laughter | 37.5 Pleasure | 48.75 Play | 60 Sleep |

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of planetary states and remedial measures for counteracting malefic astrological influences, following the traditional Sanskrit text of the Muhurta Chintamani.</meta>

<page-num>( 56 )</page-num>
<header>The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments</header>

->(Upajati Meter)<-
Travel, destruction, death, victory, laughter, and dissatisfaction; play, sleep, and eating;
Fever, trembling, and stability—these are the twelve states <note>Sanskrit: *Avasthas*</note> starting from Aries, and their results correspond to their names. || 15 ||

**The names of the states:** 1. Travel, 2. Destruction, 3. Death, 4. Victory, 5. Laughter, 6. Dissatisfaction, 7. Play, 8. Sleep, 9. Eating, 10. Fever, 11. Trembling, 12. Stability. Their results are exactly as their names suggest. || 15 ||

->(Shardulavikridita Meter)<-
A bath prepared with parched grain, costus, mallow, perfumed cherry, nutgrass, mustard, turmeric, and Himalayan cedar,
Combined with purple tephrosia and lodh-tree bark, is said to destroy the sins arising from the planets.
A cow, a conch, a red bull, gold, yellow cloth, a horse,
A black cow, a sword, and a goat—these are the ritual gifts <note>Sanskrit: *Dakshina*</note> for the Sun and the others. || 16 ||

**Medicinal herbs for the bath to ward off evil planets:** Mix parched grain (or sensitive plant), costus, mallow, perfumed cherry, nutgrass, mustard, Himalayan cedar, turmeric, purple tephrosia, and lodh-tree bark in water. Bathing with this removes the misfortune caused by planets. **Regarding the ritual gifts:** To please the Sun, give a cow; for the Moon, a conch; for Mars, a red bull; for Mercury, gold; for Jupiter, yellow garments; for Venus, a horse; for Saturn, a black cow; for Rahu, a sword; and for Ketu, a goat. || 16 ||

->(Upajati Meter)<-
The Sun, Mars, Mercury, and Venus give results five, eight, seven, and seven days before entering a sign; the Moon, three ghatis <note>A ghati is 24 minutes; 3 ghatis equal 72 minutes</note>;
Rahu, Saturn, and Jupiter give results three, six, and four months before. These are the effects of the upcoming sign. || 17 ||

The Sun gives the results of the zodiac sign it is about to enter 5 days in advance. Similarly, Mars 8 days before, Mercury 7 days, Venus 7 days, the Moon 3 ghatis before, Rahu 3 months, Saturn 6 months, and Jupiter 2 months—that is, when the planet reaches 27 degrees of its current sign, it begins to yield the results of the next sign. || 17 ||

->(Shalini Meter)<-
In an evil Yoga, one should give gold; for the Moon, a conch; for a half-tithi, grain; for a Tithi, rice;
For the Day, a gem; for the Nakshatra, a cow; for a Muhurta, gold; and for the Stars, the King should give sea-salt. || 18 ||

**Charity for evil astrological combinations during essential tasks:** Here "King" is a general term for the seeker. In bad yogas like *Vyatipata*, give gold; if the Moon is afflicted, a conch; for a *Tithi* <note>Lunar day</note>, rice; for the day of the week, a precious gem; for the zodiac sign, a cow; for an inauspicious *Muhurta*, gold; and for the *Tara* <note>Star/Constellation</note>, give salt. || 18 ||

->(Vasantatilaka Meter)<-
The Sun and Mars give results at the beginning of a sign; Venus and Jupiter in the middle; always—

<page-num>57</page-num>
<header>With Commentary—Chapter 5. (57)</header>

Mercury, the Moon, and Saturn give their full results in the final portion of a sign. Travel, food, fear of fire, virtuous thoughts, acquisition of clothing, happiness, and sorrow are the results when the birth star falls on a Sunday or subsequent days of the week in a given month. || 19 ||

->Thus ends the fourth chapter on Transits in the *Muhurtachintamani*<-
->composed by Rama, the son of the astrologer Ananta. || 4 ||<-

The Sun, Mars, and Rahu <note>The North Node of the Moon</note> give their full results within the first 10 degrees of a zodiac sign; in other degrees, they give only partial results. Similarly, Venus and Jupiter give full results in the middle 10 degrees. Mercury gives full results throughout all 30 degrees. The Moon and Saturn give their full results in the final 10 degrees. In any given month, if the birth star <note>The Nakshatra the moon was in at the time of birth</note> falls on a Sunday, it indicates travel; on a Monday, it brings food and provisions; on a Tuesday, fear of fire; on a Wednesday, a religious or virtuous mind; on a Thursday, the acquisition of clothes; on a Friday, happiness; and on a Saturday, it brings sorrow. || 19 ||

->Thus ends the fourth chapter on Transits in the commentary on the *Muhurtachintamani* by Mahidhara. || 4 ||<-

# Now begins the Chapter on Life-Cycle Rituals.

(Verse) The first menstruation is auspicious in the months of Magha, Margashirsha, Vaishakha, Phalguna, Jyeshtha, and Shravana <note>These correspond generally to late winter, spring, and early summer</note>. It is favorable during the bright fortnight, on the days of auspicious planets, in a favorable Ascendant, and during the daytime. || 1 ||
It is best under the three "Hearing" stars <note>Shravana, Dhanishtha, Shatabhisha</note>, the Soft, Swift, and Fixed stars, and Svati, especially while wearing white garments. It is middling under Mula, Punarvasu, Magha, Vishakha, and Krittika; in other stars, it is inauspicious. || 2 ||

There are 48 <term>Samskaras</term> <note>Sacred life-cycle rituals or purifications</note>, among which the observation of the first menstruation <note>Rajodarshan</note> is primary for the purpose of conception. This first menses is auspicious in the months of Magha, Vaishakha, Ashvina, Phalguna, Jyeshtha, and Shravana; during the waxing moon; on the weekdays of benefic planets; under a favorable zodiacal sign; during the day; and under the constellations of Shravana, Dhanishtha, Shatataraka, the Soft stars, the Swift stars, the Fixed stars, and Svati. It is considered mediocre under Mula, Punarvasu, Magha, Vishakha, and Krittika, and inauspicious under other constellations. Furthermore, wearing white clothing at that time is considered auspicious. || 1 || 2 ||

(Verse) The first menstruation is not auspicious during <term>Bhadra</term> <note>An inauspicious astrological timing</note>, while sleeping, on the day the sun enters a new sign, on the New Moon, on "Empty" lunar days, at twilight, on the 6th or 12th lunar days, or during the <term>Vaidhriti</term> yoga. || 3 ||
It is also not favorable during illness, on the 8th lunar day, during solar or lunar eclipses, or during the <term>Vyatipata</term> yoga. || 3 ||

The first menstruation is not auspicious and yields undesirable results if it occurs during Bhadra, while the girl is asleep, on the day of a solar transit, on the New Moon, on "Empty" dates <note>4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days</note>, at twilight, on the 6th or 12th lunar days, during the Vaidhriti yoga, during a fever or other illness, on the 8th lunar day, during an eclipse of the Sun or Moon, or during the Vyatipata yoga. || 3 ||

# Page 58
## The Jewel of Auspicious Timing (Muhurta-Chintamani)

-> (Vasantatilaka Meter) <-
> With the stars Hasta, Svati, Ashvini, Mrigashirsha, Anuradha, Dhanishta, and the fixed stars <note>Dhruva stars: Rohini, Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, along with Jyeshtha, on an auspicious lunar day and an auspicious weekday, a woman who has completed her menstrual cycle should bathe. By bathing under Mrigashirsha, Revati, Swati, Hasta, Ashvini, or Rohini, she quickly attains conception. || 4 ||

Under the constellations of Hasta, Svati, Mrigashirsha, Anuradha, Dhanishta, the fixed stars, and Jyeshtha—on an auspicious lunar day (avoiding the forbidden *Bhadra* days) and on the weekdays of auspicious planets—a woman should perform her first post-menstrual bath. By bathing specifically under Mrigashirsha, Revati, Swati, Hasta, Ashvini, or Rohini, she will soon conceive a child. || 4 ||

-> (Shardulavikridita Meter) <-
> One should avoid the three types of *Gandanta* <note>Inauspicious transition points between zodiac signs</note>, the 8th and 1st stars from the birth star, the ends of Mula, Bharani, Ashvini, Revati, and Magha; days of eclipses, *Vyatipata*, and *Vaidhriti* <note>Specific inauspicious astrological yogas</note>; the anniversary of parents' death (*Shraddha*), daytime, the first half of *Parigha* yoga, celestial or earthly portents, the 8th house from the birth sign, and stars occupied by malefic planets when approaching one's wife. || 5 ||

The auspicious timing for conception (*Garbhadhana* <term>Garbhadhana: The first of the sixteen traditional sacraments, performed to ensure a healthy child</term>) is now described: One must avoid the junctions of constellations, dates, or ascendants (*Gandanta*); the birth star; the specific stars Mula, Bharani, Ashvini, Revati, and Magha; days of solar or lunar eclipses; the astrological periods of *Vyatipata* and *Vaidhriti*; the days of the parents' *Shraddha* <note>Rituals for deceased ancestors</note>; daytime; the first half of the *Parigha* period; and any days marked by natural disasters or omens. Furthermore, the 8th house from the birth sign or birth ascendant, and any ascendant or star occupied by malefic planets, must be avoided when a husband approaches his wife for the first time after her cycle. || 5 ||

-> (Shalini Meter) <-
> Avoid *Bhadra*, the 6th lunar day, the *Parva* days <note>Full/New moon and transitions</note>, the *Rikta* days <note>4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days</note>, twilight, Tuesdays, Sundays, Saturdays, and the first four nights of the menstrual cycle. For conception, use the three *Uttara* stars, Mrigashirsha, Hasta, Anuradha, Rohini, Swati, Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha. || 6 ||

Avoid the *Bhadra* period, the 6th lunar day, the *Parva* days, the "empty" *Rikta* days, twilight hours, Tuesdays, Sundays, and Saturdays. Excluding the first four nights from the onset of menstruation, one should perform the conception rite under the three *Uttara* stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Mrigashirsha, Hasta, Anuradha, Rohini, Swati, Shravana, Dhanishta, or Shatabhisha. || 6 ||

-> (Indravajra Meter) <-
> With benefics in the angles or trines, and malefics in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses, in an ascendant aspected by male planets; with the Moon in an odd-numbered sign division and on an even-numbered night. Under Chitra, Punarvasu, Pushya, and Ashvini, the results are mediocre. || 7 ||

When auspicious planets are in the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses (angles) or the 5th and 9th houses (trines), and malefic planets are in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses; when the rising sign (*Lagna*) is aspected by male planets (Sun, Mars, Jupiter); and when the Moon is in an odd-numbered zodiac division—conception should be initiated on an even-numbered night. If female planets are strong and the Moon is in a female division on an odd night, a girl is born. If male planets are strong on an even night, a son is born.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of domestic rituals (Samskaras) related to pregnancy, specifically focusing on the timing and astrological requirements for the hair-parting ceremony and the rite for ensuring a male child.</meta>

<page-num>59</page-num>
<header>With Vernacular Commentary - Chapter 5</header>

...occurs. In mixed planetary combinations, a person of neutral gender is born. The constellations <term>Chitra, Punarvasu, Pushya, and Ashwini</term> are considered mediocre for <term>Garbhadhana</term> <note>the rite of conception</note>; if the previously mentioned auspicious constellations are unavailable, one may use these. || 7 ||

->(In the *Shardulavikridita* meter)<-
**On the days of Jupiter, Mars, and the Sun; under the constellations of Mrigashira, Pushya, Mula, Shravana, Punarvasu, and Hasta; avoiding the "Empty" lunar days, the New Moon, and the 6th, 8th, and 12th days; in the 6th or 8th month when the Lord of the Month is strong; with benefics in the angles or trines and malefics in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses; in a masculine sign or division—the hair-parting ceremony should be performed.** || 8 ||

Once pregnancy is confirmed, the auspicious timing for <term>Simantonnayana</term> <note>the "parting of the hair" ritual performed for the mother's mental well-being and the child's health</note> is described: The ritual should be performed on a Thursday, Tuesday, or Sunday under the constellations of Hasta, Mrigashira, Pushya, Mula, Shravana, or Punarvasu. One must avoid the <term>Rikta</term> <note>literally "empty" or "hollow" lunar days: the 4th, 9th, and 14th</note> days, the New Moon, and the 12th, 6th, and 8th lunar days. It should take place in the 6th or 8th month when the <term>Masesha</term> <note>the planetary ruler of that specific month of pregnancy</note> is powerful. Furthermore, auspicious planets should be in the <term>Kendras</term> <note>1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note> or <term>Trikonas</term> <note>5th or 9th houses</note>, while inauspicious planets should be in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses. The rising sign (<term>Lagna</term>) should have a masculine division on an auspicious day. Alternatively, the ritual may be performed under the "Fixed" constellations or Revati. || 8 ||

->(In the *Vasantatilaka* meter)<-
**The Lords of the Months are Venus, Mars, Jupiter, Sun, Moon, Saturn, Mercury, the Lord of the Ascendant, the Moon, and the Sun respectively. For women, the strength of the Moon is desired for marriage and pregnancy rituals; in other deeds, the husband's strength alone is considered.** || 9 ||

Regarding the duration of pregnancy: the ruler of the 1st month is Venus, 2nd is Mars, 3rd is Jupiter, 4th is the Sun, 5th is the Moon, 6th is Saturn, 7th is Mercury, 8th is the Lord of the Ascendant, 9th is the Moon, and 10th is the Sun. If these rulers are strong, the fetus is well-nourished; if weak, it may lead to wasting or complications in that specific month. In marriage and pregnancy rituals like <term>Garbhadhana</term>, the <term>Chandrashuddhi</term> <note>lunar purity/strength</note> of the woman must be checked separately. In all other rituals, only the husband's lunar strength is considered for a married woman, not the woman's separately. || 9 ||

->(In the *Indravajra* meter)<-
**The Pumsavana rite should be performed according to the previously mentioned timings in the third month, followed by the worship of Vishnu. In the eighth month, worship Vishnu, Brahma, and Jupiter in an auspicious Lagna, provided the eighth house is pure.** || 10 ||

Using the lunar days, weekdays, and constellations mentioned for the hair-parting ceremony, one should perform the <term>Pumsavana</term> <note>a ritual to favor the birth of a male child</note> in the third or fourth month. This should be done on "masculine" weekdays, under masculine rising signs and masculine-named constellations. Additionally, worship Lord Vishnu in the third month, and in the eighth month, perform worship for Vishnu, Brahma, and Jupiter. In all these pregnancy rituals, an auspicious rising sign is required, and the eighth house of the elective chart must be "pure" <note>free from malefic planets</note>. || 10 ||

<page-num>60</page-num>
<header>The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments (Muhurta Chintamani)</header>

### (Upajati Meter) The birth ceremonies <note>Jatakarma: a rite performed at birth involving honey and ghee</note> for the infant should be performed on an auspicious day, avoiding the "Void" lunar days <note>Rikta Tithis: the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight</note> and "Parva" days <note>New/Full moon and transitions</note>.
### It should occur on the eleventh or twelfth day, under the "Soft," "Fixed," "Swift," or "Mobile" constellations. || 11 ||

As soon as a son is born, the birth ceremony should be performed before the umbilical cord is cut. If that time passes for any reason, it should be done along with the naming ceremony. Therefore, a single auspicious time is given for the birth ceremonies and others: avoiding the "Void" lunar days and "Parva" days, it should be done on an auspicious weekday on the eleventh or twelfth day under the Soft, Fixed, or Swift constellations <term>Nakshatras: star clusters used in Vedic astrology</term>. For a Brahmin, this is done in 11 days; for a Kshatriya, in 13; for a Vaishya, in 16; and for a Shudra, at the end of a month. If the primary time has passed, one must consider the Sun's northern course <note>Uttarayana</note>; however, within the primary time, such detailed considerations are not required. || 11 ||

### (Vasantatilaka Meter) The ritual bath of the mother after childbirth is auspicious under Revati, the Fixed constellations, Mrigashira, Hasta, Swati, and Ashwini, provided these do not fall on a Tuesday. ||
### One should avoid the three stars starting from Ardra, Shravana, Magha, Bharani, the "Mixed" constellations, Mula, Chitra, and the days of Mercury or Saturn, as well as the 8th, 6th, 12th, 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days. || 12 ||

The woman who has recently given birth should take her ritual bath under Revati, the Fixed constellations, Mrigashira, Hasta, Swati, or Ashwini. The three stars starting from Ardra <note>Ardra, Punarvasu, Pushya</note>, Shravana, Magha, Bharani, the "Mixed" constellations <note>Vishakha and Krittika</note>, Mula, and Chitra should be avoided. Similarly, Wednesdays, Saturdays, and the 8th, 6th, 12th, 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days should not be chosen for the mother's bath. || 12 ||

### (Shardulavikridita Meter) If a child is born with teeth in the first month, he himself may perish.
### In subsequent months, teeth appearing will respectively destroy the younger brother, the sister, the mother, or the elder brother.
### From the sixth month onwards, he attains incomparable enjoyment, happiness from the father, health, wealth, and comfort. If born with teeth or if the upper teeth appear first, it destroys the father and others. || 13 ||

If a child grows teeth in the first month, he himself may pass away. In the 2nd month, it harms the younger brother; in the 3rd, the sister; in the 4th, the mother; and in the 5th, the elder brother. In the 6th month, it brings great enjoyment; in the 7th, happiness from the father; in the 8th, physical strength; in the 9th, wealth; in the 10th, contentment; and in the 11th, happiness. If the child is born with teeth already present, or if the teeth in the upper jaw appear before the lower ones, it brings destruction to the father and family. || 13 ||

### (Anushtubh Meter) For placing the child in the cradle, count from the Sun's constellation in groups of five, five, five, five, and seven.
### These indicate health, death, wasting, disease, and happiness respectively for the child. || 14 ||

To place the child in the (Dola) cradle, there is a "Cradle Chart" <note>Dola-Chakra</note>: counting from the constellation where the Sun is positioned, the first 5 constellations indicate health; the next 5 indicate death; the next 5 indicate wasting/thinness; the next 5 indicate disease; and the final 7 indicate happiness. || 14 ||

# **Chapter 5 - With Commentary**

->**Chapter 5 - With Commentary**<-

(Vasantatilaka Meter) On the 32nd, 12th, 16th, 18th, or 10th day <note>The original Sanskrit uses numerical codes: Danta (32), Arka (12), Bhupa (16), Dhriti (18), and Dik (10)</note>, on an auspicious day of the week and under soft, light, or fixed constellations, the child’s first placement in a cradle should occur. The first outing from the house <term>Nishkramana: the ritual of taking a child out of the house for the first time</term> should take place in the fourth month, either at the time prescribed by the scriptures or on the 12th day. || 15 ||

The auspicious time for placing the child in the cradle <term>Dolarohana: the cradle ceremony</term> is as follows: on the 32nd, 12th, 16th, 18th, or 10th day, on an auspicious weekday, and under soft, light, or fixed constellations <term>Nakshatras: lunar mansions used in Hindu astrology</term>. The first outing should be done in the fourth month on a date, day, and constellation prescribed by the scriptures. || 15 ||

(Bhujangaprayata Meter) Avoiding the setting of Venus or Jupiter, the months of Chaitra and Pausha, and the intercalary month <term>Adhik Masa: an extra month added to the lunar calendar to keep it aligned with the solar year</term>, one should perform the worship of water <term>Jal-Pujan: a ritual where the mother worships a water source, usually a well, after the first month of childbirth</term> when the first month after delivery is complete. This should be done on Wednesday, Monday, or Thursday, avoiding the "empty" dates, under the constellations of Shravana, Pushya, Punarvasu, Mrigashira, Hasta, Mula, or Anuradha. || 16 ||

Excluding the periods when Venus or Jupiter are combust (invisible), the months of Chaitra <note>approx. March-April</note> and Pausha <note>approx. December-January</note>, the "empty" dates <term>Rikta Tithis: the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight, considered unlucky for new ventures</term>, and the intercalary month; once one month has passed since childbirth, the mother should perform the water worship on a Wednesday, Monday, or Thursday under the constellations of Shravana, Pushya, Punarvasu, Mrigashira, Hasta, Mula, or Anuradha. || 16 ||

(Sragdhara Meter) Avoiding the "empty" dates, the "joyful" dates, the 8th, the new moon, the 12th day, and the days of Saturn, Mars, and the Sun; also avoiding an ascendant that is the 8th house or eighth division from the birth sign, and the signs of Pisces, Aries, and Scorpio; the first feeding of solid food <term>Annaprashana: the 'first rice' ceremony</term> is auspicious in even months (6th, 8th, 10th, 12th) for boys and odd months (5th, 7th, 9th, 11th) for girls, under fixed, soft, light, or movable constellations. || 17 ||

Following the first outing, the first feeding of grain should be performed for a son in even months such as the 6th, 8th, 10th, or 12th, and for a daughter in odd months such as the 5th, 7th, 9th, or 11th. For this, one must avoid the "empty" dates (4, 9, 14), the "joyful" dates (1, 6, 11), the 8th, the 30th (New Moon), and the 12th lunar day. One must also avoid Saturday, Tuesday, and Sunday. The ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> should not be the 8th from the birth sign or birth ascendant, nor should it be the ninth-part division <term>Navamsha: a 1/9th subdivision of a zodiac sign</term> of those. The signs of Pisces (12), Aries (1), and Scorpio (8) should be avoided as the ascendant, while fixed, soft, light, and movable constellations should be chosen. || 17 ||

(Vasantatilaka Meter) With auspicious planets in the center or trine houses and the third house, and the tenth house being empty; with malefic planets in the 3rd, 11th, or 6th houses; and the Moon being in any place other than the 1st, 8th, or 6th—such an ascendant is praised. Some say the constellations of Anuradha, Shatabhisha, Swati, or the birth constellation are unfavorable. || 18 ||

Regarding the purity of the ascendant for the first feeding: it is said that auspicious planets should be in the centers (1, 4, 7, 10), trines (5, 9), or the 3rd house. Malefic planets should be in the 3rd, 11th, or 6th houses. The 10th house must be "pure" (empty of planets). The Moon should be in a house other than the 1st, 8th, or 6th. In such an ascendant...

# The Jewel of Auspicious Moments

<page-num>62</page-num>
<header>Muhurta Chintamani</header>

The first feeding of solids <note>Sanskrit: Annaprashana</note> is auspicious [under the previously mentioned stars], though some authorities consider the constellations of Anuradha, Shatabhisha, Swati, and the individual's birth star to be inauspicious for this event. || 18 ||

->(Verse) The results of the waning Moon, full Moon, Jupiter, Mercury, Mars, the Sun, Saturn, and Venus,<-
->when positioned in the Trines (5th, 9th), the 12th house, the Quadrants (1st, 4th, 7th, 10th), or the 8th house, are as follows: || 19 ||<-
->One who eats the food of alms, a performer of sacrifices, long-lived, wise, suffering from bile,<-
->afflicted by skin diseases, struggling for food, suffering from wind-disorders, or a partaker of enjoyments. || 20 ||<-

The specific planetary effects during the first feeding are: if a waning Moon is in the 9th, 5th, 12th, 1st, 4th, 7th, 10th, or 8th house, the child will eat the food of alms <note>original: "bhikshashi"</note>. With a full Moon, they will perform sacrifices; with Jupiter, they will be long-lived; with Mercury, wise; with Mars, prone to bilious diseases; with the Sun, they will suffer from blood-related or skin diseases <note>original: "kushthi"</note>; with Saturn, they will face "food-distress" (difficulty digesting or obtaining food) and "wind-disorders" <note>referring to Vata imbalances in Ayurvedic medicine</note>; and with Venus, the child will be a "bhogi"—one who enjoys worldly pleasures. || 19 || 20 ||

->(Verse) Having worshipped the Earth and the Boar incarnation, when Mars is auspiciously placed,<-
->avoiding the "Empty" lunar days, in the fifth month, the child<-
->should have a waist-thread tied. Then, under the Fixed, Soft, Senior, Friendly, or Light constellations,<-
->the child should be seated upon the ground. || 21 ||<-

In the fifth month, or at the time of the first feeding, the <term>Bhumyupaveshana</term> <note>The ritual of the child's first sitting on the ground</note> ceremony is performed. After worshipping the Earth and Lord Varaha <note>The Boar avatar of Vishnu who lifted the Earth from the depths</note>, and ensuring the planet Mars is in a favorable position, one should avoid the "Empty" <note>Rikta</note> lunar days (4th, 9th, 14th). Under a stable rising sign and the Fixed, Friendly, Deer-head, Senior, or Light constellations, a waist-thread <note>original: "kati-sutra," commonly known as "kandani" or "tagadi"</note> should be tied on the child, and they should be seated on the earth. || 21 ||

->(Verse) At that time, one should place before the child cloth, weapons, books, and pens,<-
->gold and silver; whatever the child grasps first,<-
->by those means shall their livelihood and profession be predicted. || 22 ||<-

During the ceremony of sitting on the ground, there is a test of future livelihood. One should place cloth, weapons, books, pens, gold, silver, and other tools of various professions before the child. Whichever object the child grasps first, their lifelong work and livelihood will be related to that object, and they shall gain prestige through that profession. || 22 ||

->(Verse) On days other than Tuesday and Saturday, under Fixed, Soft, or Light stars, or those dedicated to Vishnu, Mula, Aditi, Indra,<-
->Swati, or Vasu; in the rising signs of Gemini, Virgo, Aquarius, Taurus, or Pisces; with gentle planets...<-

# Chapter 5: Commentary in Common Language

### Page 63

With the auspicious planets located in the <term>Kendras</term> (the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses) or <term>Trikonas</term> (the 5th and 9th houses), and the malefic planets situated in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses, the ceremony of offering <term>Tambula</term> <note>betel leaf and nut, often given as a digestive or ceremonial mark of honor</note> should be performed at the age of two and a half months, or during the <term>Annaprashana</term> <note>the first feeding of solid food/grain</note> ceremony. || 23 ||

On days other than Tuesday or Saturday, under the constellations of Shravana, Mula, Punarvasu, Jyeshtha, Swati, Dhanishtha, or the "steady" and "soft" constellations; and during the ascendants of Gemini, Capricorn, Virgo, Aquarius, Taurus, or Pisces: when the auspicious planets are in the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses (the centers) or the 9th and 5th houses (the trines), and the malefic planets are in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses—at such a time, the child should be fed betel leaf and nut. This ritual is prescribed at two and a half months of age or on the day of the first grain-feeding. || 23 ||

*(In the Sragdhara meter)* Excluding the months of Chaitra and Pausha, the lunar "decay" days, the period of Vishnu’s sleep <note>the four months of the monsoon when certain ceremonies are paused</note>, the birth month, and the <term>Rikta</term> <note>"empty" or "hollow" lunar days: the 4th, 9th, and 14th</note> dates; avoiding even-numbered years and the birth star; rather, in the 6th, 7th, or 8th month, or on the 12th or 16th day from birth; on the days of Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, or the Moon; in an odd-numbered year, under the constellations of Shravana, Dhanishtha, Punarvasu, or the "soft" and "light" stars—the <term>Karnavedha</term> <note>ear-piercing ceremony</note> is considered auspicious. || 24 ||

**The Auspicious Time for Ear-Piercing:** According to the solar calendar, one should avoid the months of Chaitra and Pausha, the "decay" lunar dates, the birth month (specifically the first 30 days from birth), and the "empty" dates (4th, 9th, 14th). Avoid even years (2nd, 4th, 6th, 8th, 10th, 12th) and the birth constellation (specifically the 1st, 10th, and 19th stars). Excluding these, the 6th, 7th, or 8th month is preferred. Alternatively, the 12th or 16th day from birth is acceptable. Beyond that, in an odd-numbered year, on Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, or Monday, under the constellations of Shravana, Dhanishtha, Punarvasu, or the "soft" and "light" stars, the ear-piercing is beneficial. || 24 ||

*(In the Praharshini meter)* When the eighth house is purified (empty of planets), with auspicious planets in the trines, centers, or the 3rd and 11th houses, and during the ascendants of Venus or Jupiter—or if Jupiter himself is in the ascendant—while malefic planets are in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses, it brings good fortune. || 25 ||

**Purification of the Ascendant for Ear-Piercing:** The 8th house should be free of planets. Auspicious planets should be in the 9th, 5th, 1st, 4th, 7th, 10th, 3rd, or 11th houses. The ceremony is best when the ascendant (Zodiac sign on the horizon) is one ruled by Jupiter or Venus (Taurus, Libra, Sagittarius, or Pisces), or when Jupiter is physically present in the ascendant. Furthermore, the "Birth Festival" rites are performed when the solar year is complete—specifically "on the day the Sun returns to the exact sign and degree of birth." Those from the South also observe the lunar birth date for this. || 25 ||

*(In the Sragdhara meter)* The consecration of divine idols or water reservoirs, marriages, the lighting of the sacred fire, entering a new home, the first haircut (<term>Choula</term>), royal consecrations, and the taking of vows—these are not auspicious during the <term>Dakshinayana</term> <note>the southern transit of the sun, roughly June to December</note>. Nor should they occur during the "infancy," "old age," or "combustion" (setting) of Jupiter and Venus, nor during the appearance of a comet... || 26 ||

The consecration of temples and ponds, weddings, establishing the sacred fire, housewarmings, the tonsure ceremony, coronations, and religious initiations should not be done during the southern transit of the sun, nor when Jupiter or Venus are in a state of astrological weakness (infancy/old age) or are hidden by the sun's light (combustion)...

# Muhurta Chintamani
<page-num>64</page-num>

One should not perform ceremonies during the appearance of a comet <note>original: "pucchalatara" or "ketu"</note>. When the comet sets (disappears), one should still avoid auspicious acts for another 15 or 7 days. Some authorities maintain that as long as comets named Ugri, Dvishikha, Trishikha, Tamas, or Kilaka are visible, the defect <note>dosha</note> persists, but not thereafter. || 26 ||

-> (Verse) The infancy of Venus lasts three days after its eastern rise and ten days after its western rise. Its old age lasts a fortnight before its eastern setting and two days before its western setting. For Jupiter, both infancy and old age are said to last a fortnight. || 27 || <-

-> Some say these periods for both planets are ten days; others say seven. In cases of emergency, others say three days, and for the Moon, old age is three days and infancy is half of that. || 28 || <-

The infancy <note>balatva - a period where the planet is too "young" to give results</note> of Venus lasts 3 days when rising in the east and 10 days when rising in the west. Similarly, its old age <note>varddhaka - a period of weakness before setting</note> is 15 days before setting in the east and 5 days before setting in the west. Jupiter remains in infancy for 15 days and in old age for 15 days. || 27 || According to some opinions, the infancy and old age of both Jupiter and Venus are 10 days each; others say 7 days. Another opinion holds that in an emergency <note>atyayika - if the auspicious time is passing and the work would otherwise be ruined</note>, one should leave only three days. For the Moon, one should avoid 3 days for old age and half of that (1.5 days) for infancy. || 28 ||

-> (Verse) The tonsure ceremony should be performed from the third year onwards in odd years. It is auspicious during the Sun's northern course, excluding the month of Chaitra, on the days of Mercury, Moon, Venus, and Jupiter, avoiding the Rikta dates, the first, sixth, and full/new moon days. It should be done in soft, movable, or light constellations, excluding Anuradha but including Jyeshtha, provided the eighth house is pure and malefic planets are in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses. || 29 || <-

-> (Verse) If the waning Moon, Mars, Saturn, or the Sun are in the ascendant, they cause death, injury by weapons, lameness, and fever respectively. If Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus are in the quadrants, it is auspicious. || 30 || <-

If the Tonsure ceremony <note>Chudakarma - the first ritual hair-cutting for a child</note> is performed separately from the Thread ceremony <note>Vratabandha</note>, the auspicious timing is as follows: in the 3rd, 5th, or 7th (odd) years; avoiding the "Empty" dates <note>Rikta: 4th, 9th, 14th</note>, the 1st, 6th, and the New/Full Moon days <note>Parva</note>; during the Sun's northern transit <note>Uttarayana</note> excluding the month of Chaitra; on Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, or Monday. The ascendant <note>Lagna</note> should not be the 8th from the birth sign, and no planet other than Venus should be in the 8th house. Excluding the birth month, it should be done in "Soft," "Movable," or "Light" constellations <note>Nakshatras</note> (including Jyeshtha but excluding Anuradha). Malefic planets should be in the 11th, 6th, or 3rd houses, and benefic planets should be in the quadrants or trines. || 29 || If the waning Moon is in the ascendant or quadrants (1, 4, 7, 10), it causes death; Mars causes injury by weapons; Saturn causes lameness...

<summary>This page details the "infancy" and "old age" periods of planets like Venus and Jupiter during which auspicious activities are prohibited, and provides specific astrological requirements for the Chudakarma (tonsure) ceremony.</summary>

( 65 )
### Chapter 5: Including the Vernacular Commentary

From the Sun comes fever, while Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus grant auspicious results; however, the purification of the *Tara* <note>The 'Star' or lunar mansion calculation relative to one's birth star</note> is essential in this. One must not choose the *Janma* (birth), *Vipat* (misfortune), *Pratyari* (enemy), or *Vadh* (death) stars. This consideration is equally applicable to Vedic tonsure (*Chaul*), non-Vedic tonsure, and shaving for personal comfort (*Kshaur*) || 30 ||

->(Verse in Anustubh Meter) If a child's mother is more than five months pregnant, the tonsure ceremony should not be performed. However, if the child is over five years of age, it is considered acceptable even if the mother is pregnant. || 31 ||<-

If the mother of the child undergoing the tonsure is more than five months pregnant, the *Chudakarma* <note>The first hair-cutting ceremony</note> should not be performed for a child under five years old. If the child is older than five years, there is no fault in proceeding even if the mother is more than five months pregnant || 31 ||

->(Verse in Shardulavikridita Meter) If the Moon is in a trine (5th or 9th house) or in its sign of exaltation, or in the auspicious divisions of Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus, and is in a favorable position via transit, then shaving and travel may be undertaken even if the *Tara* (star) is otherwise unfavorable. || 32 ||<-

If the Moon is in the 5th or 9th house, or in its exalted sign, or falls within the *Shadvarga* <note>The six divisional charts used in astrology to determine planetary strength</note> of Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus, and is in a favorable position according to current transits, then shaving (*Kshaur*) and travel-related activities may be performed in an auspicious *Nakshatra* (lunar mansion) even during a "corrupt" or unfavorable *Tara* period || 32 ||

->(Verse in Anustubh Meter) One should not perform the tonsure and other rites if the mother is menstruating or in the postnatal impurity period. The tonsure or marriage of the eldest child should not be done in the month of Jyeshtha; some say not even in Margashirsha. || 33 ||<-

If the child's mother is menstruating or has recently given birth (the *Sutika* period), one should not perform ceremonies like tonsure (*Chaul*), the thread ceremony (*Vratabandha*), or marriage. Furthermore, the tonsure, thread ceremony, or marriage of the first-born child (son or daughter) should not be performed in the month of *Jyeshtha* <note>May–June</note>. Some authorities state these should not be done in the month of *Margashirsha* <note>November–December</note> either || 33 ||

->(Verse in Shardulavikridita Meter) The cleaning of teeth, shaving, and cutting of nails are prescribed on the days and stars mentioned for tonsure. Avoiding Saturdays, Tuesdays, and Sundays, as well as the ninth day from a previous shave, the twilight hours, the *Rikta* dates, festival days, and nights. One should not shave without a seat, when prepared for battle or travel, after bathing, after applying ointments, or after eating, if they desire their own welfare. || 34 ||<-

General shaving, cleaning of teeth, and cutting of hair or nails should also be done during the lunar mansions and days prescribed for the tonsure ceremony. However, those seeking their own well-being should not shave on Saturdays, Tuesdays, or Sundays; nor on the ninth day following a previous shave; nor during twilight, the *Rikta* <note>“Empty” lunar days: 4th, 9th, and 14th</note> dates, festival days, or at night. Furthermore, one should not shave without sitting on a proper seat, when preparing for war or leaving for another village, or after having already bathed, applied oils/pastes, eaten a meal, or put on fine jewelry and clothes || 34 ||
