# Muhurta Chintamani
<page-num>66</page-num>

->( Manjubhashini Meter )<-

> In sacrifices, marriages, the Godana <note>the first ritual shaving of a youth's hair</note> ceremony, upon the death of parents, or when being released from imprisonment, or by the command of a priest or a King, one may perform shaving even on days otherwise prohibited. However, the husband of a pregnant woman must never carry a corpse, go on a pilgrimage, bathe in the sea, or shave his hair. || 35 ||

---

->( Bhujangaprayata Meter )<-

> For the benefit of Kings, the trimming of the beard and hair should be done every fifth day or during the rising of the auspicious Shravana constellations. If one performs shaving in the six cycles of Krittika, three of Anuradha, eight of Rohini, five of Magha, or four of Uttara Phalguni (or according to other opinions, four cycles of all the 'Uttara' constellations) within a single year, they meet with death. || 36 ||

<note>The text explains that for royal grooming, one should follow the specific degrees of the rising sign (Lagna). For example, in Aries, the first 13°20' belong to Ashwini. Actions prescribed for Ashwini should be completed while those degrees are rising.</note>

---

->( Panchachamara Meter )<-

> Having worshipped Ganesha, Vishnu, Saraswati <note>Goddess of Knowledge</note>, and Lakshmi, in the child's fifth year, on the 11th, 12th, 10th, 2nd, 6th, 5th, or 3rd lunar days, during the Sun's northern course <term>Uttarayana</term>, under the 'light' constellations, Shravana, Swati, Revati, Punarvasu, Ardra, Chitra, or Anuradha, on Monday, Wednesday, Thursday, or Friday, and in an auspicious Ascendant <term>Lagna</term> excluding the movable signs (1, 4, 7, 10), the child should begin the study of the alphabet. || 37 ||

---

->( Panchachamara Meter )<-

> Under Mrigashira, Ardra, Punarvasu, Hasta, Chitra, Swati, Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, Ashwini, Mula, the three Purvas <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, Purva Bhadrapada</note>, Pushya, and Ashlesha constellations; on Sunday, Thursday, Wednesday, or Friday; and on the 6th, 5th, or 3rd lunar days... || 38 ||

<meta>This page continues the discussion of auspicious timings (Muhurta) for life milestones, specifically transitioning from the commencement of education to the sacred thread ceremony (Upanayana).</meta>

# ( 67 )
## Chapter 5: Including the Vernacular Commentary

The commencement of education should begin on the 11th, 12th, 10th, or 2nd lunar days <note>Tithis</note>, and at a moment when benefic planets are situated in the "centers" (1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses) or "trines" (5th or 9th houses). Some authorities also recommend the constellations <note>Nakshatras</note> of Dhruva, Revati, or Anuradha. One must also avoid "un-study" days <note>Anadhyaya; days traditionally restricted for Vedic recitation</note> when starting education. || 38 ||

-> (In the *Shardulavikridita* meter) <-
> The binding of the vow <note>Vratabandha or Upanayana; the sacred thread ceremony</note> for Brahmins is prescribed in the 8th year from conception or birth; for Kshatriyas in the 11th; and for Vaishyas in the 12th. Alternatively, for those seeking spiritual luster, it may be the 5th for Brahmins, 6th for Kshatriyas, and 8th for Vaishyas. Wise men say that until double these ages have passed, the time is considered "secondary" <note>Gauna; acceptable but not ideal</note>. || 39 ||

There are two types of primary times for the sacred thread ceremony: the "regular" <note>Nitya</note> and the "desire-based" <note>Kamya; performed for a specific result like spiritual brilliance</note>. Measured by the solar year from either conception or birth, the regular primary time for a Brahmin is the 8th year, for a Kshatriya the 11th, and for a Vaishya the 12th. The desire-based primary time is the 5th year for a Brahmin, 6th for a Kshatriya, and 8th for a Vaishya. The "secondary" time lasts until double the regular age: up to 16 for Brahmins, 22 for Kshatriyas, and 24 for Vaishyas. Beyond this, the time is considered "lapsed" <note>Atikala</note>. || 39 ||

-> (In the *Vasantatilaka* meter) <-
> The vow is auspicious during the Swift, Fixed, Mobile, and Soft constellations, as well as Ashlesha, Mula, the three Purvas, and Ardra; on the days of the Sun, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and the Moon; on the 2nd, 3rd, 5th, 11th, 12th, and 10th lunar days; and during the first three parts of the dark fortnight, but never in the afternoon. || 40 ||

The ceremony is auspicious in the constellations mentioned above and on the specified weekdays and lunar days. It should be performed during the first half of the dark lunar fortnight, but not in the afternoon. Regarding months, the six months of the Sun's northern course <note>Uttarayana</note> are prescribed; among these, the month of Chaitra <note>March-April</note> holds great spiritual significance. || 40 ||

-> (In the *Pramanika* meter) <-
> Venus, Jupiter, the Moon, and the Lord of the Ascendant are poor in the 8th house; the Moon and Venus are likewise in the 12th; and malefic planets are poor in the 5th or 8th houses during the ceremony. || 41 ||

Regarding the purity of the Ascendant <note>Lagnashuddhi</note> for the ceremony: Venus, Jupiter, the Moon, and the planet ruling the Ascendant are considered "base" or unfavorable if placed in the 6th or 8th houses. The Moon and Venus give similar poor results in the 12th house. Furthermore, malefic planets in the 1st, 5th, or 8th houses are also unfavorable. || 41 ||

-> (In the *Anushtup* meter) <-
> In the sacred thread ceremony, benefic planets are auspicious except in the 8th, 6th, and 12th houses. Malefic planets are auspicious in the 3rd, 6th, and 11th. A full Moon in Taurus or Cancer is auspicious in the Ascendant. || 42 ||

In the context of this ceremony, benefic planets are unfavorable in the 8th, 6th, and 12th houses but auspicious elsewhere. Malefic planets are auspicious in the 3rd, 6th, and 11th houses. If the Moon is full and positioned in the signs of Taurus or Cancer, it is auspicious even if located in the 1st house <note>the Ascendant</note>. || 42 ||

It specifies auspicious lunar days, constellations, and planetary positions while defining the primary and secondary age limits for these rites.</summary>

# Muhurta Chintamani
<page-num>68</page-num>

(Shardulavikridita Meter) <term>Venus and Jupiter are the lords of the Brahmins; Mars and the Sun are the lords of the Kshatriyas; the Moon is the lord of the Vaishyas; Mercury is the lord of the Shudras; and Saturn is the lord of the outcastes. The lords of the Vedas (Shakheshas) are Jupiter, Venus, Mars, and Mercury respectively. 43</term>

> Venus and Jupiter rule the Brahmins <note>the priestly class</note>; Mars and the Sun rule the Kshatriyas <note>the warrior/ruling class</note>; the Moon rules the Vaishyas <note>the merchant class</note>; Mercury rules the Shudras <note>the laborer class</note>; and Saturn rules the Chandalas <note>those outside the formal class system</note>. Furthermore, Jupiter is the lord of the Rigveda, Venus of the Yajurveda, Mars of the Samaveda, and Mercury of the Atharvaveda. 43

---

(Vasantatilaka Meter) <term>The day and the strength of the ascendant of the Veda-lord are highly auspicious. In the sacred thread ceremony, the strength of the Veda-lord, the Sun, the Moon, and Jupiter is essential. If Jupiter or Venus are in an enemy’s sign, defeated in planetary combat, or in their sign of debilitation, the initiate will be devoid of the knowledge of the Vedas, Shastras, and the performance of daily and occasional rituals. 44</term>

> In the <term>Upanayana</term> <note>sacred thread ceremony</note>, the weekday associated with the lord of one's specific Veda (Shakhesha) and the strength of the rising sign (Lagna) are considered excellent. The transit strength of the Veda-lord, the Sun, the Moon, and Jupiter is primary. If these are well-placed, the result is auspicious; if poorly placed, it is inauspicious. Specifically, if Jupiter or Venus are in an enemy's house, in their sign of debilitation, or "defeated" in <term>Graha Yuddha</term> <note>planetary war/collision</note>, the boy being initiated will fail to follow the injunctions of the Vedas, the Shastras, and the <term>Shrauta-Smarta</term> <note>rituals derived from the Vedas and later tradition</note> duties. By extension, being in the debilitated portion of a sign (Navamsha) yields the same negative result. 44

---

(Anushtup Meter) <term>In the sacred thread ceremony, if performed during the birth-star or birth-ascendant, the initiate becomes highly learned. This applies to the first-born of Brahmins, but for Kshatriyas and others, it applies to those who are not the first-born. 45</term>

> The potential astrological flaws of performing the ceremony during the birth-constellation, birth-month, or birth-ascendant do not apply to the first-born child of a Brahmin, nor to the second or subsequent children of Brahmins. For Kshatriyas and Vaishyas, these flaws only apply to the first-born child; for their subsequent children, there is no such restriction. 45

---

(Anushtup Meter) <term>For the boy’s initiation or the girl’s marriage, Jupiter is best in the 5th, 9th, 11th, 2nd, or 7th house from the birth-moon. In the 10th, 6th, 3rd, or 1st, he is acceptable after worship. In the 4th, 8th, or 12th, he is condemned. 46</term>

> For a boy's sacred thread ceremony or a girl's wedding, the transit of Jupiter is most auspicious when he is in the 5th, 9th, 11th, 2nd, or 7th house from the birth-sign. If he is in the 10th, 6th, 3rd, or 1st house, one must perform a <term>Shanti</term> <note>propitiatory ritual</note> to mitigate the effects. Jupiter in the 4th, 8th, or 12th house is considered very unfavorable. 46

---

(Anushtup Meter) <term>If Jupiter is in his sign of exaltation, his own sign, a friend’s sign, or in his own or best division (Vargottama), he is auspicious even in the 12th, 8th, or 4th house. Conversely, if in a debilitated or enemy sign, even a "good" position becomes bad. 47</term>

> If Jupiter is in his sign of exaltation (Cancer), his own signs (Sagittarius/Pisces), a friendly sign (Aries/Scorpio), or in a <term>Vargottama</term> <note>the same sign in both the main chart and the 9th divisional chart</note> position, he remains auspicious even if transiting the 4th, 8th, or 12th houses. However, if he is in a debilitated or enemy sign, even a normally positive transit position will yield poor results. 47

It also provides specific transit rules for Jupiter's position relative to the birth moon for both initiation and marriage.</summary>

# The Jewel of Auspicious Moments

If Venus, Jupiter, or the Moon are conjoined with the Sun, the initiate will be <gloss>devoid of merit</gloss> lacking in good qualities. If conjoined with Mars, he will be <gloss>of cruel actions</gloss> violent; and if conjoined with Saturn, he will be clever. ॥ 52 ॥

-> ( <term>Pramanika</term> <note>A poetic meter consisting of eight syllables per line</note> ) If the Moon is in the signs of Venus (Taurus or Libra), Venus is in a trine (5th or 9th house), and Jupiter is in the ascendant, the initiate will be a knower of all the Vedas. If Jupiter is in the ascendant but the Moon is in a portion (Amsha) ruled by Saturn, he will be exceedingly shameless. ॥ 53 ॥ <-

-> ( <term>Jaghanachapala</term> <note>A specific variation of the Arya poetic meter</note> ) The 10th of the bright half of Ashadha, the 2nd of the bright half of Jyeshtha, the 11th of the bright half of Pausha, and the 12th of the bright half of Magha—these dates are considered "Sopapada" and are unfit for study. Furthermore, the 14th, the full moon, the 1st of the lunar fortnight, the new moon, the 8th of the dark half, and the day the Sun enters a new zodiac sign (Sankranti), as well as the anniversaries of the eras (Yugadi) and the Manu-cycles (Manvadi), are forbidden for the <term>Vratabandha</term> <note>The "binding of the vow," another name for the sacred thread ceremony</note> ceremony. ॥ 54 ॥ <-

Forbidden days for study (<term>Anadhyaya</term>) are of two types: regular and occasional. The dates mentioned above, including the transition of the Sun, are excluded from the thread ceremony due to their status as non-study days. ॥ 54 ॥

-> ( <term>Anushtubh</term> <note>The standard eight-syllable epic meter</note> ) A <term>Pradosha</term> <note>A period of time generally considered inauspicious for certain rites</note> occurs if the 13th lunar day begins before midnight on the 12th; if the 7th begins before one-and-a-half watches (approx. 4.5 hours) of the night on the 6th; or if the 4th begins before one watch (approx. 3 hours) of the night on the 3rd. In such cases, the day is undesirable for the thread ceremony. ॥ 55 ॥ <-

-> ( <term>Arya</term> <note>A common Sanskrit metrical structure based on syllabic instants</note> ) If, on the day of the ceremony, portents (such as thunder, earthquakes, meteors, or celestial fires) or forbidden study conditions arise before the <term>Brahmodhana</term> <note>A ritual cooking of rice for the Brahmins</note> is completed, then the ceremony should only proceed after performing the prescribed peace-making rituals (<term>Shanti</term>). ॥ 56 ॥ <-

For the followers of the Rigveda (Bahvrichas), the Brahmodhana ritual is central. For others, the period extends until the feeding of Brahmins associated with the initiation or the start of Vedic study. The peace-making rituals (<term>Shanti</term>) include the recitation of auspicious hymns (Svastivachana), offerings of milk-rice (Payas-homa), chanting of the Gayatri and Brihaspati mantras, the donation of a cow, and feeding Brahmins. ॥ 56 ॥

-> ( <term>Vasantatilaka</term> <note>A lyrical meter of 14 syllables, literally "The Ornament of Spring"</note> ) According to the Vedas, the following lunar mansions (Nakshatras) are auspicious: Shravana, Dhanishtha, Shatabhisha, Hasta, Chitra, Swati, Mula, Purvashadha, Purvabhadrapada, Revati, Ashwini, Anuradha, Mrigashira, Punarvasu, Pushya, and the three "Ushas" (Uttara Phalguni, Uttarashadha, Uttarabhadrapada). Specifically, for the different Vedas: Shravana, Mrigashira, Revati, and the "short" mansions are excellent... ॥ 57 ॥

It also outlines the necessary purification rituals (Shanti) if bad omens occur during the proceedings.</summary>

<page-num>71</page-num>
<header>With Language Commentary—Chapter 5</header>

According to the order of the Vedas, the following lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras; the 27 divisions of the sky used in Vedic astrology</term> are prescribed for the <term>Upanayana; the sacred thread initiation ceremony</term>: Mrigashira, Ardra, Ashlesha, Hasta, Chitra, Svati, Mula, and the three Purvas for followers of the Rigveda; Revati, Hasta, Anuradha, Mrigashira, Punarvasu, Pushya, Rohini, and the three Uttaras for followers of the Yajurveda; Ashwini, Dhanishta, Pushya, Hasta, the three Uttaras, Ardra, and Shravana for followers of the Samaveda; and Mrigashira, Pushya, Ashwini, Hasta, Anuradha, Punarvasu, and Dhanishta for followers of the Atharvaveda. || 57 ||

->Lunar Mansions according to Vedic Affiliation<-

| Rigveda | Yajurveda | Samaveda | Atharvaveda |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Mrigashira | Revati | Ashwini | Mrigashira |
| Ardra | Hasta | Dhanishta | Revati |
| Ashlesha | Anuradha | Pushya | Hasta |
| Hasta | Mrigashira | Hasta | Ashwini |
| Chitra | Punarvasu | 3 Uttaras | Pushya |
| Svati | Pushya | Ardra | Anuradha |
| Mula | 3 Uttaras | Shravana | Dhanishta |
| 3 Purvas | Rohini | 0 | Punarvasu |

(Translation of Verse) > If the mother of the candidate becomes menstruating after the ancestral invocation <note>original: "Nandishraddha"</note>, the ceremony should not be held in that specific astrological moment unless a purification is performed. ||
> The tonsure, initiation, or marriage should then be performed after a peace-offering ritual <note>original: "Shanti"</note>; otherwise, it is not auspicious. || 58 ||

If the mother of the person for whom the ritual is being performed begins her menstrual cycle after the Nandishraddha <note>a joyful preliminary rite to honor ancestors</note>, then the tonsure <insert>Chuda</insert>, initiation <insert>Vratabandha</insert>, or marriage should be moved to a different astrological timing. If another timing is not available, one must perform a peace-offering ritual <note>Shanti</note> and proceed with the fixed time. This ritual involves worshipping the goddess Lakshmi in a golden idol, reciting the Shri Sukta hymn, performing a fire sacrifice <note>Homa</note> with sweetened rice <note>Payas</note> for every verse, and a ritual bath <note>Abhisheka</note>. || 58 ||

(Translation of Verse) > The binding of the knife for kings is auspicious before marriage, excluding the month of Chaitra, and when Mars is neither setting nor in its day. || 59 ||

For the warrior class <note>Kshatriyas</note>, the "binding of the knife" <note>Churika-bandhan; a ceremony where a youth is invested with a weapon</note> is performed after the initiation ceremony but before marriage. This occurs in the months prescribed for initiation, excluding the month of Chaitra <note>March-April</note>. However, there is a specific restriction: the planet Mars must not be "combust" <note>hidden by the sun's glare</note> and it should not be a Tuesday. This is the auspicious timing for the binding of the sword. || 59 ||

<page-num>72</page-num>
<header>The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments</header>

> (Translation) The *Keshanta* <note>The first shaving of the beard</note> ceremony should be performed in the sixteenth year on a day deemed auspicious for the *Choula* <note>tonsure/hair-cutting</note> rite. The *Samavartana* <note>graduation/return from the teacher's house</note> is desired two days after the days prescribed for the vows. || 60 ||

->Thus ends the fifth chapter on Sacraments in the "Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments" composed by Rama, the son of the astrologer Ananta.<-

For a Brahmin, the *Keshanta* rite should be performed in the 16th year, and for Kshatriyas and Vaishyas in the 22nd year, using the auspicious moments prescribed for the *Chudakarma* <note>the first hair-cutting ceremony</note>. In the 13th year the *Mahanamni* vow is taken, in the 14th the *Mahavrata*, in the 15th the *Upanishad-vrata*, and in the 16th the *Keshanta* and *Godana* <note>gift of a cow</note> vow-sacraments occur. For all of these, the timing is the same as the *Choula* rite. After studying the Vedas and sciences and completing the *Godana* sacraments, the *Samavartana* <note>graduation</note> ceremony should be performed at the auspicious time mentioned for the thread-ceremony and others. || 60 ||

->Thus ends the chapter on Sacraments in the vernacular commentary on the "Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments" created by Mahidhara.<-

### Now, the Chapter on Marriage.

After the *Samavartana* <note>graduation</note>, it is said that one should marry for the purpose of obtaining a son who will uplift his lineage. This is of 8 types:
1. When the groom is invited and the girl is given, decorated with ornaments according to one's ability, without causing any loss to him, it is **Brahma marriage**. His son uplifts 21 generations, past and future.
2. When the girl is given as a gift during a sacrifice, it is **Daiva**. This offspring purifies 14 generations of the past and 6 of the future.
3. When the girl is given at the request of the groom for the sake of assisting in religious duties, it is **Prajapatya**. His son purifies 6 generations before and 6 after.
4. When the girl is given after taking 1 cow and 1 bull, or 2 cows, from the groom for the sake of the sacrifice or for the girl herself—but not with the intent of a (price) sale—it is named **Arsha**. This is equal to the *Daiva* type.
5. When the marriage occurs after giving wealth to the girl's father and others, or by satisfying the girl with wealth, it is **Asura**.
6. When a marriage occurs according to the mutual desire of the bride and groom after initial love and embraces, it is **Gandharva**.
7. When a girl is taken by force or by winning in battle, it is **Rakshasa** marriage.
8. When one violates a girl by force while she is sleeping or unconscious due to the night etc., it is the lowest **Paishacha** marriage.

Among these, the *Prajapatya*, *Brahma*, *Daiva*, and *Arsha* marriages give auspicious results at the stated times. The children born from these are pure in divine and ancestral rites, and possess qualities such as being righteous, wise, and believers in the divine. The *Arsha* marriage is also similar by alternative view. The *Asura*, *Gandharva*, *Rakshasa*, and *Paishacha* are inferior; their children are unrighteous, hypocritical, slanderous, and atheistic. (Taking a girl in battle) *Rakshasa* and the *Swayamvara* <note>self-choice of a husband</note> form of *Gandharva* are the duties of Kings (Kshatriyas) and not others. Marriage that occurs by giving money (*Asura*) is extremely contemptible; this—

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the astrological and spiritual foundations of marriage, transitioning from the classification of unions to the specific purpose and timing of the wedding ceremony.</meta>

<page-num>73</page-num>
<header>With Commentary: Chapter 6</header>

The <term>Dharmashastra</term><note>Ancient Indian legal and ethical texts</note> does not consider a wife taken for pleasure alone to be a "lawful wife" capable of assisting in rites for the gods or ancestors; she is counted among the servants. Her children are also not considered ritually pure. For the first four types of marriage <note>The "righteous" forms: Brahma, Daiva, Arsha, and Prajapatya</note>, there are no strict time constraints; one may marry whenever they wish. The saying of the <term>Grihyakara</term><note>Authors of the domestic ritual texts</note> that "marriage is for all times" refers specifically to the Gandharva <note>love marriage</note> and other similar forms.

### The Purpose of Marriage

->( In the Vasantatilaka meter )<-
> A wife endowed with good character is the means to achieving the <term>Trivarga</term><note>The three goals of human life: Dharma (duty), Artha (prosperity), and Kama (pleasure)</note>. Her good character depends upon the <term>Lagna</term><note>The rising sign or ascendant at the time of the wedding</note>. Therefore, the time of marriage must be carefully considered, for the children, character, and religious duties of the couple are all dependent upon it. || 1 ||

A wife who is favorable to her husband and others, and who possesses a virtuous disposition, is the proper vessel for achieving the three goals of life: Virtue, Wealth, and Pleasure. Her character is governed by the rising sign, and that sign is determined by the specific moment of the wedding. Marriage for women and the <term>Upanayana</term><note>The sacred thread ceremony or initiation</note> for men are considered a second birth. Therefore, the nature of the children, the temperament, and the religious merit of the couple will mirror the quality of the astrological sign at those moments. A householder is burdened by three debts—to the gods, to the ancestors, and to humanity. These are redeemed through virtuous offspring. Since such offspring depend upon a wife with auspicious qualities, the auspicious moments (<term>Muhurta</term>) for marriage are now described. || 1 ||

->( In the Sragdhara meter )<-
> First, honor the astrologer with gems and other gifts while he is in a calm state of mind. Then, inquire about the girl's marriage. If the Moon is in the 10th, 11th, 3rd, 7th, or 5th house from the <term>Prashna Lagna</term><note>The rising sign at the moment the question is asked</note> and is aspected by Jupiter, it brings an immediate marriage. If Taurus, Libra, or Cancer is the rising sign of the inquiry, and it is occupied or aspected by benefic planets, know that the marriage will occur soon. || 2 ||

The word "Atha" (Now/Then) used here in the middle of the text serves as an auspicious invocation. To ask a question, one should first honor a composed astrologer with gold, clothes, fruit, and other gifts, and then ask about the girl's marriage. Regarding the "Inquiry Yoga" <note>astrological combination</note>: if the Moon is in the 10th, 11th, 3rd, 7th, or 5th house from the moment of asking and is seen by Jupiter, the wedding will happen quickly. Likewise, if Taurus, Libra, or Cancer is the rising sign and is influenced by auspicious planets, the marriage is imminent. || 2 ||

->( In the Drutavilambita meter )<-
> If the Moon and Venus are in odd signs and odd <term>Navamshas</term><note>A ninth-part division of a zodiac sign used for precision</note>, are strong, and aspect the rising sign, they bring a husband to the girl. If these two are in even signs and even divisions, they grant a bride to the youth. || 3 ||

In the chart of inquiry, if the Moon and Venus are in odd signs and odd ninth-divisions, are powerful, and look upon the rising sign, the girl will find a groom. If those same planets (Moon and Venus) are in even signs and even divisions, the man will find a bride. Both of these marriage combinations serve the same essential purpose. || 3 ||

<summary>This page explains the spiritual necessity of choosing an astrologically auspicious time for marriage to ensure virtuous offspring and the fulfillment of life's duties. It provides specific planetary configurations (Yogas) that indicate a speedy or successful union during a consultation with an astrologer.</summary>

# The Jewel of Auspicious Moments
<note>original: "Muhurta Chintamani"</note>

### (Shalini Meter)
### If the Moon is in the sixth or eighth house from the Ascendant of the query, or if a malefic planet is in the seventh, or Mars is in the seventh;
### Or if the Moon is in the first house and Mars is in the seventh, she will become a widow in eight years. || 4 ||

If the Moon is in the sixth or eighth house from the Ascendant of the query <note>Prashna Lagna: the zodiac sign rising at the moment the question is asked</note>, she will become a widow within eight years and may also pass away herself. (1) Likewise, if a malefic planet is in the Ascendant and Mars is in the seventh house, the same result follows. (2) And if the Moon is in the Ascendant and Mars is in the seventh house, the result is also the same. (3) These are the configurations for widowhood <term>Vaidhavyayoga: astrological combinations indicating the loss of a husband</term>. || 4 ||

### (Dodhaka Meter)
### If a malefic planet is in the fifth house from the Ascendant of the query, aspected by an enemy planet, and in its sign of debilitation, then the girl will surely be unchaste or her children will die. || 5 ||

If a malefic planet in the fifth house of the query's Ascendant is seen by an enemy planet and is in its lowest <note>debilitated</note> sign, she will be a woman of loose character or one whose children die at birth <term>Mritavatsa: a mother whose children do not survive</term>. || 5 ||

### (Pushpitagra Meter)
### If the Moon is in an even-numbered sign in the dark fortnight, and is in the sixth or eighth house from the Ascendant, aspected by inauspicious planets, this becomes the destroyer of the marriage. || 6 ||

If the Moon of the dark fortnight <note>the waning phase</note> is in an even sign (2nd, 4th, etc.) and placed in the 6th or 8th house from the query's Ascendant while being aspected by malefics, the marriage will be destroyed—that is, the marriage will not take place. || 6 ||

### (Shardulavikridita Meter)
### Having observed the yogas for childhood widowhood in the birth chart, the daughter should perform the Savitri vow or the Peepal tree vow in secret;
### After performing a formal marriage with an image of Vishnu, a Peepal tree, or a water-pot at an auspicious moment, she should be given to a long-lived groom; then the fault of being a "twice-married" woman does not occur. || 7 ||

If the combinations for childhood widowhood mentioned in the horoscopes are seen in a girl's chart, her father or guardians should privately and with certainty have her perform the Savitri vow <note>a ritual dedicated to Savitri, who reclaimed her husband from the god of death</note>. They should also perform the vow related to the Peepal tree <note>Ficus religiosa</note>. Alternatively, at an auspicious time and under favorable planetary combinations, a formal marriage ceremony should be conducted with an image of Lord Vishnu, a Peepal tree, or a clay pot. After this, the girl should be given in marriage to a long-lived groom (one whose chart shows a long life). By performing this remedy, the fault of widowhood is removed, and the social stigma of being a <term>Punarbhu: a woman married for a second time</term> does not apply. || 7 ||

### (Sragvini Meter)
### At the moment of the query, whatever kind of child the woman who approaches the astrologer is carrying or accompanied by;
### Whether it be a girl or a boy, the wise should predict that the subject of the query will have that same kind of offspring. || 8 ||

At the time of the query, the astrologer should observe the woman who approaches him and give the answer accordingly. For example, if a woman comes carrying a son, the girl about whom the marriage query is made will have sons. If she comes carrying a girl...

<summary>This page details astrological configurations (Yogas) that predict widowhood or misfortune in marriage and provides ritual remedies, such as symbolic marriage to Vishnu or a tree, to neutralize these "faults." It also introduces the concept of omens based on the appearance of people who approach the astrologer.</summary>
<keywords>widowhood, astrology, marriage remedies, Vishnu, Peepal tree, Savitri vow, omens, lunar phases, planetary aspects</keywords>

# Chapter 6: Including the Commentary

If [the signs] appear, there will be daughters; if both appear, there will be both sons and daughters. By extension, whatever qualities the mother possesses—whether she is fortunate, unfortunate, blessed with children, or barren—the same should be predicted for the daughter. ॥ 8 ॥

(**Sragvini Meter**) If the sounds of a <term>conch shell</term> <note>original: "Shankha"</note>, a kettle-drum, or a <term>lute</term> <note>original: "Vipanchi"</note> are heard, it brings auspiciousness; if the opposite occurs, one should take note of it.
->If a crow, a donkey, a dog, or even a jackal makes a sound at the moment the astrological question is asked.<- ॥ 9 ॥

At the time of inquiry, if auspicious omens such as the sound or sight of a <term>conch</term> <note>Shankha</note>, (kettle-drum), trumpet, or <term>lute</term> <note>Veena</note> occur, it will be auspicious. Similarly, seeing elephants, horses, ceremonial umbrellas, or any objects that please the mind is considered a good omen. If a (crow) <note>Vayasa</note>, donkey, dog, or jackal makes a sound at that time, it should be known as inauspicious; owls and buffaloes are also considered the same. ॥ 9 ॥

(**Matta Meter**) In the constellations of Uttarashadha, Swati, Shravana, the three Purvas, Anuradha, Dhanishtha, or Krittika, or in other constellations suitable for marriage;
Having first satisfied them with fruits and flowers along with clothing and ornaments, the proper selection of the bride should take place. ॥ 10 ॥

<term>Kanyavarana Muhurta</term> <note>The auspicious time for choosing or betrothing a bride</note>—One should perform the <term>Kanyavarana</term> (engagement) according to the rites in the constellations of Uttarashadha, <term>Swati</term>, <term>Shravana</term>, the three <term>Purvas</term> <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, <term>Anuradha</term>, <term>Dhanishtha</term>, and <term>Krittika</term>, as well as other constellations prescribed for marriage, using fruits, flowers, clothing, and jewelry. ॥ 10 ॥

(**Matta Meter**) Either a priest or the bride's own brother, on an auspicious day accompanied by songs and music;
Should perform the selection of the groom with clothing and the sacred thread in the fixed constellations and the three Purvas starting with Krittika. ॥ 11 ॥

A (Brahmin) priest or the bride's biological brother should perform the <term>Vagdana</term> <note>the formal promise of marriage or "giving of the word"</note> on an auspicious day of the week. This should be done during the <term>fixed constellations</term> <note>Dhruva Nakshatras</note>, as well as <term>Krittika</term> and the three Purvas, accompanied by auspicious songs and music, presenting the groom with clothing, ornaments, and the <term>sacred thread</term> <note>Yajnopavita</note>. ॥ 11 ॥

(**V. Ma. Meter**) Marriage is auspicious for girls based on the purity of Jupiter in even years after the age of six;
For grooms, it depends on the purity of the Sun, and for both, it depends on the purity of the Moon. ॥ 12 ॥

Marriage is auspicious when there is <term>Jupiter Purity</term> <note>Guru Shuddhi: a favorable transit of Jupiter</note> for the girl, <term>Sun Purity</term> <note>Surya Shuddhi</note> for the groom, and <term>Moon Purity</term> <note>Chandra Shuddhi</note> for both. For the girl, it should be in an even-numbered year after the age of six; for the groom, it should be in odd-numbered years. Here, another teacher's opinion is that marriage is auspicious from three months to nine months after an odd year of birth, and up to three months into an even year. ॥ 12 ॥

(**Drutavilambita Meter**) When the Sun is in Gemini, Aquarius, Taurus, Scorpio, Capricorn, or Aries;
Even in Gemini, only the first ten days of the bright fortnight are pure.
In Scorpio, Capricorn, and Aries, marriage also occurs in the months of Kartika, Pausha, and Chaitra. ॥ 13 ॥

Marriage is auspicious when the Sun is in the zodiac signs of <term>Gemini</term>, <term>Aquarius</term>, <term>Taurus</term>, <term>Scorpio</term>, <term>Capricorn</term>, and <term>Aries</term>. Among these, in the month of Ashadha (when the Sun is in Gemini), only the <term>first ten days</term> <note>Trilava: specifically the 1st through 10th of the bright lunar fortnight</note> are auspicious. It is not suitable after the <term>Harishayani</term> <note>the 11th day of the bright half of Ashadha, when Lord Vishnu is said to begin his four-month sleep</note> Ekadashi. Additionally, the months of <term>Kartika</term> (Sun in Scorpio), <term>Pausha</term> (Sun in Capricorn), and <term>Chaitra</term> (Sun in Aries) are also accepted for marriage. ॥ 13 ॥

<summary>This page details the auspicious omens, specific constellations (Nakshatras), and planetary alignments required for engagement and marriage ceremonies. It provides specific rules for the timing of the "giving of the word" (Vagdana) and the selection of the bride and groom based on their ages and the Sun's position in the zodiac.</summary>

# Muhurta Chintamani
## (The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Timing)

<page-num>( 76 )</page-num>
->**The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Timing**<-

( **Rathoddhata Meter** ) The marriage of a first-born son or daughter is not appropriate during their birth month, on their birth star (<term>Nakshatra</term>), or on their birth date (<term>Tithi</term>). However, the wise praise these times for those who are not first-born, as it is said to grant them sons. || 14 ||

The marriage of a first-born (<term>Adyagarbha</term> <note>literally "first-womb"</note>) son or daughter is not proper during the birth month (the 30 days from the birth date), the birth star, or the birth date. For those born second or later, these birth-related timings in marriage are considered to be "givers of sons" (auspicious). || 14 ||

( **Shalini Meter** ) A "Double Jyeshtha" is declared as mediocre, but a "Triple Jyeshtha" is never suitable at any time. Some say this applies until the Sun enters the constellation of Krittika; others say a marriage should never occur between two first-borns. || 15 ||

A "Double Jyeshtha" (a first-born son in the month of Jyeshtha, or a first-born daughter in the month of Jyeshtha) is considered mediocre. A "Triple Jyeshtha" (a first-born son marrying a first-born daughter in the month of Jyeshtha) is never acceptable for marriage. Some authorities claim the defect of Double or Triple Jyeshtha does not apply once the Sun enters the constellation of Krittika <note>The third lunar mansion, associated with fire</note>. Furthermore, a marriage should not take place between a first-born daughter and a first-born son. || 15 ||

( **Harini Meter** ) Within six months of a son’s wedding, one should not perform a daughter’s wedding; likewise, within the same family, after a <term>Mandana</term> (joyous ceremony like a wedding), one should not perform a <term>Mundana</term> (tonsure/shaving ceremony). Two brothers born of the same womb should not marry two sisters born of the same womb. Within one year of a sibling's marriage, one should not perform ancestral rites. || 16 ||

Within six months of a son's marriage, a daughter's marriage should not be performed. Similarly, after a <term>Mandana</term> (wedding), a <term>Mundana</term> (the <term>Chaul</term> tonsure ceremony, the <term>Upanayana</term> initiation, or the four great Vedic vows) should not be performed for six months. However, if the Hindu New Year (<term>Samvatsara</term>) changes in between, the rule relaxes. For example, if a wedding occurred in the month of Phalguna, a tonsure or another wedding could occur in Vaishakha. This rule applies to the "own clan" (<term>Nija-kula</term>), defined here as relatives within three generations of shared ancestry (<term>Sapindya</term>). Furthermore, for six months following a joyful event, one should not perform <term>Pitri-kriya</term> (ancestral shraddha rites). Two biological brothers should not be given two biological sisters in marriage. Also, the marriages of two biological siblings should not occur within six months of each other. A son's wedding can occur four days after a daughter's wedding, but for children born of the same womb (son-daughter, son-son, or daughter-daughter), a gap of six months is required. || 16 ||

( **Upajati Meter** ) If, after the marriage date has been fixed, someone within three generations of the bride or groom’s family passes away, it causes destruction. The marriage is desired only after one month has passed and a peace ritual (<term>Shanti</term>) is performed, or, according to others, after the period of ritual impurity (<term>Sutaka</term>) has ended. || 17 ||

If, after the wedding invitation/date has been formally decided (<term>Dinpata</term>), a relative within three generations (<term>Tripurusha</term> <term>Sapindya</term>) dies, the marriage should be postponed for one month, followed by a purification ritual. Some teachers say it can proceed immediately after the period of ritual impurity (<term>Ashaucha</term>) ends. However, this "one month" rule applies to three generations of relatives, not to parents. For example, the impurity for a father's death lasts one year, and similarly for a mother...

<summary>This page outlines astrological and genealogical restrictions for Hindu marriage, specifically prohibiting "Triple Jyeshtha" (three first-born factors) and regulating the timing between multiple family ceremonies or deaths.</summary>
<keywords>Muhurta, Jyeshtha, first-born, Sapindya, ritual impurity, Ashaucha, marriage timing, tonsure ceremony, Mundana</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological timing for domestic rituals and the specific influence of birth stars (Nakshatras) on family relationships during marriage.</meta>

<page-num>77</page-num>
<header>With Commentary—Chapter 6.</header>

...6 months, for a woman 3 months, and for nephews and similar relatives 1 month is the duration; this is the reason. There is a further distinction: in times of famine, loss of kingdom, when the father's life is in danger, or in the marriage of an "over-age" <note>original: "praudha" - a girl who has passed the ideal age for marriage</note> daughter, no such obstacles are considered unfavorable. || 17 ||

->(Upajati Meter)<-
**The tonsure ceremony and the initiation rite should not be performed within six months of a marriage among the three generations of male lineage. Similarly, after the entry of a new bride, the departure of a daughter (for her own marriage) is auspicious only after six months or after the change of the calendar year.** || 18 ||

Within three generations of male lineage, for six months following a marriage or until the year changes, one should not perform the tonsure ceremony <term>Chuda-karma: the first ritual hair-cutting of a child</term>, the thread ceremony <term>Vratabandha: Upanayana or sacred thread initiation</term>, and by the word "also," the four great vows <note>original: "mahanamnyadi" - specific Vedic student vows</note>. Furthermore, from the time a new bride enters the home, for that same duration, a daughter should not be given away (sent out) from the house. The "three generations" <note>original: "tri-purushi"</note> are counted from the root ancestor up to three generations; the fourth generation is free from this restriction. || 18 ||

->(Vasantatilaka Meter)<-
**A daughter or son born under Ashlesha causes the destruction of the mother-in-law; those born under Mula destroy the father-in-law. A daughter born in Jyeshtha destroys her husband's elder brother, and one born in Vishakha becomes the destroyer of the husband's younger brother.** || 19 ||

A daughter or son born in the Ashlesha star directly causes the destruction of the mother-in-law, but not a step-mother-in-law. Those born in Mula cause the destruction of the father-in-law. A daughter born in Jyeshtha destroys her husband's biological elder brother <note>original: "jyeshtha"</note>, and similarly, one born in Vishakha destroys the husband's biological younger brother <note>original: "devar"</note>. There are other texts that state: a man born in Jyeshtha destroys the bride's elder brother, and one born in Vishakha destroys the younger brother-in-law <note>original: "shala"</note>. "A man born in the Jyeshtha star kills the wife's elder sister or elder brother; likewise, one born in the star of the two deities (Vishakha) kills the wife's sister or younger brother-in-law." In some places, sisters are mentioned instead of elder or younger brothers. The meaning is that this applies to the son or daughter regardless of whether they were the first-born or a later pregnancy. || 19 ||

->(Anushtup Meter)<-
**A girl born in the first three quarters of Vishakha gives happiness to the younger brother-in-law. The final quarter of Mula and the first quarter of Ashlesha are auspicious for the respective parents-in-law.** || 20 ||

There is a specific consideration regarding the aforementioned faults: a girl born in the first three quarters <term>padas: the four divisions of a Nakshatra</term> of Vishakha does not cause harm to the younger brother-in-law; on the contrary, she brings happiness. Only the fourth quarter is forbidden. Similarly, the fourth quarter of Mula is auspicious for the father-in-law, and the first quarter of Ashlesha is auspicious for the mother-in-law, for both the groom and the bride. || 20 ||

->(Anushtup Meter)<-
**Varna (Class), Vashya (Control), Tara (Star), Yoni (Nature), Graha-maitra (Planetary Friendship), Gana-maitra (Temperament), Bhakuta (Relative Zodiac Sign), and Nadi (Pulse/Energy)—these are the increasing qualities (to be matched).** || 21 ||

It introduces the eight-fold system of compatibility (Ashta-kuta) used in matrimonial matching.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for matrimonial compatibility (Melaka) based on the Muhurta Chintamani, a classic 16th-century Sanskrit text on electional astrology. The previous context likely introduced the necessity of marriage rites.</meta>

# Muhurta Chintamani

The consideration of marriage compatibility <note>Melaka</note> is described as follows: if there is friendship of Caste <note>Varna</note>, (1) point is awarded; in Dominion <note>Vashya</note> (2); in Star-type <note>Tara</note> (3); in Yoni <note>sexual compatibility/animal type</note> (4); in Planetary Friendship (5); in Clan <note>Gana</note> (6); in Sign-relative position <note>Bhakuta</note> (7); and in Pulse <note>Nadi</note> (8) points are awarded. The sum of all these results in a total of (36) points <note>Gunas</note>. A higher score indicates a favorable match, while a lower score is progressively inauspicious. The specific consideration of each of these is explained hereafter. || 21 ||

->** (Pramanika Meter) **<-
->The signs Pisces, Scorpio, and Cancer are the protectors of the twice-born <note>Brahmins</note>; then follow the Kings <note>Kshatriyas</note>, the Traders <note>Vaishyas</note>, and the Shudras. || 22 ||<-
->A bride of a higher caste than the groom is not praised by the wise. || 22 ||<-

Pisces, Scorpio, and Cancer are the Brahmin caste; [Aries, Leo, Sagittarius] are Kshatriya; [Taurus, Virgo, Capricorn] are Vaishya; and [Gemini, Libra, Aquarius] are the Shudra caste. A bride of a lower caste than the groom is auspicious; a groom of a lower caste than the bride's caste is not good. If both are of the same caste, it is excellent. When the groom is of a higher caste, (1) point is obtained; if the bride is higher, no point is given. || 22 ||

->** (Indravajra Meter) **<-
->Except for the Lion <note>Leo</note>, all signs are subject to the Human signs; likewise, the water-born signs are the "food" of humans. || 23 ||<-
->All are under the power of the Lion, except for the Scorpion <note>Scorpio</note>; other relationships of dominance should be known from human behavior. || 23 ||<-

Dominion <term>Vashya-kuta: a method to determine who will dominate the relationship</term>—The human signs [Gemini, Virgo, Libra, Sagittarius (first half), and Aquarius] have all signs under their control except Leo. Water-dwelling signs, being the "food" of humans, are also subject to them. Furthermore, all signs are under the power of Leo except Scorpio. Other mutual dominance or subjection should be understood from worldly behavior. Here too, if the bride's sign is subject to the groom's sign, (2) points are obtained; if the opposite is true, no points are given. || 23 ||

->** (Anushtup Meter) **<-
->Count from the bride's star to the groom's star, and from the groom's star to the bride's star. || 24 ||<-
->Divide by nine; if the remainder is three, five, or seven, it is considered inauspicious. || 24 ||<-

Counting from the bride's birth star <note>Nakshatra</note> to the groom's star, and from the groom's star to the bride's star, one should divide the total by 9 to determine the Star-type <note>Tara</note>. If the remainders are 3, 5, or 7, it is inauspicious; others are auspicious. From the auspicious ones, (3) points are obtained. || 24 ||

->** (Shardulavikridita Meter) **<-
->The Horse is declared for Ashwini and Shatabhisha; the Buffalo for Swati and Hasta; the Lion for Dhanishtha and Purva Bhadrapada; the Elephant for Bharani and Revati. The Ram<-
->for Krittika and Pushya; the Monkey for Shravana and Purva Ashadha; the Mongoose for Uttarashadha and Abhijit; the Serpent for Rohini and Mrigashira. || 25 || The Deer is<-
->declared for Jyeshtha and Anuradha; the Dog for Mula and Purva Ashadha; the Cat for Punarvasu and Ashlesha; the Cat also for Magha and Purva Phalguni...<-

<summary>This page details the first three of the eight criteria (Ashta-kuta) for Hindu marriage compatibility: Varna (caste), Vashya (dominion), and Tara (auspicious stars), while beginning the classification of Yoni (animal types).</summary>
<keywords>marriage compatibility, Guna Milap, Varna, Vashya, Tara, Nakshatra, Hindu astrology, Muhurta Chintamani</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological compatibility (Kuta) for marriage, specifically focusing on Yoni Kuta (physical/instinctive compatibility) and Graha Maitri (planetary friendship).</meta>

(79)
# Chapter 6: Including the Commentary in Common Language

**The tiger and the cow; the mongoose and the snake; the fire-sign and the elephant; the horse and the buffalo; the lion and the ram; the dog and the deer; the monkey and the sheep; the cat and the rat—these pairs possess natural, great enmity. One should avoid a match between these opposing animal types.** <note>original: "व्याघ्रोन्द्राग्निभ..." This verse in Sanskrit outlines the "Mahavaira" or great enmities between specific animal archetypes assigned to birth stars.</note> **|| 26 ||**

**Yoni Kuta <note>The "Yoni" classification determines physical and instinctive compatibility based on the birth star (Nakshatra).</note>:**
The Horse yoni belongs to Ashwini and Shatabhisha. Swati and Hasta are the Buffalo. Dhanishta and Purva Bhadrapada are the Lion. Bharani and Revati are the Elephant. Pushya and Krittika are the Sheep (Ram). Shravana and Purva Ashadha are the Monkey. Uttara Ashadha and Abhijit are the Mongoose. Rohini and Mrigashira are the Serpent. Jyeshtha and Anuradha are the Deer. Mula and Ardra are the Dog. Punarvasu and Ashlesha are the Cat. Magha and Purva Phalguni are the Rat. Vishakha and Chitra are the Tiger. Uttara Phalguni and Uttara Bhadrapada are the Cow yoni.

If the bride and groom share the same yoni, it is excellent. If the yonis are friendly, it is good; if neutral, it is average; and if there is mutual enmity, it is inauspicious. Their natural enmities are as follows:
*   Cow and Tiger
*   Elephant and Lion
*   Horse and Buffalo
*   Dog and Deer
*   Mongoose and Serpent
*   Monkey and Sheep
*   Cat and Rat

These should be understood as they are observed in the common behavior of the world. If there is "Yoni Friendship" (compatibility), 4 points <note>referred to as "Gunas" in the scoring system</note> are awarded. || 25 || 26 ||

-> ( <term>Shardulavikridita</term> <note>A specific Sanskrit poetic meter consisting of 19 syllables per line.</note> ) <-

**The Sun’s friends are Mars, Jupiter, and the Moon; Venus and Saturn are its enemies, while Mercury is neutral. For the Moon, Mercury and the Sun are friends; it has no enemies, and the rest are neutral. For Mars, the Moon, Jupiter, and the Sun are friends; Mercury is the enemy, while Venus and Saturn are neutral. For the son of the Moon (Mercury), Venus and the Sun are friends, the Moon is the enemy, and Jupiter, Saturn, and Mars are neutral. For the Teacher of the Gods (Jupiter), the Sun, Mars, and the Moon are friends; Mercury and Venus are enemies, and Saturn is neutral. For the Poet (Venus), Mercury and Saturn are friends; the Moon and Sun are enemies, while Mars and Jupiter are neutral. For Saturn, Venus and Mercury are friends; the Moon, Sun, and Mars are enemies. || 27 || 28 ||**

**Graha Kuta <note>Planetary Friendship: This measures the psychological and social compatibility based on the relationship between the ruling planets of the couple's Moon signs.</note>:**
*   **Sun:** <term>Mars</term>, <term>Jupiter</term>, <term>Moon</term> are friends; <term>Venus</term>, <term>Saturn</term> are enemies; <term>Mercury</term> is neutral.
*   **Moon:** <term>Mercury</term>, <term>Sun</term> are friends; others are neutral; there are no enemies.
*   **Mars:** <term>Moon</term>, <term>Jupiter</term>, <term>Sun</term> are friends; Mercury is the enemy; <term>Venus</term>, <term>Saturn</term> are neutral.
*   **Mercury:** <term>Venus</term>, <term>Sun</term> are friends; <term>Moon</term> is the enemy; <term>Jupiter</term>, <term>Saturn</term>, <term>Mars</term> are neutral.
*   **Jupiter:** <term>Sun</term>, <term>Mars</term>, <term>Moon</term> are friends; <term>Mercury</term>, <term>Venus</term> are enemies; <term>Saturn</term> is neutral.
*   **Venus:** <term>Mercury</term>, <term>Saturn</term> are friends; <term>Moon</term>, <term>Sun</term> are enemies; <term>Jupiter</term>, <term>Mars</term> are neutral.
*   **Saturn:** <term>Venus</term>, <term>Mercury</term> are friends; <term>Moon</term>, <term>Sun</term>, <term>Mars</term> are enemies.

If the lords of the signs <note>Rashi-ish</note> of the bride and groom are friends or the same planet, 5 points are awarded. Friend/Neutral earns 4 points; Neutral/Neutral earns 3; Friend/Enemy earns 2; Neutral/Enemy earns 1; and Enemy/Enemy earns 0 points. A match between two enemies is never recommended, as it results in <term>Mrityu-Shashtashtaka</term> <note>A "Death-Six-Eight" configuration, considered a very dangerous astrological placement for a couple.</note>. || 27 || 28 ||

<summary>This page details the Yoni Kuta and Graha Kuta systems of Vedic astrology, explaining how animal archetypes and planetary friendships determine the compatibility score (Gunas) for marriage.</summary>
<keywords>Yoni Kuta, Graha Maitri, planetary friendship, Nakshatra, marriage compatibility, Vedic astrology, Guna scoring, Shashtashtaka</keywords>

<page-num>80</page-num>
<header>The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments</header>

## Table of Friends, Enemies, and Neutrals

| Planet | Sun | Moon | Mars | Mercury | Jupiter | Venus | Saturn |
|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|
| **Friend** | Moon, Mars, Jupiter | Sun, Mercury | Jupiter, Moon, Sun | Sun, Venus | Sun, Moon, Mars | Mercury, Saturn | Mercury, Venus |
| **Neutral** | Mercury | Mars, Jupiter, Venus, Saturn | Venus, Saturn | Mars, Jupiter, Saturn | Saturn | Mars, Jupiter | Jupiter |
| **Enemy** | Venus, Saturn | None | Mercury | Moon | Mercury, Venus | Sun, Moon | Sun, Moon, Mars |

( <term>Vasantatilaka</term> <note>A poetic meter consisting of 14 syllables per line.</note> ) The groups of Demons, Humans, and Gods are determined respectively by the constellations: Magha, Ashlesha, Dhanishta, Jyeshtha, Mula, Shatabhisha, Krittika, Chitra, Vishakha; the three Purvas, the three Uttaras, Rohini, Bharani, Ardra; and Anuradha, Punarvasu, Mrigashira, Shravana, Revati, Swati, Ashwini, Pushya, and Hasta. ॥ 29 ॥

Magha, Ashlesha, Dhanishta, Jyeshtha, Mula, Shatabhisha, Krittika, Chitra, and Vishakha belong to the **Demon group** (<term>Rakshasa-gana</term>). The three Purvas (Purva Phalguni, Purvashadha, Purvabhadrapada), the three Uttaras (Uttara Phalguni, Uttarashadha, Uttarabhadrapada), Rohini, Bharani, and Ardra belong to the **Human group** (<term>Manushya-gana</term>). And Anuradha, Punarvasu, Mrigashira, Shravana, Revati, Swati, Ashwini, Pushya, and Hasta belong to the **Divine group** (<term>Deva-gana</term>). ॥ 29 ॥

( <term>Malini</term> <note>A poetic meter consisting of 15 syllables per line.</note> ) Within one's own group, love is said to be excellent; between Divine and Human, it is declared middling. Between Demon and Human, death is predicted; between Demon and Divine, there will be absolute enmity in this matter. ॥ 30 ॥

If the groom and bride belong to the same group, there is great affection. Between Divine and Human, the affection is average. Between Demon and Human, it leads to death. Between Divine and Demon, there is conflict. Regarding the Human and Demon pairing, there is a specific detail: if the groom is a Demon and the bride is a Human, there is enmity; if the groom is a Human and the bride is a Demon, it leads to the death of the groom—this is supported by many authorities. In this compatibility test (<term>Kuta</term>), the points are assigned as follows: 6 points for same groups, 5 points for Divine-Human, and 0 points for Divine-Demon or Human-Demon. Specifically, a Demon bride and Divine groom get 2 points, while a Divine bride and Human groom get 4 points. ॥ 30 ॥

( <term>Anushtubh</term> <note>The most common 8-syllable Sanskrit meter.</note> ) Counting from an odd-numbered zodiac sign of the bride, the sixth sign is unfavorable (the 6th-8th relationship). Counting from an even sign, the sixth is considered auspicious, but the eighth is the opposite (inauspicious). Death is known in the 6th-8th position; loss of children in the 9th-5th position; and poverty in the 2nd-12th position. In other positions, it brings happiness. ॥ 31 ॥

<meta>CC-0 Shri Krishna Museum, Kurukshetra. Digitized by eGangotri</meta>

It also introduces the "Bhakuta" or zodiac sign compatibility, warning against specific relative positions like the 6th-8th or 2nd-12th.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological compatibility (Kuta) for marriage, specifically addressing the "Bhakuta" (zodiac sign relationship) and its potential remedies.</meta>

<page-num>( 81 )</page-num>
<header>With Language Commentary - Chapter 6.</header>

The sixth sign from an odd-numbered sign, and the eighth from an even-numbered sign, is the same; this is known as the **Enemy Shadashtaka** <note>original: "Shatru-Shadashtaka"; a 6-8 relationship between signs considered unfavorable</note>. The lords of these signs are enemies. Conversely, the sixth from an even sign and the eighth from an odd sign is the **Friend Shadashtaka** <note>original: "Mitra-Shadashtaka"</note>. Their lords are friends, and this is auspicious. Anything contrary to this is inauspicious; the Enemy Shadashtaka is said to cause death. If the bride and groom's signs are in a 5-9 relationship (five signs from one another), it causes the loss of sons, and if they are in a 2-12 relationship, it leads to poverty. Other positions are considered auspicious. || 31 ||

> ->(Shardulavikridita Meter)<-
> If a corrupted Bhakuta <note>zodiac compatibility</note> is declared, the marriage is still auspicious if there is "Single Lordship" <note>original: "Ekadhipatya"; when both signs are ruled by the same planet</note>, or if there is friendship between the lords of the signs, provided there is purity of the Nadi <note>pulse/energy channel</note> and the Nakshatra <note>lunar mansion</note>. If the lords of the signs and the lords of the Navamsha <note>the ninth-part divisional chart</note> are mutually friendly and strong, and if there is purity of Nadi and Tara <note>stellar strength</note>, then even in the absence of sign-subservience, it is sanctioned by the wise. || 32 ||

Even when a "corrupted Bhakuta" is present as described, there is a remedy: if the lord of the bride and groom's signs is the same—for example, Aries and Scorpio (both ruled by Mars) or Taurus and Libra (both ruled by Venus)—then the marriage is auspicious. It is also auspicious if the lords of the signs are friends, provided there is Nadi-purity and Nakshatra-purity. If the lords of the signs and the lords of the divisional portions <term>Amshas: the sub-divisional lords of the zodiac signs</term> are mutually friendly and powerful, and there is Nadi-purity and Tara-purity, and the sign-attraction is appropriate, then the defect of planetary enmity does not apply. Here, the remedies for the 6-8 relationship (Shadashtaka) are: (1) Friendly 6-8 relationship, (2) Single Lordship, (3) Friendship between strong divisional lords, (4) Sign-subservience, and (5) Stellar purity. If even one of these is present, the Shadashtaka defect is nullified, but Nadi-purity must be present in all cases. || 32 ||

> ->(Shalini Meter)<-
> If there is friendship between the two lords of the signs or the two lords of the divisional portions, there is no defect of the Ganas <note>the temperament categories: Deva (Divine), Manushya (Human), and Rakshasa (Demonic)</note>. A good Bhakuta <note>sign relationship</note> destroys planetary enmity, and planetary friendship destroys a corrupted Bhakuta. || 33 ||

Regarding the remedies for Gana-kuta, Bha-kuta, and Graha-kuta: if there is mutual friendship between the sign lords and divisional lords of the bride and groom, then the defect of "corrupted Gana" (such as a Demonic and Human match) does not apply. Furthermore, if there is a favorable sign relationship (such as 3rd and 11th positions), the defect of planetary enmity is removed. Indeed, the affection between sign lords destroys defects like the 6-8 relationship. || 33 ||

> ->(Sragdhara Meter)<-
> Jyeshtha, Ardra, Aryaman (Purva Phalguni), and Varuna (Shatabhisha) form one pair; the two Ashvinis form another—these belong to the same Nadi. Pushya, Shravana, Chitra, Mrigashira, Mula, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, and Purva Bhadrapada belong to the Middle Nadi...

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of matrimonial astrology (Muhurta), specifically focusing on the compatibility of "Nadi" (pulse or life-force channels) between a prospective bride and groom.</meta>

<page-num>82</page-num>
->The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments<- <note>original: "Muhurtachintamani"</note>

## The constellations of Vayu (Svati), Agni (Krittika), Vyala (Ashlesha), Vishwodu (Uttarashadha), and the pairs following them, as well as the constellation of Paushna (Revati) and those following it in reverse order—
## If the bride and groom share the same Nadi, the marriage is inauspicious; if they share the Middle Nadi, death occurs. 34.

The **First Nadi** <note>Adya Nadi</note> consists of pairs starting from Jyeshtha, Ardra, Uttaraphalguni, and Shatataraka. The **Middle Nadi** <note>Madhya Nadi</note> consists of Pushya, Mrigashira, Chitra, Anuradha, Bharani, Dhanishtha, Purvashadha, Purvaphalguni, and Uttarabhadrapada. The **Last Nadi** <note>Antya Nadi</note> consists of pairs starting from Svati, Krittika, Ashlesha, and Uttarashadha. If the constellations <note>Nakshatras</note> of the groom and bride fall within the same Nadi, the result is inauspicious. If they both fall in the Middle Nadi, death is certain for both. Some traditions hold that the defect of the side Nadis (First and Last) does not apply south of the Godavari River or to the Warrior caste <note>Kshatriyas</note> and others. Another opinion states that the First Nadi harms the groom, the Last Nadi harms the bride, and the Middle Nadi harms both; among these, the Last Nadi is sometimes accepted if other remedial factors are present.

"From the constellation of Ashwini, depending on whether the bride's constellation has four, three, or two quarters <note>Padas</note> in one zodiac sign, the Nadi calculation follows the three, four, or five-fold division respectively." Other texts describe these three, four, and five-fold Nadi systems: if the bride's constellation has four quarters in one sign, use the three-Nadi system; if three quarters, use the four-Nadi system; and if two quarters, use the five-Nadi system. The three-Nadi system is counted from Ashwini, the four-Nadi from Krittika, and the five-Nadi from Mrigashira. However, the four-Nadi system is used in the Ahalla region, the five-Nadi in Punjab, while the three-Nadi system is forbidden everywhere <note>meaning its defects are universally avoided</note>. Some say the defect is specific to the first, fourth, third, or second quarters of the constellation. Nadi consideration is applicable to bride and groom, master and servant, new friends, countries, new lands, villages, cities, and houses.

<note>The following lines are underlined in the original manuscript for emphasis.</note>

> Where the constellation-based Nadi defect exists but the quarter-based <note>Charana</note> Nadi defect does not, the fault is considered minor. When the aforementioned remedies are applicable, there is also a ritual peace-making <note>Shanti</note> for the Nadi defect, such as chanting the "Conqueror of Death" mantra <note>Mahamrityunjaya Japa</note>, donating a golden Nadi, and for other compatibility factors <note>Kutas</note> like Varna, donating cows, food, clothes, or gold. || 34 ||

# Language Commentary Included - Chapter 6

The fifth type of relationship is that of the "Eater and the Eaten" <note>Sanskrit: *bhakshya-bhakshaka*; a natural predatory enmity</note>. For example, the eagle and the snake, or the cat and the mouse. If the birth stars <term>Nakshatras: the 27 lunar mansions of Hindu astrology</term> of a man and a woman fall into this category, it is considered inauspicious. Some practitioners also calculate this for a groom and bride, or a master and servant, based on the first letter of their names. || 35 ||

> (Shalini meter) If the zodiac sign is the same but the birth stars are different, or if the birth star is the same but the zodiac signs are different, then the flaws of the pulse <term>Nadi-dosha: a serious mismatch in Ayurvedic temperament</term> and the flaws of the temperament class <term>Gana-dosha: mismatch between divine, human, or demonic temperaments</term> are nullified. If the birth star is the same but the quarters <term>Padas: the four quarters of a Nakshatra</term> are different, it is auspicious. || 36 ||

If the couple shares one zodiac sign but has two different birth stars, or shares one birth star but has two different zodiac signs, or shares a birth star but belongs to different quarters (not the same quarter), then the "pulse flaw," the "class flaw," and by extension, the "star flaw" <note>original: *taradi-dosha*</note> do not apply. In matters of daily business, royal service, war, village affairs, and friendship, the results are determined by the zodiac sign associated with one's name. || 36 ||

> (Manjubhashini meter) Mars, Venus, Mercury, the Moon, the Sun, Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, Saturn, and Jupiter are the lords of the signs. The method for calculating the Ninth Division <term>Navamsha: a 1/9th sub-division of a sign used to determine the hidden strength of a planet</term> begins from Aries, Capricorn, Libra, and Cancer respectively. || 37 ||

**Lords of the Signs:** Mars rules Aries and Scorpio; Venus rules Libra and Taurus; Mercury rules Gemini (3) and Virgo (6); the Moon rules Cancer (4); the Sun rules Leo (5); Jupiter rules Sagittarius (9) and Pisces (12); and Saturn rules Capricorn (10) and Aquarius (11). Regarding the **Ninth Division (Navamsha)**: one zodiac sign consists of 30 degrees. These are divided into 9 parts of 3 degrees and 20 minutes each. <note>The text lists the mathematical increments: 3°20', 6°40', 10°0', 13°20', 16°40', 20°0', 23°20', 26°40', 30°0'</note>. The counting for signs 1, 5, and 9 starts from Aries; for 2, 6, and 10 from Capricorn; for 3, 7, and 11 from Libra; and for 4, 8, and 12 from Cancer. For example, in Aries, the first 3°20' is the Aries division, up to 6°40' is the Taurus division, and so on. In Taurus, up to 3°20' is the Capricorn division, and up to 6°40' is the Aquarius division. || 37 ||

> (Shashivadana meter) In even signs, the first half belongs to the Moon and the second to the Sun. In odd signs, the first half belongs to the Sun and the second to the Moon. || 38 ||

**The Hour (Hora):** In an even sign, the first 15 degrees belong to the Moon and the remaining 15 to the Sun. In an odd sign, the first 15 degrees belong to the Sun and the remaining 15 to the Moon. || 38 ||

> (Vasantatilaka meter) Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, Saturn, and Mars rule the portions of 5, 7, 8, 5, and 5 degrees respectively in even signs. In odd signs, this order is reversed. The lords of the Decans <term>Drekkana: 1/3rd division of a sign (10 degrees)</term> are the lords of the first, fifth, and ninth signs from the current one. || 39 ||

<summary>This page details the rules for neutralizing astrological flaws in compatibility and explains the mathematical sub-divisions of zodiac signs, including the Navamsha (ninths), Hora (halves), and Drekkana (thirds).</summary>
<keywords>astrological compatibility, Nadi-dosha, Gana-dosha, Navamsha calculation, Hora, planetary lords, zodiac divisions, Hindu astrology</keywords>

--- Page 103 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of planetary divisions and inauspicious transitional periods (Gandanta) within the Muhurta Chintamani, a classic text on electional astrology.</meta>

# Muhurta Chintamani
## Page 86

In an even-numbered <term>Zodiac sign</term> (Rashi), the <term>Trishansha</term> <note>a 1/30th division of a sign, used to determine planetary strength and character</note> is distributed as follows: the first 5 degrees belong to Venus, the next 7 to Mercury, the next 8 to Jupiter, the next 5 to Saturn, and the final 5 to Mars. In an odd-numbered sign, the order is reversed: the first 5 degrees belong to Mars, the next 5 to Saturn, the next 8 to Jupiter, the next 7 to Mercury, and the final 5 to Venus. 

Regarding the <term>Drekkana</term> <note>a 1/10th division, though usually referring to 10-degree decans</note>: for the first 10 degrees, the ruler is the lord of that sign; from 11 to 20 degrees, the ruler is the lord of the 5th sign from it; and from 21 to 30 degrees, the ruler is the lord of the 9th sign from it. || 39 ||

->*(In the Vasantatilaka meter)*<-
The <term>Dwadashansha</term> <note>1/12th division</note> begins from the sign itself. The <term>Hora</term> <note>1/2 division</note>, <term>Drekkana</term>, <term>Navamsha</term> <note>1/9th division</note>, and <term>Trishansha</term>—these six are called the <term>Shadvarga</term> <note>The Six-fold Divisions</note>. Benefic planets in these divisions bring auspicious results, while malefic planets bring inauspiciousness. || 40 ||

**Dwadashansha:** One sign of 30 degrees is divided into 12 parts, each consisting of 2.5 degrees (2°30'). The counting starts from the sign itself. For example, in Aries: the first 2°30' is the Dwadashansha of Aries; up to 5°0' is Taurus; up to 7°30' is Gemini, and so on for all. The Hora, Drekkana, Navamsha, Dwadashansha, Trishansha, and the Sign itself constitute the **Shadvarga**. In all undertakings, the Shadvarga of benefic planets is auspicious, and that of malefic planets is inauspicious. || 40 ||

->*(In the Shardulavikridita meter)*<-
The final two <term>Ghatikas</term> <note>1 Ghatika = 24 minutes</note> of Jyeshtha, Revati, and Ashlesha, and the first two Ghatikas of Mula, Ashwini, and Magha are called <term>Gandanta</term> <note>A "knot" or "end-point" considered spiritually perilous</note>. Similarly, the last half-Ghatika of Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces, and the first half-Ghatika of Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius are the <term>Lagna-Gandanta</term>. The final one Ghatika of the <term>Purna</term> dates (5th, 10th, 15th) and the first one Ghatika of the <term>Nanda</term> dates (1st, 6th, 11th) constitute the <term>Tithi-Gandanta</term>, which is inauspicious. || 41 ||

There are junctions (Sandhi) in all divisions of time, including the <term>Panchanga</term> <note>The five limbs of the calendar: Tithi, Vara, Nakshatra, Yoga, Karana</note>, years, seasons, months, fortnights, and days. Among these, the junctions of Tithi, Nakshatra, and Lagna are specifically named **Gandanta**. 

The **Nakshatra-Gandanta** consists of 4 Ghatikas total: 2 at the end of Jyeshtha, Revati, or Ashlesha, and 2 at the start of Ashwini, Magha, or Mula. The **Lagna-Gandanta** consists of 1 Ghatika total: half a Ghatika at the end of the water signs (Cancer, Scorpio, Pisces) and half at the start of the fire signs (Aries, Leo, Sagittarius). The **Tithi-Gandanta** consists of 2 Ghatikas total: 1 at the end of Purna Tithis (5, 10, 15) and 1 at the start of Nanda Tithis (1, 6, 11). 

Children born during Gandanta may face or cause difficulties; this was explained in detail in the chapter on Nakshatras. Gandanta should be avoided for auspicious works. However, other texts mention that Tithi-Gandanta is neutralized if the Moon is strong, and Lagna-Gandanta is neutralized if Jupiter is strong. Similarly, the last 3 days of a month and the last 15 days of a year are considered "junction" Gandantas. The junctions of Yoga and Karana last 1 Ghatika each, as do the junctions of day, night, midnight, and midday. || 41 ||

<summary>This page details the calculation of the Shadvarga (six-fold planetary divisions) and defines the various types of Gandanta (inappropriate transitional periods) for Tithis, Nakshatras, and Lagnas.</summary>
<keywords>Trishansha, Drekkana, Shadvarga, Dwadashansha, Gandanta, Tithi, Nakshatra, Lagna, Muhurta Chintamani, Vedic Astrology</keywords>

--- Page 104 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological combinations (yogas) affecting marriage and general prosperity, specifically focusing on the "Kartari" (Scissor) formation and the relationship between the birth sign and the wedding ascendant.</meta>

<page-num>87</page-num>
<header>With Vernacular Commentary - Chapter 6</header>

> (Anushtubh Verse) If malefic planets are situated in the 2nd and 12th houses from the Ascendant <note>the 'Lagna' or rising sign</note>, one being retrograde and the other in direct motion, this is known as the **Kartari** <term>Kartari: literally 'scissors', where the house is 'cut' or squeezed between two harmful influences</term> yoga. It bestows death, poverty, and grief. || 42 ||

If a malefic planet in the 2nd house is retrograde and a malefic in the 12th house is in direct motion from the Ascendant, it is called **Kartari**. In marriages and other auspicious rites, it results in death, poverty, or grief. Similarly, a Kartari formation on the 7th house <note>the house of marriage</note> is said to be inauspicious. It also produces these results if formed around the Moon. In birth charts, this yoga brings harmful results to the specific significations of whichever house it affects. || 42 ||

> (Anushtubh Verse) When the Moon is joined with the Sun and others, the results are poverty, death, auspiciousness, happiness, rivalry, and detachment respectively. If joined by two malefics, death occurs. || 43 ||

If the Moon is with the Sun, it causes poverty; with Mars, death; with Mercury, auspiciousness; with Jupiter, happiness; with Venus, rivalry <note>specifically 'sapatnya', referring to co-wives or domestic competition</note>; and with Saturn, detachment or a mendicant's life <note>original: "fakiri"</term>. The same should be understood for Rahu and Ketu <note>the lunar nodes</note>. If the Moon is joined by two malefic planets, death occurs. However, according to other texts, if the Moon is in a friend's sign, its own sign, its exaltation, or a favorable divisional chart (Vargottama), the fault of being joined by a malefic does not apply. || 43 ||

> (Anushtubh Verse) A marriage is not desired when the wedding ascendant is the 8th house from the birth ascendant or birth Moon. However, if the same planet rules both signs or if the lords are friends, there is no fault. || 44 ||

The 8th sign (Ascendant) from one's birth Ascendant or birth Moon sign is not auspicious for marriage or other holy works. But if there is "common lordship" <note>where one planet rules both signs, such as Mars ruling Aries (1) and Scorpio (8)</note> or if the lords of the signs are friends (such as Leo (5) and Sagittarius (9/12)), then the fault of the 8th house relationship does not apply. || 44 ||

> (Upajati Verse) When Pisces, Taurus, Cancer, Scorpio, Capricorn, or Virgo are the 8th sign, the 8th-house fault is not produced. Due to mutual friendship, the bride will be blessed with children, long life, and domestic happiness. || 45 ||

If the signs 12, 2, 4, 8, 10, or 6 <note>Pisces, Taurus, Cancer, Scorpio, Capricorn, and Virgo</note> are the 8th from the birth Ascendant or birth Moon, the aforementioned 8th-house fault is absent because their lords are mutual friends. Because of this, even if these signs are the 8th, the bride receives the joy of children, longevity, and a happy home. Another opinion states that if the lord of the 8th sign is in a quadrant (Kendra) or in its exaltation, the 8th-house fault is nullified. || 45 ||

->(Kusumavichitra Meter)<-

> If the Navamsha <note>the 1/9th divisional chart</note> of the 8th house or its lord is in the wedding Ascendant, it is not auspicious. If the 12th house, its Navamsha, or its lord is present, it will cause strife. || 46 ||

<summary>This page details the "Kartari" (Scissor) yoga and the "Ashtama" (8th house) restrictions for selecting a marriage timing, providing exceptions where planetary friendships override these negative omens.</summary>
<keywords>Kartari Yoga, Marriage Astrology, Moon Conjunctions, 8th House Fault, Ashtama Lagna, Planetary Friendship, Muhurta</keywords>

( 88 )
# Muhurtachintamani #
<note>The "Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments," a classic 16th-century Sanskrit text on Hindu astrology.</note>

It is not auspicious if the aforementioned eighth sign's <term>Navamsha</term><note>A one-ninth division of a zodiac sign</note> or the lord of the eighth house is present in the Ascendant. If the Ascendant at the time of an event is the twelfth house from the birth sign, or its division, or if its lord is in the Ascendant, it causes strife; some say it causes loss of wealth. || 46 ||

->*(Vamshastha Meter)*<-
> From the 50th, 24th, 30th, 40th, 14th, 21st, 30th, 20th, and 32nd; || 47 ||
> From the 30th, 20th, 18th, 21st, 20th, 14th, 14th, 10th, and 14th; || 48 ||
> From the 56th, 24th, 20th, 10th, 10th, 18th, 16th, 24th, and 30th.
> These <term>Visha Nadis</term><note>Poisonous pulses of time</note> are to be avoided in auspicious acts and births. These constants are based on a 60-ghati duration; to find the exact start, multiply the constant by the total duration and divide by 60. || 49 ||

<term>Visha Ghati</term>—Starting after the following <term>Ghatis</term><note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> for 4 Ghatis: Revati, Punarvasu, Krittika, and Magha after 30; Rohini after 40; Ashlesha after 32; Ashwini after 50; Bharani after 24; Shatabhisha after 18; Purva Phalguni, Chitra, Uttarashadha, and Pushya after 20; Vishakha, Swati, Mrigashira, and Jyeshtha after 14; Ardra after 21; Purva Bhadrapada after 16; Uttara Bhadrapada, Purvashadha, and Bharani after 24; Anuradha, Dhanishta, and Shravana after 10; and Mula after 56. These **Poisonous Pulses** are inauspicious for all auspicious works and births (to be avoided). This is calculated based on a standard 60-ghati duration. If a <term>Nakshatra</term><note>Lunar mansion</note> is more or less than 60 ghatis, multiply the constant by the actual duration and divide by 60 to find the precise start of the Visha Ghati. Other texts offer a remedy: if the Moon is strong in a <term>Kendra</term> or <term>Trikona</term><note>Angular or succedent houses</note> (excluding the Ascendant), or if the Lord of the Ascendant is in a Kendra with a benefic planet, the flaw of Visha Ghati is nullified. || 47 || 48 || 49 ||
