<note>This table lists the specific 'Ghati' (a unit of 24 minutes) on each lunar day that is considered 'poisonous' or inauspicious for beginning new works.</note>

->(Verse in Malini Meter)<-
**Shiva, the Serpent, Mitra, the Ancestors, the Vasus, Water, the Universal Gods, Abhijit, and then Brahma, Indra, Indra-Agni, Nirriti, the Lord of Waters, Aryaman, and Bhaga are the lords of the fifteen Muhurtas of the day in sequence. || 50 ||**

The lords of the 15 <term>Muhurtas</term> <note>A Muhurta is a traditional unit of time lasting 48 minutes, or 2 Ghatis</note> of a single day are: 1. Mahadeva (Shiva), 2. the Serpent, 3. Mitra, 4. the Ancestors (Pitrs), 5. the Vasus, 6. Water (Apah), 7. the Universal Gods (Vishvedevas), 8. Abhijit, 9. Brahma, 10. Indra, 11. Indra-Agni, 12. the Demon (Nirriti), 13. Varuna, 14. Aryaman, and 15. Bhaga. One Muhurta consists of 2 Ghatis. || 50 ||

->(Verse in Anushtubh Meter)<-
**Shiva, Aja-Pada, Ahirbudhnya, Pushan, the Ashvins, Yama, Agni, Brahma, the Moon, Aditi, Jupiter, Vishnu, the Sun, Tvashtar, and Vayu are proclaimed as the lords of the Muhurtas at night. || 51 ||**

The Night Muhurta lords are: 1. Shiva, 2. Aja-Pada, 3. Ahirbudhnya, 4. Pushan, 5. the Ashvins, 6. Yama, 7. Agni, 8. Brahma, 9. <unclear>the Moon</unclear> <note>original: Chandrama; text obscured by staining</note>, 10. Aditi, 11. Jupiter, 12. Vishnu, 13. the Sun, 14. Tvashtar, and 15. Vayu. These are the presiding lords of the night. The purpose of knowing these is that whatever task is prescribed for a specific <term>Nakshatra</term> <note>Lunar mansion/star cluster</note> should be performed during the Muhurta ruled by that same deity. As the author has clearly stated: <quote>“A task should be done in the portion of the day ruled by the lord of the star.”</quote> <note>original: "Dhishnye proktam svamitithyamshakesya"</note> || 51 ||

->(Verse in Bhujangaprayata Meter)<-
**Aryaman on Sunday; Brahma and the Demon on Monday; Agni and the Ancestors on Tuesday; Abhijit on Wednesday; Water and the Demon on Thursday; Brahma and the Ancestors on Friday; and Shiva and the Serpent on Saturday—these Muhurtas are forbidden. || 52 ||**

On Sunday the Aryaman Muhurta is forbidden; on Monday, Brahma and the Demon (Rakshasa); on Tuesday, Agni and the Ancestors; on Wednesday, Abhijit; on Thursday, Water and the Demon; on Friday, Brahma and the Ancestors; and on Saturday, Shiva and the Serpent Muhurtas are prohibited for auspicious works. || 52 ||

->(Verse in Praharshini Meter)<-
**Marriage is auspicious during the piercing-free stars of Mrigashira, Hasta, Mula, Anuradha, Magha, Rohini, Revati, the three Uttaras, and Svati; on days of auspicious planets, excluding the Rikta and New Moon days. Abhijit occurs in the last quarter of the Universal Gods' star and the first portion of the following star. || 53 ||**

For a <term>Marriage Muhurta</term> <note>The calculated time for a wedding ceremony</note>, the following <term>Nakshatras</term> (stars) are auspicious if they are free from astrological "piercing" (Vedha): Mrigashira, Hasta, Mula, Anuradha, Magha, Rohini, Revati, the three Uttaras (Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada), and Svati. Marriage is favorable on the weekdays of auspicious planets. One should not choose the <term>Rikta</term> <note>The "empty" lunar days: 4th, 9th, and 14th</note> or the New Moon (30th) days. Furthermore, within 4 days of the marriage...

<meta>The text cuts off mid-sentence at the bottom of the page regarding marriage prohibitions.</meta>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion on Muhurta (electional astrology), specifically focusing on planetary obstructions (Vedha) and the calculation of the Abhijit nakshatra.</meta>

<page-num>90</page-num>
<header>The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments</header>

If it is the day of a <term>Shraddha</term><note>A ceremony performed in honor of a deceased ancestor</note> or the <term>Amavasya</term><note>The new moon day</note>, one should not perform the activity; this is also an authoritative rule. The fourth quarter of the constellation <term>Uttara Ashadha</term> and the first 4 <term>Ghatis</term><note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes; 4 ghatis is 96 minutes</note> of <term>Shravana</term> constitute the <term>Abhijit</term> constellation. || 53 ||

> (*In the Shardulavikridita meter*) There is a mutual obstruction caused by planets between Rohini and Abhijit; Bharani and Anuradha; Uttara Ashadha and Mrigashira; Shravana and Magha; Hasta and Uttara Bhadrapada; Swati and Shatabhisha; Mula and Punarvasu; and Uttara Phalguni and Revati. If a planet is situated there, the obstruction occurs between the first and fourth quarters, and between the second and third quarters. || 54 ||

The **Five-Spoke Obstruction** (<term>Pancha-shalaka-vedha</term>) occurs between: Rohini and Abhijit; Bharani and Anuradha; Uttara Ashadha and Mrigashira; Shravana and Magha; Hasta and Uttara Bhadrapada; Swati and Shatabhisha; Mula and Punarvasu; and Uttara Phalguni and Revati. For the remaining constellations, the obstruction should be understood according to the **Seven-Spoke** method mentioned in the next verse. Regarding the quarters (<term>Padas</term>) of the constellations: the first quarter obstructs the fourth, the second obstructs the third, the third obstructs the second, and the fourth obstructs the first. || 54 ||

> (*In the Shardulavikridita meter*) Jyeshtha and Pushya; Shatabhisha and Krittika; Purva Ashadha and Ardra; Revati and Uttara Phalguni; Dhanishta and Vishakha; Uttara Ashadha and Mrigashira; Ashwini and Purva Phalguni; Ashlesha and Anuradha; Hasta and Chitra; Rohini and Mrigashira; Mula and Aditi (Punarvasu); Chitra and Swati; Shravana and Magha; Krittika and Shravana—these are mutually obstructed, O moon-faced one. || 55 ||

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological impediments (doshas) in the context of marriage and auspicious timing, specifically focusing on planetary "piercings" (vedha) and "kicks" (latta).</meta>

# Page 91
## Chapter 6: Language Commentary Included

The mutual <term>Seven-Spike Piercing</term> <note>Saptashalaka Vedha: A method of checking planetary obstructions by drawing seven horizontal and seven vertical lines to see if planets intersect on specific stars.</note> of the planets occurs between the following stars:
<term>Hand</term> (Hasta) and <term>Latter Beautiful Steps</term> (Uttara Bhadrapada); <term>Red One</term> (Rohini) and <term>Victorious</term> (Abhijit); <term>Root</term> (Mula) and <term>Return of the Wealthy</term> (Punarvasu); <term>Bright One</term> (Chitra) and <term>Former Beautiful Steps</term> (Purva Bhadrapada); <term>Bearer</term> (Bharani) and <term>Mighty One</term> (Magha); <term>The Cutters</term> (Kruttika) and <term>Hearing</term> (Shravana). The result of this <term>piercing</term> (vedha) is as follows:

> "If a woman’s <term>Moon</term> is pierced by the <term>Seven-Spike</term> alignment—whether by malefic or even non-malefic planets—at the time of her wedding, she will go to the cremation ground weeping, still wrapped in her wedding garments. (1)"

If at the time of a woman's marriage, the <term>Moon</term> is pierced by <term>malefic planets</term> <note>Papagraha: Planets like Saturn, Mars, or the Sun that are considered naturally harsh in Vedic astrology.</note> through the <term>Seven-Spike</term> alignment, she will go to the cremation ground crying in her wedding clothes; that is, she will soon become a widow and her desires will remain unfulfilled. || 55 ||

> (Verse) Stars that are pierced by cruel planets, or those currently occupied or about to be entered by cruel planets, are said to become fit for auspicious works only after they are released by those planets and then subsequently joined by the Moon. || 56 ||

When a <term>star</term> (nakshatra) is freed from being pierced by a malefic, or similarly when a malefic has finished passing through it or is no longer occupying it, that <term>star</term> becomes pure and suitable for auspicious work only after the Moon has traveled through it. Other texts state regarding "two-sign stars" <note>Stars that span across two different zodiac signs.</note> that only the specific portion of the sign where the <term>malefic planet</term> resides is forbidden; the other portion remains acceptable for auspicious work. || 56 ||

> (Verse) Mercury, Rahu, the Full Moon, and Venus 'kick' backwards at the 7th, 9th, 22nd, and 5th stars respectively. The Sun, Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars 'kick' forward at the 12th, 8th, 6th, and 3rd stars. || 57 ||

<term>The Kick</term> (Latta)—<term>Mercury</term> creates the <term>Kick Defect</term> (Latta-dosha) on the 7th star behind the one it occupies; <term>Rahu</term> <note>The North Node of the Moon.</note> on the 9th star behind it; the <term>Full Moon</term> on the 22nd star behind it (this typically occurs between the 6th, 7th, and 8th days of the <term>dark fortnight</term>); and <term>Venus</term> on the 5th star behind it. Furthermore, the <term>Sun</term> creates this defect on the 12th star ahead of it, <term>Saturn</term> on the 8th ahead, <term>Jupiter</term> on the 6th ahead, and <term>Mars</term> on the 3rd ahead. For a <term>retrograde planet</term>, the direction of the <term>kick</term> should be understood as the opposite of the order mentioned above. || 57 ||

> (Verse) At the end of the turnings of the Joyful, Fatal, Achievable, Calamitous, Joint, and Spear-like alignments, the star that is struck down by the fall is considered afflicted. || 58 ||

<term>The Fall</term> (Pata)—The <term>star</term> in which the (end) completion of the <term>Joyful</term> (Harshana), <term>Fatal</term> (Vaidhriti), <term>Achievable</term> (Sadhya), <term>Calamitous</term> (Vyatipata), <term>Joint</term> (Ganda), and <term>Spear-like</term> (Shula) yogas occurs is afflicted by the <term>Fall Defect</term> (Pata-dosha). It is forbidden for auspicious work; this is also known as <term>The Weapon of the Fierce Lord</term> (Chandish-chandayudha). || 58 ||

> (Verse) [Regarding the equality of brilliance between the Moon and Sun in various signs...] || 59 ||

<header>The Jewel of Auspicious Moments (Muhurta Chintamani)</header>

**Declination Equality (Krantisamya):** When the Sun and Moon are in the same line of declination across the following pairs of zodiac signs—Aries and Leo; Taurus and Capricorn; Libra and Aquarius; Virgo and Pisces; Cancer and Scorpio; Sagittarius and Gemini—the fault of *Krantisamya* <note>original: "क्रांतिसाम्य" - a state where the Sun and Moon have equal declination, considered highly inauspicious</note> occurs. This is forbidden for all auspicious activities and is also known as the *Mahapata* <note>The "Great Fall" or a major astronomical disturbance</note>. || 59 ||

| 12 (Pisces) | | | | 6 (Virgo) |
| 8 (Scorpio) | | | | 4 (Cancer) |
| | 9 (Sagittarius) | 5 (Leo) | 10 (Capricorn) | |

(Indravajra Meter) **The Yogas of Vyaghata, Ganda, Vyatipata, Shula, Vajra, Parigha, and Atiganda...**
->**...create a conflict (Ekargala) if the Moon is in an odd-numbered constellation (Nakshatra) relative to the Sun. || 60 ||**<-

**The Single-Bolt Obstruction (Ekargala):** If on a day when unfavorable *Yogas* <note>astrological combinations</note> like *Vyaghata*, *Ganda*, or *Vyatipata* (and others like *Shula*, *Vaidhriti*, *Vajra*, *Parigha*, *Atiganda*) occur, the Moon's constellation is at an odd-numbered distance from the Sun's constellation, the *Ekargala* fault is formed. If the Moon's constellation is at an even distance from the Sun's, this fault does not occur even during those Yogas. This is also called *Kharjura* <note>original: "खार्जूर" - literally meaning 'date palm', likely referring to the visual structure of the calculation chart</note>. || 60 ||

<margin>Ekargala Chart</margin>

->**...constellations from the Sun are the Minor Planets (Upagrahas). These are inauspicious...**<-

**Minor Planets (Upagraha):** If the Moon's constellation is the 5th, 8th, 10th, 14th, 17th, 19th, 15th, 18th, 21st, 22nd, 23rd, 24th, or 25th from the Sun's constellation, the *Upagraha* fault occurs. This specifically causes ill effects in the regions of *Bahlika* <note>Ancient Bactria/modern Punjab/Afghanistan borderlands</note> and *Kuru* <note>The region around modern Delhi/Haryana</note>. Some authorities offer a remedy (parihara) here: the fault only applies if the Moon is in the exact same quarter (charana) of the constellation as the Sun; otherwise, it is not a fault. These remedies also apply to the aforementioned *Kharjura-Ekargala*. || 61 ||

(Anushtubh Meter) **In Pata, Upagraha, and Latta, the specific quarter of the constellation is considered...**
->**...the day is divided into eight parts to determine the half-watch (Ardha-yama). || 62 ||**<-

**(Pata):** In the faults of *Chandisha*, *Chandayudha*, *Upagraha*, and *Latta* <note>planetary "kicks" or strikes</note>, the specific quarter (pada/charana) of the constellation is afflicted. For instance, the specific quarter where the *Pata* or *Upagraha* falls is the only part to be avoided. In the case of *Latta*, the fault exists for a number of days corresponding to the quarter of the constellation the planet occupies. Regarding the "Half-Watch" (*Ardha-yama*), it refers to the current...

<page-num>93</page-num>
<header>With Language Commentary - Chapter 6.</header>

Multiply the day of the week <note>Sunday=1, Monday=2, etc.</note> by 3 and divide by 8; to the remainder, add 1. This determines the **Ardhyama Dosha** <note>a specific "half-watch" affliction</note>. This is forbidden for auspicious works during the day, but not at night. || 62 ||

*(Verse)* The Kulika periods for the Sun [Sunday] onwards are the 14th, 12th, 10th, 8th, 6th, 4th, and 2nd Muhurtas. At night, subtract one from these. For Saturn, the end is also censured. || 63 ||

**Kulika:** During the day, the Kulika <note>a specific inauspicious time-segment</note> falls on the 14th Muhurta for Sunday, 12th for Monday, 10th for Tuesday, 8th for Wednesday, 6th for Thursday, 4th for Friday, and 2nd for Saturday. In the night, subtract 1 from these values: Sunday 13th, Monday 11th, Tuesday 9th, Wednesday 7th, Thursday 5th, Friday 3rd, and Saturday 1st. These Muhurtas are highly condemned in marriage as they cause widowhood; for this reason, they are mentioned here again. They were also mentioned in the first chapter on auspicious/inauspicious timings, but there they were counted as general flaws; in other works, their results are not considered as harmful. || 63 ||

### Muhurta <note>A unit of time approximately 48 minutes long</note>

*(Verse)* When the Sun is in Sagittarius or Pisces; Taurus or Aquarius; Cancer or Aries; Virgo or Gemini; Leo or Scorpio; or Capricorn or Libra—the second and subsequent lunar dates become "burnt" (Dagda). || 64 ||

**Dagda Tithi (Burnt Lunar Dates):** When the Sun is in Sagittarius or Pisces, the 2nd lunar date is burnt; in Taurus or Aquarius, the 4th; in Cancer or Aries, the 6th; in Gemini or Virgo, the 8th; in Leo or Scorpio, the 10th; and in Capricorn or Libra, the 12th. These "Month-Burnt" dates are only forbidden in the *Madhyadesha* <note>the central region of India</note>. || 64 ||

*(Verse)* If a planet is in the seventh house from the Ascendant or the Moon, marriage should not take place. Or, if the seventh house falls at 55 degrees <note>original: "baana-ashuga" meaning 5 and 5</note>, it is harmful. || 65 ||

**Yamitra Dosha:** If a planet is in the seventh house from the Ascendant (Lagna) or the Moon, the *Yamitra* flaw occurs. it produces inauspicious results in marriages and similar events. Or, if the Ascendant or Moon is situated at the 55th degree in the Navamsha <note>the ninth divisional chart</note>...

# Page 94
## Muhurta Chintamani

->The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments<-

...there is a specific flaw. For example, if the Ascendant or the Moon is at 5 degrees of Libra, then the 5th degree of Aries is the 55th <note>This refers to a specific divisional calculation in the zodiac</note>. Any planet situated there creates a *Yamitri* <note>A specific planetary opposition flaw</note>. This is the "subtle" *Yamitri*. In other texts, the *Yamitri* of beneficial planets is actually considered auspicious. ॥ 65 ॥

**(Indravajra Meter)**
The flaws of *Upagraha* <note>Secondary or "shadow" planets</note> should be avoided in the regions of Kuru and Bahlika <note>Modern-day Punjab and parts of Afghanistan/Pakistan</note>. The flaw of *Pata* <note>referring to the nodes or specific astronomical intersections</note> should be avoided in Kalinga and Vanga <note>Modern-day Odisha and Bengal</note>. The flaw of *Latta* <note>planetary "kick" or influence on a star</note> should be avoided in Saurashtra and Shalva <note>Modern-day Gujarat and Rajasthan</note>. However, the flaw of *Vedha* <note>astrological piercing or interference</note> must be avoided in all regions. Some say the flaw of *Yuti* <note>conjunction</note> is specific to the Gauda region <note>Central/Eastern Bengal</note> and *Yamitri* to the Yamuna region. ॥ 66 ॥

**(Indravajra Meter)**
The flaws known as *Ekargala* (also called *Kharjura*), *Upagraha*, *Latta*, *Pata*, *Yamitri*, *Kartari* <note>being "scissored" between two malefic planets</note>, and the rising or setting of planets (to be discussed later)—all these flaws in a marriage horoscope are destroyed if the Sun and the Moon are powerful. Just as the darkness of night is destroyed when the Sun rises. ॥ 67 ॥

**(Upajati Meter)**
Add the number of the Moon’s constellation (*Nakshatra*) to the Sun’s constellation and divide by 27. If the remainder is 1, 4, 6, 10, 11, 15, 18, 19, or 20, these are known as the *Dasha Yoga* <note>The Ten Combinations</note>. ॥ 68 ॥

**(Shardulavikridita Meter)**
The results of the *Dasha Yoga* are as follows:
If the remainder is 1, there is a flaw of Wind (*Vayu*); if 4, fear of clouds/storms; if 6, fear of the King (government); if 10, fear of thieves; if 11, death; if 15, disease; if 18, fear of lightning/thunderbolts; if 19, quarrel; and if 20, loss of wealth. 

To find the specific affected constellation: if the remainder is an even number, divide it by two and add 14. The resulting number is the constellation counting from Ashwini. For example, if the even remainder is 10, half is 5; add 14 to get 19, which is the constellation *Mula*. If the remainder is an odd number, add 1 and then divide by two. For example, if the odd remainder is 15, add 1 to get 16; half is 8, which is the constellation *Pushya*. Draw fourteen horizontal lines... <note>The text begins to describe a diagrammatic method for calculating interference between the Sun and Moon</note>. ॥ 69 ॥

<summary>This page details specific astrological flaws (doshas) like Yamitri and Latta, noting that some are only applicable in certain geographic regions of India, and explains how a strong Sun and Moon can nullify these negative influences.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological calculations (Prakarana 6), specifically focusing on the "Bana" (arrows/afflictions) and their impact on various life events.</meta>

(95)
### Chapter 6: Including the Commentary

To create the chart, one must write all the <term>Nakshatras</term><note>Lunar mansions or star clusters used in Vedic astrology</note> including <term>Abhijit</term><note>The 28th intercalary star cluster</note> on both sides of the chart lines in the prescribed order. Place the planets in their respective Nakshatras. If any planet is on the same line as the Moon, it is considered an "aspect-piercing" (<term>Vedha</term>). This is inauspicious. However, according to other texts, if Jupiter, the Lord of the Ascendant, or Venus are strong and positioned in the <term>Kendras</term><note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses of a birth chart</note>, the flaw of the "Ten Doshas" does not apply. || 69 ||

> (Shalini Meter) Add the Ascendant (<term>Lagna</term>) to the elapsed lunar days (<term>Tithis</term>) of the bright fortnight plus 8; divide by 9. If the remainders are 8, 3, 4, 6, or 1, they correspond to the "Arrows" of Disease, Fire, King, Thief, and Death respectively. This method is famous in the South (<term>Dakshinatya</term>). || 70 ||

If you add the Ascendant to the lunar date of the bright fortnight plus 8 and divide by 9: if the remainder is 8, it is the Arrow of Disease; 3 is Fire; 4 is King; 6 is Thief; and 1 is the Arrow of Death. This calculation is famous in the Southern and Maharashtra regions, but not elsewhere. || 70 ||

> (Malini Meter) Add 6, 3, 1, 8, and 4 respectively to the degrees elapsed since the Sun's entry into a sign (<term>Sankranti</term>). Divide by 9; if the remainder is 5, the respective Arrow is present. If the sum of these remainders multiplied by 9 and divided by 5 leaves a remainder of 5, it is "With a Splinter" (<term>Sashalya</term>). || 71 ||

<margin>9</margin>
<margin>5</margin> Add 6, 3, 1, 8, and 4 separately to the degrees elapsed from the Sun's <term>Nirayana Sankranti</term><note>The moment the Sun enters a new zodiac sign according to the sidereal system</note>. Divide each by 9. If the remainder is 5, identify the Arrow: 6 leads to Disease, 3 to Fire, 1 to King, 8 to Thief, and 4 to Death. This is known as the "Wooden Splinter Arrow." To check for an "Iron Splinter," add all the remainders from the previous step, multiply by 9, and divide by 5. If the remainder is 5, it is <term>Sashalya</term> (with a splinter/thorn). Any other remainder is without a splinter. The "With Splinter" condition is considered extremely censurable and auspicious. || 71 ||

> (Shardulavikridita Meter) Thief and Disease arrows at night; King in the day; Fire always; Death at the two junctions (twilight). On Saturday the King; Wednesday Death; Tuesday Fire and Thief; Sunday Disease. These must be avoided by the wise in rituals, housing, service, travel, and marriage. || 72 ||

The Arrows of Thief and Disease are forbidden at night. The King arrow is forbidden during the day. The Fire arrow is forbidden always (day and night). The Death arrow is forbidden during the <term>Sandhyas</term><note>The twilight periods of dawn and dusk</note>. Furthermore, the King arrow is avoided on Saturdays, Death on Wednesdays, Fire and Thief on Tuesdays, and Disease on Sundays. Specifically: avoid the Disease arrow during <term>Vratabandha</term><note>The thread ceremony or religious vows</note>, the Fire arrow during house construction or protection, the King arrow during royal service, the Thief arrow during travel, and the Death arrow during marriage. || 72 ||

<summary>This page details the calculation of the five "Banas" (Arrows or astrological afflictions)—Disease, Fire, King, Thief, and Death—and specifies the times and activities (like marriage or travel) during which they must be avoided.</summary>
<keywords>Bana, Nakshatra, Vedha, Sankranti, Sashalya, Vratabandha, Muhurta, Indian Astrology</keywords>

<header>The Jewel of Electional Astrology (Muhurta Chintamani)</header>

### The Arrow Diagram (Bana Chakra)

| | Ar. | Ta. | Ge. | Ca. | Le. | Vi. | Li. | Sc. | Sa. | Cp. | Aq. | Pi. | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Disease Arrow | 7 17 26 | 7 16 25 | 6 15 24 | 5 14 23 | 4 13 22 | 3 12 21 30 | 3 12 21 30 | 2 11 20 29 | 9 18 27 | 8 17 26 | 6 15 24 | 7 16 25 | Forbidden dates in Disease Arrow. |
| Fire Arrow | 2 20 29 | 11 19 28 | 9 18 27 | 8 17 26 | 7 16 25 | 7 15 24 | 5 14 23 | 4 13 22 | 3 12 21 | 2 11 20 | 10 19 28 | 9 18 27 | Forbidden dates in Fire Arrow. |
| King Arrow | 4 22 1 | 3 21 30 | 2 20 29 | 10 19 28 | 9 18 27 | 8 17 26 | 7 16 25 | 6 15 24 | 5 14 23 | 4 13 22 | 3 12 21 30 | 2 11 20 29 | Forbidden dates in King Arrow. |
| Thief Arrow | 6 15 24 | 5 14 23 | 4 13 22 | 3 12 21 30 | 2 11 20 29 | 10 19 28 | 9 18 27 | 8 17 26 | 7 16 25 | 6 15 24 | 5 14 23 | 4 13 22 | Forbidden dates in Thief Arrow. |
| Death Arrow | 1 19 28 | 9 18 27 | 8 17 26 | 7 16 25 | 6 15 24 | 5 14 23 | 4 13 22 | 3 12 21 30 | 2 11 20 29 | 1 19 28 | 9 18 27 | 8 17 26 | Forbidden dates in Death Arrow. |

<note>The table above lists the <term>Tithis</term> (lunar days) that are considered inauspicious or "forbidden" when the Sun is in a particular <term>Rashi</term> (Zodiac sign). The abbreviations in the top row represent the signs from Aries to Pisces.</note>

->(Upajati Meter)<-
Planets cast their gaze upon the third and tenth houses, the trine (fifth and ninth), the square (fourth and eighth), and the seventh house with increasing quarters of strength. Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars, along with the others, have full sight in a specific sequence. || 73 ||

<note>original: "ड्याशंत्रिकोणं चतुरस्रमस्तंपश्यन्ति..."</note>
Planets look from their current sign at the 3rd and 10th houses with 1 quarter of sight; at the 9th and 5th with 2 quarters; at the 4th and 8th with 3 quarters; and at the 7th with a full 4 quarters of sight <note>This refers to <term>Drishti</term> or planetary aspects</note>. Specifically, Saturn has full sight on the 3rd and 10th; Jupiter on the 9th and 5th; Mars on the 4th and 8th; and all other planets have full sight on the 7th house. || 73 ||

->(Shikharini Meter)<-
When the lord of the <term>Navamsha</term> <note>the ninth divisional chart</note> of the Ascendant aspects or is joined with that Navamsha or the Ascendant itself, it creates great auspicious results for the groom. If the lord of the seventh house of the Navamsha aspects the Navamsha, the seventh house, the Ascendant, or the seventh house of the Ascendant, it is auspicious for the bride; otherwise, it is known to be inauspicious. || 74 ||

->(Muhurta Pradipa)<-
If the lord of the Navamsha aspects the Navamsha, or the lord of the Ascendant aspects the Ascendant house, or they aspect each other, it is auspicious for the groom. If the lord of the seventh house of the Navamsha aspects the Navamsha or the seventh house, or the lord of the Ascendant aspects the seventh house, or they aspect each other, it is auspicious for the bride. || 75 ||

->(Malini Meter)<-
If the lord of the Navamsha is a natural benefic or a friend and aspects the Navamsha or the Ascendant, it is seen in the scriptures as auspicious for the one performing the marriage. If the friend of the lord of the seventh house of the Navamsha, along with a benefic planet, aspects the Navamsha, the seventh house, the Ascendant, or the seventh house of the Ascendant, it brings happiness to the bride. || 76 ||

<summary>This page details the 'Bana Chakra' table for identifying forbidden lunar days and explains the rules for planetary aspects (Drishti), specifically how they influence the auspiciousness of a marriage for the bride and groom.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological timing for marriage, specifically focusing on the "Purification of Rising and Setting" (Udayasta Shuddhi) and the prohibited periods during planetary transits.</meta>

(97)
### Chapter 6: Including the Vernacular Commentary

**Purification of the Rising and Setting:** If the Lord of the Ascendant <note>Lagnēśa: the planet ruling the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth or the time of the wedding</note> or the Lord of the Navamsha <note>Amshēśa: the ruler of the specific 1/9th division of the sign</note> aspects <note>dṛṣṭi: planetary "sight" or influence</note> the Ascendant or its division, or is positioned within them, it brings very auspicious results for the groom. For example, if in an Aries Ascendant, Mercury (the ruler of the Gemini Navamsha) is in Libra and aspects Gemini—this is the method for considering the purification of the Ascendant. 

If the ruler of the seventh Navamsha from a strong Navamsha aspects or is joined with the seventh house or its division; and if the Lord of the Seventh House <note>Saptamēśa</note> or the ruler of the seventh house's division aspects or is joined with the seventh house or its division, it grants extremely auspicious results for the bride. If the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Navamsha do not aspect the Ascendant or its division, it is inauspicious (even indicating death) for the groom. Similarly, if the Lord of the Seventh House or its divisional ruler does not aspect or join the seventh house or its division, it is harmful for the bride. || 74 ||

If the Lord of the Ascendant aspects the Ascendant, and the Lord of the Navamsha aspects the division, or if they mutually aspect the Ascendant, it is auspicious for the groom. Likewise, if the Lord of the Seventh House aspects the seventh house, and the ruler of the seventh division aspects that division (or if the divisional ruler aspects the house and the house ruler aspects the division), it is auspicious for the bride. Furthermore, if the Lord of the Seventh House aspects both the Ascendant and the seventh house, or if the ruler of the seventh house's division aspects both, it is also auspicious for the bride. If the Lord of the Ascendant or the Lord of the Ascendant's Navamsha aspects both the seventh house and the Ascendant, it is auspicious for both the bride and groom. || 75 ||

If a natural benefic planet <note>benefic: planets like Jupiter, Venus, or a strong Moon that bring positive influence</note> becomes a friend to the Lord of the Ascendant's Navamsha and aspects its own division or the Ascendant, the marriage will yield auspicious fruits such as sons and grandsons. If a friendly benefic planet of the ruler of the seventh house's division aspects the seventh house and the Ascendant's Navamsha, or aspects the seventh house from the Ascendant, the bride will receive the auspicious blessings (children and grandchildren) described in the scriptures. One should know that the aforementioned combinations and aspects involving malefic planets <note>malefic: planets like Mars or Saturn that bring challenges</note> result in inauspiciousness everywhere. || 76 ||

->(Manjubhashini Meter)<-
> One should avoid the days of the Equinoxes and Solstices, including the days immediately preceding and following them. During other planetary transits <note>Sankranti: the moment the Sun enters a new zodiac sign</note>, a wise person should avoid sixteen *ghatis* <note>Ghati: a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> before and after the event when performing auspicious rites. || 77 ||

Regarding the Equinoxes (Aries and Libra transits) and Solstices (Cancer and Capricorn transits): the day before, the day after, and the day of the transit itself—all three days—are forbidden for auspicious ceremonies like marriage or the thread ceremony <note>Vratabandha</note>. For the other eight transits, a total of 32 *ghatis* (16 before and 16 after the transit moment) are forbidden. || 77 ||

->(Anushtubh Meter)<-
> The forbidden periods for the transits of the Sun and other planets are, in order: thirty-three, two, nine, nine, eighty-eight, nine, and one hundred and sixty *nadis* <note>Nadi: synonymous with Ghati (24 minutes)</note>. The Sun's transit is considered the most censurable. || 78 ||

The forbidden times for planetary transits in auspicious works are as follows: 33 *ghatis* for the Sun (covering the period before and after), 2 for the Moon, 9 for Mars, 9 for Mercury, 88 for Jupiter, 9 for Venus, and 160 *ghatis* for Saturn. A detailed discussion on this has already been provided in the chapter on Transits. || 78 ||

<summary>This page details the astrological requirements for a successful marriage by analyzing the "purification" of the Ascendant and Seventh House through planetary aspects. It also lists specific prohibited time periods (ghatis) surrounding the transits of the Sun and other planets.</summary>
<keywords>Udayasta Shuddhi, Ascendant, Navamsha, Seventh House, planetary aspects, Sankranti, Equinox, Solstice, forbidden times, Hindu astrology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological timing (Muhurta) for marriages, specifically focusing on the physical defects attributed to certain zodiac signs at specific times of day.</meta>

# Muhurta Chintamani
<page-num>98</page-num>

(Upajati Meter) Sagittarius, Libra, and Scorpio are "deaf" during the day; Capricorn and Sagittarius are "deaf" at night. Leo, Aries, and Taurus are "blind" during the day.
Virgo, Gemini, and Cancer are "blind" at night. Aquarius is "lame" during the day, and Pisces is "lame" at night. || 79 ||
<note>The text classifies the zodiac signs (Lagnas) into categories of physical disability based on the time of day. 7 and 8 (Libra and Scorpio) are deaf in the day; 10 and 9 (Capricorn and Sagittarius) are deaf at night. 5, 1, and 2 (Leo, Aries, Taurus) are blind in the day; 6, 3, and 4 (Virgo, Gemini, Cancer) are blind at night. 11 (Aquarius) is lame in the day and 12 (Pisces) is lame at night.</note>

->(Vasantamalika Meter)<-

Sagittarius, Libra, and Scorpio are deaf in the afternoon. Gemini, Cancer, and Virgo are blind at night.
Leo, Taurus, and Aries are blind during the day. Capricorn, Aquarius, and Pisces are "hunchbacked" during the twilight hours. || 80 ||
<note>This verse provides a slight variation: Signs 9, 7, and 8 are deaf in the last third of the day. Signs 3, 4, and 6 are blind at night. Signs 5, 2, and 1 are blind during the day. Signs 10, 11, and 12 are considered hunchbacked during the morning and evening twilights.</note>

->(Praharshini Meter)<-

Poverty results from a "deaf" ascendant; widowhood from a "day-blind" ascendant; death of the child from a "night-blind" ascendant. In a "lame" ascendant, all wealth is destroyed.
However, if these signs are aspected by their own Lord or by Jupiter, there is no fault. || 81 ||
<note>The "aspect" refers to a planet "looking" at a house in a birth chart, which can cancel out negative influences.</note>
Performing a marriage in a deaf sign leads to poverty; in a day-blind sign to widowhood; in a night-blind sign to the death of offspring; and in a lame sign to the loss of all wealth. If the Lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagnesha: the planet ruling the rising sign</term> or Jupiter aspects these, the fault is removed. Furthermore, there is an exception:
> "Lame, blind, and one-eyed signs, as well as Month-Empty signs <note>original: 'Masashunya'</note>, are to be avoided in the regions of Gauda <note>modern Bengal</note> and Malwa <note>central India</note>; they are not prohibited in other lands." || 81 ||

->(Chitrapada Meter)<-

If the Navamsha <term>Navamsha: a 1/9th division of a zodiac sign used for precision</term> of Sagittarius, Libra, Virgo, Gemini, or Pisces is rising,
then the bride will surely be a virtuous and faithful wife. || 82 ||
If the marriage ascendant falls in the 9th, 7th, 8th, 6th, 3rd, or 12th divisions, the married girl will certainly remain devoted to her husband. || 82 ||

->(Shrichhanda Meter)<-

One should not marry in the final Navamsha of a sign, unless it is a Vargottama.
In a movable ascendant, do not use a movable Navamsha if the Moon is in Libra or Capricorn. || 83 ||
In the rising sign, the last ninth-part (e.g., Sagittarius Navamsha in Aries, or Capricorn in Taurus) should not be taken. However, if it is <term>Vargottama: when the sign and its 1/9th division are the same</term>, it is acceptable. For signs 3, 9, 12, and 10, the last Navamsha is indeed Vargottama. Also, if the Moon is in Libra or Capricorn, do not take a movable Navamsha within a movable sign; if the Moon is in any other sign, a movable Navamsha in a movable sign is acceptable. || 83 ||

<summary>This page details the "defective" zodiac signs (deaf, blind, lame) and their negative impacts on marriage, while providing specific astronomical exceptions and regional exemptions where these rules do not apply.</summary>
<keywords>Muhurta, Astrology, Marriage Timing, Navamsha, Vargottama, Zodiac Defects, Indian Geography, Jupiter's Aspect</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological timing for marriages, specifically focusing on the placement of planets in the birth chart at the moment of the wedding (Muhurta). It follows previous sections detailing auspicious and inauspicious planetary alignments.</meta>

# Chapter 60 (Page 99)

### (Upajati Meter) Saturn in the twelfth, Mars in the tenth, Venus in the third, and the Moon or malefic planets in the ascendant are not praised.
### The Lord of the Ascendant, the Sun, and the Moon in the sixth or eighth; and the Lord of the Ascendant, Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, the Moon, and Mars in the eighth house are not auspicious. || 84 ||

If at the time of the wedding <term>Lagna</term> (the ascendant or rising sign), Saturn is in the 12th house, Mars is in the 10th, Venus is in the 3rd, or the Moon and malefic planets are in the 1st house, it is unfavorable. Furthermore, it is inauspicious if the Lord of the Ascendant, Venus, or the Moon are in the 6th or 8th houses, or if the Lord of the Ascendant, Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, the Moon, or Mars are in the 8th house. 

The specific results are as follows: Saturn in the 12th makes the bride a "drinker of intoxicants" <note>original: "madyapa"</note>; Mars in the 10th makes her a "meat-eater" or a sorceress <note>original: "shakini"</note>; Venus in the 3rd makes her "devoted to her brother-in-law" <note>likely implying an illicit or socially complex relationship</note>. Other placements result in widowhood or death. The effects of benefic planets in the 7th house have already been discussed in the section on <term>Yamitri</term> (the seventh house influence). || 84 ||

### (Vasantatilaka Meter) The Sun, Ketu, Rahu, and Saturn are auspicious in the 3rd, 11th, and 6th houses. 
### Mars is auspicious in the 3rd, 11th, and 6th; the Moon in the 2nd, 3rd, and 11th. 
### Mercury and Jupiter are auspicious in all houses except the 7th, 12th, and 8th. 
### Venus is auspicious except in the 8th, 3rd, 7th, 6th, and 12th houses. || 85 ||

The Sun, Ketu, Rahu, and Saturn gain <term>Vinshopaka</term> strength (a specific measure of planetary dignity) when placed in the 3rd, 11th, or 6th houses from the wedding ascendant. Similarly, Mars is strong in the 3rd, 11th, and 6th; the Moon in the 2nd, 3rd, and 11th. Mercury and Jupiter are favorable in any house except the 7th, 12th, or 8th. Venus is considered auspicious when it avoids the 8th, 3rd, 7th, 6th, and 12th houses. || 85 ||

### (Shardulavikridita Meter) If the malefic planets creating a "Scissor's Defect" are in debilitation or combust, 
### there is no defect for Venus in the enemy's or debilitated house; the defect of the 6th house also vanishes. 
### If Mars is in an enemy's house or debilitated, the 8th house defect of Mars does not occur. 
### If the Moon is in a debilitated sign or a debilitated Navamsha, the defects of the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses do not apply. || 86 ||

If the malefic planets causing <term>Kartari Dosha</term> (the "Scissor's Defect," where a house is hemmed in by malefics) are themselves in a state of debilitation or are combust (too close to the sun), and a benefic planet sits between them, then the defect does not apply to the 1st or 7th houses. Furthermore, if Venus is in the 6th house but is in a debilitated or enemy sign, the usual flaw of "Venus in the 6th" is cancelled. If Mars is in the 8th house but is debilitated or combust, the "Mars in the 8th" flaw is negated. Finally, if the Moon is in the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses but is in its sign of debilitation or a debilitated <term>Navamsha</term> (ninth-part division), those specific house defects are also cancelled. || 86 ||

### (Vasantatilaka Meter) Defects of the year, solstice, season, lunar day, month, and the five "burnt" lunar days, 
### as well as "blind" or "deaf" signs, are all destroyed when Jupiter, Venus, or Mercury 
### are in the center or corner houses; likewise, the defects of malefic Moon-signs and Navamshas are removed. || 87 ||

Various astrological flaws related to the year, the sun's course (<term>Ayana</term>), the season, the lunar date (<term>Tithi</term>), the month, and the "burnt" dates, as well as "blind" or "deaf" zodiac signs <note>technical terms for signs with diminished power in certain directions</note>, are all neutralized if Jupiter, Venus, or Mercury are positioned in the <term>Kendra</term> (1st, 4th, 7th, 10th houses) or <term>Trikona</term> (1st, 5th, 9th houses). Similarly, the flaws arising from a malefic Moon or an unfavorable <term>Navamsha</term> are also dispelled by these strong benefic placements. || 87 ||

<summary>This page details the specific planetary positions (Muhurta) that are considered auspicious or inauspicious for a wedding ceremony, including various "cancellation" rules where a negative placement is neutralized by other factors. It emphasizes the importance of Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury in central houses to overcome minor astrological defects.</summary>
<keywords>Marriage Astrology, Muhurta, Lagna, Kartari Dosha, Navamsha, Planetary Strength, Hindu Wedding Rituals, Saturn, Mars, Venus</keywords>

--- Page 117 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological timing (Muhurta), specifically focusing on how strong planetary placements can neutralize various "doshas" or astrological flaws in a birth or event chart.</meta>

# Muhurta Chintamani
<page-num>100</page-num>

The flaws related to the year (1), the sun's solstice (2), the season (3), the lunar day (4), the month (5), the lunar mansion (6), the lunar fortnight (7), and flaws of the ascendant such as "burnt" lunar days, or "blind, one-eyed, deaf, or crippled" ascendants, as well as unseasonable rain (8)—all these flaws fail to produce evil results if Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus are positioned strongly in the **cardinal houses** <note>Kendra: houses 1, 4, 7, and 10</note> or **trinal houses** <note>Kona: houses 5 and 9</note>. Likewise, the flaw of a Moon joined with malefic planets or a malefic **divisional chart** <note>Navamsha</note> is also destroyed. || 87 ||

->( Shalini Meter )<-

> If Jupiter is in a cardinal or trinal house, or the Sun is in the eleventh house, or the Moon is in the ascendant or in its best divisional sign (Vargottama), all flaws are destroyed. If the Moon is in the eleventh house, the many flaws of an inauspicious moment are likewise removed. || 88 ||

If Jupiter—and by implication Mercury and Venus—are in the cardinal (1, 4, 7, 10) or trinal (5, 9) houses, or if the Sun is in the 11th house, or if they are in the **improving houses** <note>Upachaya: 3, 6, 10, 11</note> from the ascendant, or in the **best divisional sign** <note>Vargottama: when a planet occupies the same zodiac sign in both the main chart and the 9th divisional chart</note>, then all the aforementioned flaws are destroyed. Similarly, if the Moon is in the 11th house, the "Durmuhurta" <note>original: "ravāvaryametyādi" - referring to specific inauspicious timings</note> and the flaws of malefic divisional signs are also destroyed. || 88 ||

->( Shikharini Meter )<-

> A benefic planet in a trine or a cardinal house (excluding the seventh) destroys a hundred flaws; Venus destroys twice that, and the Preceptor of the Gods (Jupiter) destroys a hundred thousand. If the Lord of the Ascendant or the Lord of the Rising Navamsha is in the eleventh or a cardinal house, they quell the multitude of flaws like fire quells a pile of cotton. || 89 ||

If Mercury is in a cardinal (1, 4, 10—excluding the 7th) or trinal (9, 5) house relative to the wedding ascendant, it removes one hundred flaws. If Venus is so placed, it removes two hundred, and Jupiter removes one hundred thousand <note>original: laksha</note> flaws. Furthermore, if the **Lord of the Ascendant** <note>Lagnesha</note> or the Lord of the Ascendant's divisional sign is in the 11th house or a cardinal house (1, 4, 7, 10), they burn the "pile" of flaws just as fire burns a heap of cotton in an instant. || 89 ||

->( Anushtup Meter )<-

> Two points each for Mercury and Venus; five for the Moon; three and a half for the Sun; three for Jupiter; and one and a half each for Saturn, Rahu, and Ketu—these are the Vishopaka strengths. || 90 ||

In the auspicious positions for planets mentioned previously (such as in the verse beginning "Tryayastashtashatsu"), the planets gain **Vishopaka strength** <note>A traditional 20-point scale of planetary strength</note> as follows: Mercury (2), Venus (2), Moon (5), Sun (3.5), Jupiter (3), Saturn (1.5), Rahu (1.5), and Ketu (1.5). This strength is only gained when the planet is in its specific auspicious station, not otherwise. If all planets were in their respective best positions, they would achieve a full strength of 20. The sum of these digits is 21.5, but since Rahu and Ketu are always opposite each other, one will be in an auspicious spot while the other is in an inauspicious one, thus subtracting 1.5 from the total. || 90 ||

<summary>This page details how strong placements of benefic planets (Jupiter, Venus, Mercury) and the Sun can neutralize hundreds of thousands of astrological flaws (doshas) in a chart. It also introduces the "Vishopaka" numerical system for calculating the specific strength of planets based on their house positions.</summary>
<keywords>Muhurta, planetary strength, Vishopaka, Jupiter, Venus, cardinal houses, trinal houses, astrological flaws, neutralization, Hindu astrology</keywords>

# Chapter 6

> (Upajati Meter) The mother-in-law is represented by Venus; the father-in-law by the Sun; the body by the Ascendant; the husband by the Lord of the Seventh House; and the mind by the Moon. Having carefully evaluated the strength of these, the practitioner of the <term>Tantras</term><note>In this context, "Tantras" refers to the technical systems of astrology or the practitioners (Jyotishis) themselves.</note> should declare the happiness to be gained from the marriage. || 91 ||

For the bride, the mother-in-law is Venus; the father-in-law is the Sun; the physical body is the Ascendant (<term>Lagna</term>); the husband is the Lord of the Seventh House; and the mind is the Moon. The astrologer, after observing the strength of these factors and considering their auspicious or inauspicious nature, determines the success of the marriage. For example, if the aforementioned planets are in their sign of debilitation (<term>Neecha</term>), in an enemy's sign, combust (<term>Asta</term>), or located in the "Triple Houses of Misfortune" (<term>Trik</term>)<note>The 6th, 8th, and 12th houses of a birth chart.</note>, they should be known as inauspicious. If they are in their sign of exaltation, their own sign, or other auspicious houses, they should be known as auspicious. || 91 ||

---

> (Mattamayura Meter) If the "clasping of the hand" (marriage) occurs during the dark fortnight of the month, or on a Saturday, Tuesday, or Sunday, or under forbidden constellations, it results in the attainment of children, long life, wealth, and love for those of mixed castes. This is the established rule for them. || 92 ||

If a marriage takes place during the dark fortnight (<term>Krishna Paksha</term>), on a Saturday, Tuesday, or Sunday, or under constellations not usually prescribed for weddings, it brings wealth, sons, long life, and profit to those of "mixed" status. For these groups, the standard auspicious timings are reversed. <insert>The term "mixed" (<term>Sankirna</term>) refers to those of mixed-caste heritage and the <term>Chandalas</term><note>Historically marginalized groups outside the four-fold varna system.</note>.</insert> || 92 ||

---

> (Anushtubh Meter) In the Gandharva and other forms of marriage, counting from the Sun's constellation to the Moon's constellation: 4, 2, 3, 1, 1, 4, 6, 3, 1, 3. These are respectively inauspicious and auspicious. || 93 ||

In <term>Gandharva</term> marriages<note>A marriage based on mutual consent and love without formal parental rituals.</note> and similar types, one only looks at the cycle starting from the Sun's constellation to the Moon's constellation: 4 (bad), 2 (good), 3 (bad), 1 (good), 1 (bad), 4 (good), 6 (bad), 3 (good), 1 (bad), 3 (good). There is also a variant reading ("Tripadyam") which refers to the "Three-Ghari Cycle" used for writing the marriage scroll. || 93 ||

---

> (Prithvi Meter) Having observed the strength of the Moon, one should perform the grinding of grains, the cleaning of rice, the decoration of the house and courtyard, and the construction of the altar and the marriage pavilion under the constellations prescribed for marriage. However, one should not perform these three, nine, or six days prior to the wedding day. || 94 ||

**Preparatory Marriage Rituals:** The grinding of wheat and pulses, the cleaning of rice, the placement of the auspicious water-pot (<term>Mangala-kalasha</term>), the tidying of the house and courtyard, the preparation of ornaments and jewelry, the construction of the altar (<term>Vedi</term>) and pavilion (<term>Mandapa</term>), and the hanging of festive garlands—all these should begin only after checking the strength of the Moon and choosing a marriage-prescribed constellation. However, these should not be started exactly 3, 6, or 9 days before the wedding. This same consideration applies to the sowing of barley sprouts (<term>Javankura</term>), the ceremonial oil-anointing (<term>Tailalapana</term>), and the worship of the auspicious Ganesha. || 94 ||

<summary>This page details the astrological significators for family members in a marriage and provides specific timing exceptions for mixed-caste unions and Gandharva marriages. It also outlines the auspicious timing for preparatory wedding rituals like building the pavilion and cleaning the house.</summary>
<keywords>astrological significators, Venus, Sun, mixed castes, Gandharva marriage, wedding preparations, Mandapa, Moon strength, Muhurta</keywords>

--- Page 119 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for wedding rituals and timing from the Muhurta Chintamani, a classic 16th-century Sanskrit treatise on electional astrology.</meta>

### (Shalini Meter)
> The altar, known as the *Vedi*, should be one cubit <note>original: "hasta"; the length from the elbow to the tip of the middle finger</note> in height and four cubits square on all sides. It should be constructed to the left side of the courtyard. On an even-numbered day—excluding the sixth—or on the fifth or seventh day, the ceremonial dismantling of the pavilion <note>original: "Mandapa-udvasana"</note> is considered auspicious. || 95 ||

In the front-left area of the house's courtyard, an altar should be built one cubit high and four cubits square (4x4) using the bride's hand-span as a measure, complete with pillars and steps. A pavilion of 16 cubits is best. In cases of limited space, 12, 10, or 8 cubits are mentioned as secondary options. After the wedding, the dismantling of the pavilion should occur on an even day (avoiding the 6th) or on the 5th or 7th day. || 95 ||

---

### (Indravajra Meter)
> When the Sun is in Virgo, Leo, or Aquarius, the first pillar should be placed in the Northeast; in Sagittarius, Capricorn, or Scorpio, in the Northwest; in Pisces, Aries, or Aquarius <note>The commentary clarifies the signs</note>, in the Southwest; and in Taurus, Gemini, or Cancer, in the Southeast. || 96 ||

The placement of the first pillar <note>original: "Stambha-niveshana"</note> in the pavilion follows the Sun's position: if the Sun is in the 6th, 5th, or 7th signs <note>Virgo, Leo, Libra</note>, start in the Northeast; if in the 8th, 9th, or 10th <note>Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn</note>, in the Northwest; if in the 12th, 1st, or 11th <note>Pisces, Aries, Aquarius</note>, in the Southwest; and if in the 2nd, 3rd, or 4th <note>Taurus, Gemini, Cancer</note>, in the Southeast. This same rule applies to the commencement of building a house. || 96 ||

---

### (Mandakranta Meter)
> There is no need to consider the lunar mansion, the lunar day, the half-day, or the rising sign; nor is there concern for the day of the week, the divisional charts, or the specific hour. Neither the planetary combinations, the eighth house, nor the seventh-house flaws matter. The "Dust-of-the-Cows" hour <note>original: "Godhuli"; twilight</note> is declared by the sages to be auspicious for all tasks. || 97 ||

In the *Godhuli* <note>The time when cows return home, kicking up dust</note> period, there is no requirement to check the constellation, lunar date, or astrological period. One need not analyze the rising sign, the day, the specific divisions, or the hour. Malefic combinations, the purity of the eighth house, or flaws in the seventh house do not apply. Sages have declared this time auspicious for all endeavors. || 97 ||

---

### (Jaladharamala Meter)
> When the sun becomes a solid orb in the winter; when it is half-set in the summer; and when it has completely set during the rainy season—this is how the "Dust-of-the-Cows" hour should be applied to all auspicious works. || 98 ||

Defining the time of *Godhuli*: In the four months of the Winter season <note>starting from Margashirsha, approx. Nov-Dec</note>, it is when the sun appears as a rayless orb due to mist. In the four months of the Hot season <note>starting from Chaitra, approx. March-April</note>, it is when the sun's disk is half-submerged. In the four months of the Rainy season <note>starting from Shravana, approx. July-August</note>, it is when the sun has fully set. This time grants success in all virtuous deeds. || 98 ||

---

### (Vaishvadevi Meter)
> If the Sun, Jupiter, or Saturn are in the rising sign at sunset, or if the Moon is in the eighth or sixth house, it leads to the destruction of the bride. Furthermore, if Mars is in the eighth or twelfth house, the groom is lost. However, the Moon in the second or third house brings happiness. || 99 ||

<summary>This page details the architectural dimensions of the wedding altar and pavilion, the astrological timing for setting the first pillar, and the supreme auspiciousness of the "Godhuli" (twilight) hour, which overrides typical astrological flaws.</summary>
<keywords>Vedi construction, Mandapa, Godhuli Muhurta, pillar placement, wedding astrology, seasonal twilight definitions, Hindu calendar</keywords>

--- Page 120 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological timing and planetary calculations from the previous section, focusing specifically on the 'Godhuli' (twilight) period and solar motion.</meta>

<page-num>103</page-num>
<header>With Commentary - Chapter 6</header>

There is another type of Godhuli <note>literally "cow-dust time," the auspicious period of twilight</note>: on Thursdays, Godhuli occurs at sunset; however, one must avoid the half-ghati <note>approximately 12 minutes</note> before sunset as it constitutes an 'Ardhyama' <note>an inauspicious period</note>. On Saturdays, the ceremony should take place while the sun is still visible, because at the exact moment of sunset, the 'Kulik' <note>a specific inauspicious astrological timing</note> begins. Furthermore, if the Moon is in the 8th, 6th, or 1st house from the evening Ascendant <term>Lagna: the rising sign on the eastern horizon</term>, or in the Ascendant itself, it leads to the destruction of the bride. If Mars is in the 1st, 7th, or 8th house, it leads to the destruction of the groom. Such major defects must be avoided even during Godhuli. The purity of the Panchang <note>the five limbs of the Hindu calendar</note> is also a primary consideration. If the Moon is in the 11th, 2nd, or 3rd house, it grants happiness; if it is in these positions during Godhuli, it is especially auspicious. || 99 ||

> (Indravajra Meter) The motion of the sun starting from Aries is: 58, 57, 57, 57, 58, 59, 60, 61, 61, 61, 60, 59. || 100 ||

The approximate daily motion of the Sun in the signs starting from Aries is as follows: Aries 58, Taurus 57, Gemini 57, Cancer 57, Leo 58, Virgo 59, Libra 60, Scorpio 61, Sagittarius 61, Capricorn 61, Aquarius 60, and Pisces 59 <note>these values are in 'palas' or minutes of arc per day</note>. || 100 ||

> (Anushtubh Meter) Multiply the elapsed days since the Sun's transit by the motion, divide by sixty; the resulting degrees and minutes should be added to the elapsed sign to find the true Sun. || 101 ||

Multiply the elapsed time (ghatis and palas) since the Sun's transit <term>Sankranti: the moment the sun enters a new zodiac sign</term> by the aforementioned approximate motion and divide by 60. The resulting degrees and minutes should be placed in the position of the Sun's traversed sign to determine the 'True Sun' <term>Spashta Bhaskara: the mathematically corrected position of the sun</term>. || 101 ||

> (Anushtubh Meter) Multiply the Navamshas prior to the desired degree of the Ascendant by ten; divide by three to find the degrees for calculating the divisional charts. || 102 ||

To determine the Navamsha <note>the 1/9th divisional chart</note> of the desired Ascendant, take the number of completed Navamshas, multiply by 10, and divide by 3. The resulting figure provides the elapsed portion of the Ascendant, which is then used to calculate the 'Shadvarga' <note>the six types of divisional charts used to assess planetary strength</note>. || 102 ||

> (Shalini Meter) Multiply the remaining and elapsed degrees of the Sun and the Ascendant (with precession) by the local rising times, divide by thirty; the sum of the remaining and elapsed times, including the intervening signs, divided by sixty, gives the desired time. || 103 ||

Multiply the remaining degrees of the Sun's sign (including precession <term>Sayana: the tropical longitude including the precession of the equinoxes</term>) by the local rising time (in palas) and divide by 30; the result is the 'Bhogya' <note>remaining</note> time in palas. Similarly, multiply the elapsed degrees to find the 'Bhukta' <note>elapsed</note> time in palas. The sum of these times, along with the rising times of the signs between the Sun and the Ascendant...

--- Page 121 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for calculating auspicious timings (Muhurta) for marriage, specifically focusing on the mathematical determination of the "desired time" (Ishtakala) and a comprehensive list of astrological afflictions to be avoided.</meta>

Page 104
# The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments

By adding the proportional minutes of the intervening zodiac signs and dividing by 60, the desired time <note>Ishtaghati: the time elapsed since sunrise</note> is determined from the start of sunrise. || 103 ||

-> (Shalivikridita Meter) <-
**If the tropical ascendant and the Sun are in the same zodiac sign, the difference between them should be multiplied by the local rising period and divided by thirty. This gives the desired time. If the ascendant is less than the Sun, it indicates the remaining part of the night; for night calculations, add six signs to the Sun.** || 104 ||

If the tropical <note>Sayana: including the precession of the equinoxes</note> ascendant and the Sun are in the same sign, multiply the degrees between them by the local rising period <note>Lagnakhanda</note> and divide by 30 to find the desired time from sunrise. For nighttime calculations, add 6 signs <note>180 degrees</note> to the position and proceed as described. || 104 ||

-> (Shardulavikridita Meter) <-
**One should avoid portents, Great Calamities, "burnt" lunar days, and evil yogas; likewise the setting of Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus, the decrease or increase of lunar days, the three types of "knot-ends," the Vishti karana, the day of solar transit, and the setting of the lords of the ascendant or its division. One must also avoid the lords of the ascendant or the Moon being in the 6th or 8th houses, and the divisional charts of malefic planets.** || 105 ||

Portents—such as those involving malefic planets—Great Calamities <note>Mahapata: specific astronomical latitudinal crossings</note>, "burnt" lunar days <note>Daghdha Tithi: days considered scorched or unlucky</note>, evil combinations, the setting <note>combustion by the sun</note> of the Moon, Jupiter, or Venus, the loss or gain of a lunar day, the three types of "knot-ends" <note>Gandanta: junctions between specific signs/asterisms</note>, the Bhadra <note>Vishti</note> period, the day the sun enters a new sign, the combustion of the lord of the ascendant or the lord of the Navamsha <note>ninth-part division</note>, the placement of these lords or the Moon in the 6th or 8th houses <note>houses of debt, disease, and death</note>, and the six-fold divisions <note>Shadvarga</note> of malefic planets: all these previously mentioned faults must be avoided in marriage. || 105 ||

-> (Shardulavikridita Meter) <-
**The Moon with a malefic, the rising degree of a malefic, the purity of rising and setting, the "fierce weapon" degrees, the Khajura and Ten-fold Yogas, the seventh-house affliction, the "kick" of planets, and the piercing of stars; the "arrow" affliction, minor planets, the "malefic scissors" yoga, and evil combinations arising from lunar days, stars, or yogas; the half-hour of the "Kuli" and other day-based faults.** || 106 ||
**The star occupied by a malefic, the star of an eclipse, the star a malefic is approaching, the three types of portents, the star struck by a comet, and the star rising at twilight; likewise, stars involved in planetary "piercing" or "warfare"—all these must be abandoned in marriage and auspicious works, as well as faults of the ascendant caused by the planets.** || 107 ||

**Thus ends the Section on Marriage in the "Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments" composed by Rama, son of the astrologer Ananta.** || 6 ||

Likewise, the Moon joined with a malefic, a malefic in the ascendant or its division, the purity of the rising and setting, the "fierce" degrees, the "fierce weapon" <note>Chandayudha</note>, the Khajura, the Ten-fold Yogas, the seventh-house <note>Jamitra</note> affliction, the "kick" <note>Latta</note> of planets, the piercing <note>Vedha</note> of stars, the "arrow" <note>Bana</note> affliction, minor planets <note>Upagraha</note>, the "malefic scissors" <note>Papakartari: being hemmed in by malefics</note>, and the faults of lunar days and weekdays...

Chapter 7: Including the Vernacular Commentary

Regarding the liquid <note>original: "drava"</note> (referring to the Sun's portion, etc.), the "Death Yoga" arising from the combination of lunar mansions and weekdays, and the "Hand-Sun-Fifth" <note>original: "hastārka-pañcamī," a specific astrological combination</note> and other such harmful yogas; the Half-Yama <note>a period of 1.5 hours</note>, the Kulika period, and other such flaws; a lunar mansion occupied by a malefic planet, one just vacated by a malefic, or one about to be entered by a malefic; the lunar mansion of an eclipse, the three types of portents <note>celestial, atmospheric, and terrestrial</note>, the lunar mansion where a comet appears, the 14th mansion from the Sun, a mansion pierced by a planet, or a mansion where planetary war occurs—all these flaws, as well as flaws caused by planetary combustion, are forbidden in marriage and all auspicious ceremonies. || 106 || 107 ||

->Thus ends the Chapter on Marriage, being the sixth chapter of the Muhurta Chintamani with the commentary by Mahidara.<-

# Now begins the Chapter on the Bride’s Entrance.

(In the Upajati meter) The bride’s entrance into her husband's home should occur on even days—the 2nd, 4th, 6th, 8th, 10th, 12th, 14th, or 16th—within sixteen days of the wedding. It is also auspicious on the 5th, 7th, or 9th days. Beyond that, it should be done in odd months or odd years. After five years have passed, there is no restriction on even or odd; one may perform it whenever desired according to a favorable almanac. || 1 ||

The act of the married girl entering the groom's house after the wedding is called "Vadhu-Pravesha" <term>Bride's Entrance: the formal homecoming of the bride</term>. If it occurs within 16 days of the wedding, it should be on the even days: 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 14, or 16. It may also be done on the 5th, 9th, or 7th days. If it does not happen within 16 days, it should be done in odd months or odd years on the aforementioned days. If five years pass, the rule of even/odd no longer applies; it can be done at will on an auspicious day. || 1 ||

(In the Anushtubh meter) The Bride’s Entrance is auspicious during the Fixed, Swift, and Soft lunar mansions <note>categories of Nakshatras</note>, as well as Shravana, Dhanishtha, Mula, Magha, and Swati. It should avoid the "Empty" <note>Rikta</note> dates (4th, 9th, 14th) and should not fall on a Tuesday, Sunday, or Wednesday. || 2 ||

The Bride’s Entrance is auspicious in the Fixed <note>Rohini, Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Swift <note>Ashwini, Pushya, Hasta</note>, and Soft <note>Mrigashira, Chitra, Anuradha, Revati</note> mansions, and the specific stars Shravana, Dhanishtha, Mula, Magha, and Swati. It is favorable on dates other than the 4th, 9th, and 14th, and on days other than Tuesday, Sunday, and Wednesday. || 2 ||

(In the Indravajra meter) If the bride stays in her husband's house during the first month of Jyeshtha after the wedding, it brings death to the husband's elder brother; in an intercalary month, it harms the husband; in Ashadha, the mother-in-law; in Pausha, the father-in-law; and in a decayed month, herself. If she stays in her father's house during the first Chaitra after the wedding, it brings death to her father. || 3 ||

->Thus ends the Chapter on the Bride’s Entrance in the Muhurta Chintamani.<-

If, following the wedding, she stays in her husband's house during the first month of Jyeshtha <note>May-June</note>, the flaw of death falls upon the husband's elder brother. In an extra month <note>Adhika Masa</note>, it harms the husband; in Ashadha <note>June-July</note>, the mother-in-law; in Pausha <note>December-January</note>, the father-in-law; in a decayed month <note>Kshaya Masa</note>, it harms her own body. Furthermore, if she stays in her father's house during the first Chaitra <note>March-April</note> after the wedding, the father may die. || 3 ||

->Thus ends the seventh chapter, the Chapter on the Bride’s Entrance,<-
->in the Muhurta Chintamani with the commentary by Mahidara.<-

# Now, the Chapter on the Second Entry (Dviragamana)

<note>Dviragamana refers to the ceremony where a bride, after having returned to her father's home following the wedding, travels back to her husband's house for the second time.</note>

(Panchachamara Meter) One should perform the Second Entry in odd-numbered years <note>1st, 3rd, or 5th year after marriage</note> when the Sun is in Aquarius, Scorpio, or Aries, provided there is purification of Jupiter and the Sun. It should be done on the day of a benefic planet, under the ascendants of Gemini, Pisces, Virgo, Libra, or Taurus, and during the Light, Fixed, Movable, Sharp, or Soft constellations. ॥ 1 ॥

If the bride, after her initial entry into the husband's home, goes back to her father's house and then returns again to the husband's house, it is called the **Second Entry**. This should be done in the 1st, 3rd, or 5th year; when the Sun is in the 11th, 1st, or 8th signs <note>Aquarius, Aries, Scorpio</note>; when the Sun and Jupiter are purified as prescribed for marriage; on the weekdays of auspicious planets; in the 3rd, 12th, 6th, 7th, or 2nd ascendants; and under the Light, Fixed, Movable, Mula, or Soft constellations <term>Nakshatras</term>. ॥ 1 ॥

(Praharshini Meter) If Venus <note>original: "Daityejya" - the teacher of the demons</note> is facing the traveler or is to the right, then children, pregnant women, and newlywed brides should not travel. If a child travels, they meet with calamity; if a newlywed travels, she becomes barren; and if a pregnant woman travels, she loses her fetus. ॥ 2 ॥

In the initial journey to the husband's house during the wedding, the scriptural purifications regarding the position of Venus (Shukra) are not considered. However, because it is necessary to observe them for the Second Entry, the purification of Venus is stated: if Venus is in front or to the right, a child, a pregnant woman, or a newlywed should not travel. In this state of "Opposing Venus" <term>Prati-shukra</term>, if a child travels, they may face death; a pregnant woman may lose her pregnancy; and a newlywed may become barren. > "Even if Jupiter or Venus are set <note>invisible in the sky</note>, or if Jupiter is in Leo, a girl may enter her husband's house on the days surrounding the Festival of Lights <note>Deepavali</note>." ॥ 1 ॥
Some authorities hold the opinion that even if Jupiter or Venus are set, or if Venus is in an unfavorable position, or if Jupiter is in Leo—if there is an urgent necessity—the new bride may enter the husband's house two days before or two days after the **Festival of Lights** (Deepavali) without fault. ॥ 2 ॥

(Praharshini Meter) In the event of entering a city due to disturbances, during the marriage ceremony itself, during pilgrimages to deities or holy rivers, or when being oppressed by a king, the "Opposing Venus" does not cause any harm for the entry of a new bride. ॥ 3 ॥

In cases of foreign invasion, rebellion, or oppression by a king causing one to enter their own city; or when moving elsewhere due to famine or distress; or during the marriage ritual; or during journeys to forts, temples, or holy sites; or if being expelled by a king—the fault of Venus being in front or to the right does not apply to the bride's entry into her husband's home. ॥ 3 ॥

### Chapter 9: Including the Vernacular Commentary
<meta>Digitized by eGangotri; watermark at bottom mentions CC-0 Shri Krishna Museum, Kurukshetra.</meta>

> (Indravajra meter) If the development of the breasts or the first menses occurs while the girl is still in her father's house, then the "Adverse Venus" <note>original: "Prati-Shukra"; a period when Venus is in a position that traditionally forbids travel or new beginnings</note> does not cause a fault. Likewise, there is no fault for those born in the lineages of the sages Bhrigu, Angira, Vatsa, Vashistha, Kashyapa, Atri, and Bharadwaja. || 4 ||

-> Thus ends the section on the Second Entry of the Bride in the *Muhurta Chintamani*. || 8 || <-

If the girl's breasts develop or her first menstruation occurs in her father's house, the "Adverse Venus" fault does not apply. By implication, the requirements for the purity of the Sun and Jupiter are also waived. Furthermore, for those belonging to the lineages (clans) of the sages Bhrigu, Angira, Vatsa, Vashistha, Kashyapa, Atri, and Bharadwaja—that is, for people of these specific *Gotras* <note>lineage or clan names</note>—the fault of "Adverse Venus" never applies. || 4 ||

-> Thus ends the eighth chapter in the vernacular commentary by Mahidhara on the *Muhurta Chintamani*. || 8 || <-

# Now, the Section on Establishing the Sacred Fire.

The establishment of the sacred fire <term>Agnyadhana: the ritual of placing or kindling the sacrificial fires</term> refers to the maintenance of the fire for performing Vedic and Smriti rituals. Some perform this at the time of marriage, while others do so when they begin living separately from their father or brother.

> (Vasantatilaka meter) The ritual for the daily fire sacrifice should take place when the Sun is in its northern course, under the Mixed, Constant, Revati, Mrigashirsha, Jyeshtha, or Pushya constellations. It should not be on "Empty" lunar days, nor when the Moon, Mars, Jupiter, or Venus are in their signs of debilitation, are combust, defeated in planetary war, or situated in the houses of their enemies. || 1 ||

The Auspicious Time for Establishing the Fire: One should establish the daily fire sacrifice when the Sun is in its northern course <note>Uttarayana: the six-month period between the winter and summer solstices</note> and under the constellations <note>Nakshatras</note> categorized as Mixed, Constant, or specifically Revati, Mrigashirsha, Jyeshtha, and Pushya. However, the "Empty" <note>Rikta</note> lunar days (the 4th, 9th, and 14th) should not be chosen. Furthermore, the ritual is auspicious only if the Moon, Mars, Jupiter, and Venus are not in their signs of debilitation, are not hidden by the Sun's glare (combust), are not defeated in planetary "warfare," and are not in the signs of their enemies. || 1 ||

> (Vasantatilaka meter) One should not choose the rising signs or sub-charts of Cancer, Capricorn, Pisces, or Aquarius; nor should the Moon be in the first house. The Sun, Moon, Jupiter, and Mars should be in the trines (5th or 9th houses), or in the 1st, 4th, 7th, 10th, 6th, 3rd, or 11th houses. Other planets should be in the 3rd, 11th, 6th, or 10th, provided the eighth house is pure. || 2 ||

One should not use Cancer, Capricorn, Pisces, or Aquarius as the rising sign <note>Lagna</note> or the ninth-division <note>Navamsha</note> chart. The Moon should not be in the first house. The ritual is auspicious if the Sun, Moon, Jupiter, and Mars are in the trines (5th and 9th houses) or in the 1st, 4th, 7th, 10th, 6th, 3rd, or 11th houses. Other planets (Mercury, Venus, Saturn, Rahu, and Ketu) should be in the 3rd, 11th, 6th, or 10th houses. Additionally, the eighth house from the rising sign must be empty of planets, and the chosen rising sign should not be the same as one's birth sign or the eighth sign from it. || 2 ||

> (Anushtubh meter) If Jupiter is in Sagittarius in the first house, or if Mars is in Aries in the tenth house, or if the Moon or Sun are in the 6th, 3rd, or 11th houses, the fire established will surely bring victory. || 3 ||

-> Thus ends the section on Establishing the Sacred Fire in the illustrious *Muhurta Chintamani*. || 9 || <-

If the aforementioned Ascendant <note>Lagna</note> is occupied by Jupiter, it brings wealth (1); or if Mars is in Aries in the tenth or seventh house (2); or if the Moon is in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses (3); or if the Sun is in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses (4)—if any of these combinations <note>Yogas</note> are present, the performer of the Agnihotra <note>the daily fire ritual</note> will certainly become one who performs the great Jyotistoma and other major sacrifices. || 3 ||

# Now begins the Chapter on Royal Consecration (Rajabhisheka).

*(Indravajra meter)* The royal consecration is auspicious during the Sun's northern course <note>Uttarayana; the period between the winter and summer solstices</note> when Jupiter, the Moon, and Venus are risen and endowed with strength. It should be done when Mars, the Sun, the Lord of the Ascendant, the Lord of the Tenth House, and the Lord of the Birth Sign are strong. It should not be performed in the month of Chaitra, on "Empty" <note>Rikta; the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days</note> dates, on Tuesdays, at night, or during an intercalary month <note>Malimlucha/Adhik Maas</note>. || 1 ||

**Auspicious Timing for Coronation** — Royal consecration is auspicious during the Sun's northern course when Jupiter, the Moon, and Venus have risen and are powerful; when Mars, the Sun, the Lord of the Ascendant, and the Lord of the Tenth House are strong; and when the Lord of the Birth Ascendant is also momentarily powerful. One must avoid the month of Chaitra <note>approx. March-April</note>, the "Empty" lunar dates (4th, 9th, 14th), Tuesdays, and the intercalary month. Coronation should also not be performed at night. || 1 ||

*(Indravajra meter)* In the constellations <note>Nakshatras</note> of Jyeshtha, Shravana, the "Swift" <note>Kshipra</note>, "Soft" <note>Mridu</note>, or "Fixed" <note>Dhruva</note> groups; in the "Head-rising" <note>Shirshodaya</note> signs (3, 5, 6, 7, 8, 11); or in the "Increasing" <note>Upachaya</note> houses (3, 6, 10, 11) from the birth ascendant or birth sign, provided they are occupied or aspected by benefic planets. Benefic planets should be in the Centers (1, 4, 7, 10), Trines (5, 9), or the 11th, 2nd, and 3rd houses. Malefic planets should be in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses. In such a moment, the royal consecration is auspicious. || 2 ||

*(Indravajra meter)* If malefic planets are in the Ascendant, there is disease; in the eighth, death; in the fifth, distress to sons; in the 2nd or 12th, poverty. If in the tenth, one becomes idle/powerless; in the 4th or 7th, one falls from their position. If the Moon is in the 6th, 8th, or 12th, it also brings death. All benefics should be in the Centers. || 3 ||

If a malefic planet <note>Papagraha</note> is in the Ascendant, the King will suffer disease; if in the 8th house, death; if in the 5th house, trouble regarding his children; if in the 2nd or 12th houses, loss of wealth (poverty). If in the 10th house, he becomes inactive or loses his initiative; if in the 4th or 7th houses, he will be deposed from his position of power. The Moon in the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses also results in death. || 3 ||

<summary>This page concludes the rules for fire rituals and details the astrological requirements for a king's coronation, emphasizing the strength of specific planets and the avoidance of inauspicious times like the month of Chaitra.</summary>
