<page-num>132</page-num>
# ->The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments<-

The dietary cravings <note>original: "dohada" — specifically refers to ritual offerings or foods consumed to satisfy the "desires" of a particular time or deity</note> of the Lunar Mansions <note>original: "Nakshatras"</note> are described as follows: 

In the 1st mansion (Ashwini), black gram and rice; in the 2nd, sesame and rice; 3rd, black gram; 4th, cow's curd; 5th, cow's clarified butter <note>ghee</note>; 6th, cow's milk; 7th, venison; 8th, blood of a female deer; 9th, rice pudding <note>original: "payasa"</note>; 10th, meat of the Indian Roller bird; 11th, deer meat; 12th, rabbit meat; 13th, sixty-day ripened rice; 14th, foxtail millet; 15th, sweets fried in ghee; 16th, partridge meat; 17th, excellent fruits; 18th, tortoise meat; 19th, myna bird meat; 20th, monitor lizard meat; 21st, Shala fish meat; 22nd, ritual offerings such as mung beans; 23rd, khichdi <note>rice and lentil porridge</note>; 24th, mung bean porridge; 25th, barley flour; 26th, rice with fish; 27th, various sweets; and in the 28th <note>Abhijit</note>, curd-rice. By eating, seeing, smelling, or touching these items according to the customs of one's country and family, the defects associated with those Lunar Mansions are neutralized. || 81 || 82 ||

<note>The following verse is in the Anushtup meter</note>
> Clarified butter, sesame-rice, fish, and milk respectively;
> One should consume these cravings and then depart toward the directions starting with the East. || 83 ||

**The cravings of the directions:** When traveling toward the East, consume clarified butter; toward the South, rice mixed with sesame; toward the West, fish; and toward the North, milk. By doing so, no evil results occur. || 83 ||

> (Anushtup meter) Shikhariṇi <note>a yogurt-based sweet</note>, rice pudding, sour gruel, boiled milk, curd,
> Raw milk, and sesame-rice; these should be eaten as the cravings of the days. || 84 ||

**The cravings of the days of the week:** On Sunday, eat Shikhariṇi; Monday, rice pudding; Tuesday, sour gruel; Wednesday, boiled milk; Thursday, curd; Friday, raw milk; and Saturday, sesame-rice before departing. || 84 ||

<note>The following verse is in the Vasantatilaka meter</note>
> From the start of the lunar fortnight: Calotropis leaf, rice-water, clarified butter, gruel, ritual grains,
> Gold-infused water, fried cakes, citron fruit, water, cow urine, barley,
> Rice pudding, jaggery, blood-red rice, and mung beans; eat these before traveling. || 85 ||

**The cravings of the lunar dates <note>original: "Tithi"</note>:** On the 1st day of the fortnight, eat a leaf of the Calotropis plant; 2nd, rice-wash water; 3rd, clarified butter; 4th, sour gruel; 5th, ritual grains <note>original: "Havishyanna"</note>; 6th, water in which gold was dipped; 7th, fried sweet cakes; 8th, citron fruit; 9th, water; 10th, cow urine; 11th, barley; 12th, rice pudding; 13th, jaggery; 14th, red-colored rice; and on the 15th, mung bean porridge. One should travel after consuming these. || 85 ||

# Chapter 11: With Commentary in the Common Tongue

->(Written in the *Praharshini* meter)<-
First, lifting the right foot and walking thirty-two steps in the intended direction, the King should then mount his vehicle. Before departing, he should present a gift of sesame, clarified butter, gold, and a copper vessel to the chief astrologer <note>original: "ganakavaraya"</note> and then proceed. || 86 ||

At the time of travel, the King should first lift his right foot and walk 32 steps on foot. Then he should mount the vehicle described hereafter. At that time, he should give a gift of sesame, ghee, gold, and a copper vessel to the astrologer, and after giving as much additional dakshina <note>honorarium or sacrificial fee</note> as he is able, he should depart. || 86 ||

->(Written in the *Anushtup* meter)<-
The King should go toward the East by elephant, toward the South by chariot, toward the West by horse, and toward the North by palanquin <note>literally: "by men," referring to a litter or palanquin carried by people</note>. || 87 ||

When traveling to the East, use an elephant; to the South, a chariot; to the West, a horse; and to the North, travel in a vehicle carried by men. || 87 ||

->(Written in the *Padakulaka* meter)<-
From the temple of the deity, or the house of the Guru, or from one's own home, or from the house of the principal wife—having consumed the *havishya* <note>sacred food fit for an offering</note> and with the permission of the Brahmins, seeing and hearing auspicious things, he should go forth. || 88 ||

At the time of travel, whether from the room where the deity is worshipped, or the teacher's residence, or his own sleeping quarters, or (in the case of many wives) from the house of the Chief Queen <note>original: "Patrani"</note>, he should consume the remains of the sacrificial offering. With the permission of the Brahmin—who gives the command to take the first step with the mantra "Idam Vishnur..." <note>A Vedic verse invoking Lord Vishnu's three steps</note>—and while seeing and hearing auspicious sounds, songs, instruments, and water-pots, he should depart. || 88 ||

->(Written in the *Praharshini* meter)<-
If there is a delay in departure due to business, then a Brahmin's sacred thread, a weapon, honey, or a pure fruit should be moved forward immediately; or whatever is dear to the heart of each person. || 89 ||

If, during the auspicious moment <term>Muhurta: a specific period of time calculated by astrology as favorable</term> for travel, there is a delay due to some work, then a Brahmin should send his sacred thread forward, a Kshatriya <note>warrior caste</note> his weapon, a Vaishya <note>merchant caste</note> honey, and a Shudra <note>laborer caste</note> a fruit like a coconut. This is called <term>Prasthana: the ritual of sending a personal object ahead to "start" a journey at the correct astrological time even if the person is delayed</term>. Alternatively, everyone may send forward an object that is dear to their own mind. || 89 ||

->(Written in the *Mandakranta* meter)<-
Garga says moving from one house to another constitutes the departure; Bhrigu says moving from one boundary to another; Bharadwaj says the distance of an arrow-shot is a departure; and Vashistha says the departure for the purpose of travel should be outside the city. || 90 ||

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the astrological timing and rituals for setting out on a journey (Prasthana), following the rules for auspicious moments (Muhurta).</meta>

# The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments
<page-num>134</page-num>
->The Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments<-

Regarding the distance for setting out on a journey (*Prasthana* <note>The formal act of leaving one's home at an auspicious time to begin a journey</note>), the sage Garga says that even moving to a neighboring house constitutes a journey. Bhrigu states it is moving from one's own boundary to another boundary. Bharadwaja says it is as far as an arrow flies when shot with great force. Vashistha says the journey begins only after leaving the city. All these views are correct. || 90 ||

**(*Vasantatilaka* meter) Some say the distance for setting out is five hundred bows, others say two hundred, and still others say only ten. Whoever has set out from their house and traveled toward their destination must do so with great effort. || 91 ||**

Some define the distance for setting out as 500 bows (2000 cubits <note>original: "haat"</note>) from one's home; some say 200 bows (800 cubits); and some say only 10 bows. Therefore, one should consider "near" or "far" based on the necessity of the task. It is best to personally set out in the direction of the destination; if that is not possible, one may send a symbolic object (*Vastu-prasthana* <note>Sending a personal item like a staff or garment ahead if the person cannot leave at the exact auspicious moment</note>). On the first day of travel, one should go a short distance, a bit further on the second, and thus gradually increase the distance for a long journey. || 91 ||

**(*Sragdhara* meter) After setting out, a king should not remain in one place for more than ten days; a local chieftain for seven nights; and other men for five nights. Beyond this, even if the day is auspicious, he must depart. Furthermore, for seven nights prior to the day of departure, one who desires victory over enemies should refrain from sexual intercourse. || 92 ||**

If a King, after formally setting out, remains in one place for ten days, he must calculate the auspicious moment for the journey all over again. Similarly, a *Mandala* <note>A ruler of a small group of villages</note> should not stay for 7 days, and others (such as Brahmins) should not stay for 5 days. If by chance this period expires, one must return home and set out again at a new auspicious moment. One should abstain from intimacy with a woman for seven nights before the day of travel. If, due to the woman's menstrual cycle or other reasons, one cannot abstain for seven nights, then at the very least, one must abstain on the day immediately preceding the journey. || 92 ||

**(*Shalini* meter) Milk should be avoided for three nights prior; shaving should be avoided for five nights prior. On the day of the journey itself, the King must certainly and diligently avoid honey, oil, and induced vomiting. || 93 ||**

A king seeking a successful journey should not drink milk for three nights before departure. He should not undergo *Kshaura* <note>The ritual shaving of the head or beard</note> for five nights prior. On the actual day of the journey, he must strictly avoid eating honey, applying oil to the body, or taking emetic medicines to induce vomiting for internal cleansing. These things are certainly forbidden. || 93 ||

<meta>This page continues the discussion of omens and astrological considerations for travel (Yatra), specifically focusing on dietary prohibitions, unseasonable weather, and auspicious sights before departure.</meta>

<page-num>135</page-num>
->### Chapter 11: Including the Vernacular Commentary<-

(**Giti Meter**) If one departs after eating oil, jaggery, alkaline substances, or cooked meat; or if one departs after insulting a woman or a Brahmin, they will return ill or meet with death. || 94 ||

If a traveler eats items cooked in oil, jaggery, or—contrary to the cravings of a pregnant woman—milk and cooked meat before going, they will return sick. If they depart after insulting a woman or a Brahmin through verbal abuse or physical striking, they will face death on this journey. <note>Death is considered to be of eight types in this context, not just the physical leaving of the body.</note> || 94 ||

(**Santamaala Meter**) If rain occurs during the four months starting from Pausha <note>Mid-December to mid-January</note> until Chaitra <note>Mid-March to mid-April</note>, it is considered unseasonable rain. As long as the earth is not marked by the footprints of animals and humans, there is no harm; otherwise, it is a bad omen. || 95 ||

If it rains during the four months from Pausha to Chaitra, it is called unseasonable rain in regions other than the mountains. Alternatively, if rain occurs in any region during a time when it is not the rainy season, it is a flaw for travel. However, if the rain is so light that it does not allow the footprints of animals or humans to be imprinted in the soil, there is no fault. When the rain is heavy enough to leave footprints, it is a bad omen. || 95 ||

(**Atishakkari and Gatha Meters**) In light rain, the fault is small; in heavy rain, the fault is great. If there is the sound of thunderclouds or rain, the King should have golden images of the Sun and Moon disks made and give them to Brahmins. In the event of bad omens, he should give gold with clarified butter <term>Ghee</term> and then depart at his will. || 96 ||

If unseasonable rain is light, the omen is only slightly bad; if the rain is heavy, the fault is great and one should not travel. If rain occurs after the journey has already begun, there is no fault. However, rain accompanied by thunder is a bad omen for a traveling King. Despite these faults, if the journey is mandatory, one should donate golden images of the Sun and Moon to Brahmins. If bad omens occur at the time of departure, one should donate gold and Ghee and then proceed as desired. || 96 ||

(**<term>Shardulavikridita Meter</term>** <note>A complex Sanskrit poetic meter</note>) A Brahmin, a horse, an elephant, fruit, food, milk, curd, a cow, white mustard, a lotus, clothing, a prostitute, musical instruments, a peacock, a blue jay, a mongoose, a single tethered animal, meat, pleasant speech, flowers, sugarcane, a full water-pot, an umbrella, earth, a maiden, jewels, a turban, a white bull, wine, a woman with her son, a blazing fire, a mirror, collyrium <note>eye-liner</note>, washed clothes, a washerman, fish, Ghee, a throne, a corpse (without wailing), a flag, honey, a goat, weapons, and <term>Gorochana</term> <note>a bright yellow pigment found in a cow's gall bladder, used for tilaks</note>. || 97 ||

->**The Bharadvaja bird, royal vehicles, the sound of the Vedas, auspicious songs, and the elephant goad**<-
->**If seen, these grant good results at the time of departure; as does an empty water pot if it is behind the traveler. || 98 ||**<-

At the time of departure, the following are auspicious if seen in front of the traveler: many Brahmins <note>priestly class</note>, a horse, an elephant (that is not in a state of rut), fruit, grain, milk, curd, a cow, a woman, white mustard, lotus flowers, clean clothes, a courtesan, musical instruments like the mridangam drum, a peacock, a blue jay, a mongoose, a four-footed animal (such as a bull) tied with a rope, meat, pleasant speech, flowers, sugarcane, a full water pot, an umbrella, wet clay, a young maiden, gems, a turban, a white bull, wine, a woman with her son, a bright fire, a mirror, kohl <note>eye liner</note>, washed clothes, a washerman, fish, ghee <note>clarified butter</note>, a throne, a corpse (provided no one is wailing over it), a flag, honey, a goat, weapons such as a bow, *gorochana* <note>a bright yellow pigment found in a cow's gall bladder, used for tilaks</note>, the Bharadvaja bird <note>skylark</note>, a palanquin, the sound of Vedic chanting, auspicious songs, and an elephant goad. These items are favorable when facing the traveler. Additionally, an empty pot is auspicious if it is behind the traveler, or if it is being carried by someone going to fill it. || 97 || 98 ||

(In the Shardulavikridita meter) **A barren woman, leather, husks, bones, a snake, salt, charcoal, fuel wood, a eunuch, feces,**
->**Oil, a madman, fat, medicine, one with matted hair, a monk, grass, a sick person,**<-
->**A naked person, one anointed with oil, one with disheveled hair, a fallen person, one with a physical deformity, one distressed by hunger, blood,**<-
->**Menstrual blood, a lizard, one's own house on fire, cats fighting, a sneeze,**<-

->**A shaven-headed person, wet clothes, harsh words, the blind, the deaf, and a menstruating woman—these are not auspicious when seen. || 99 || 100 ||**<-

A barren woman, leather, grain husks, bones, a snake, salt, smokeless fire <note>embers</note>, firewood, a eunuch, excrement, oil, a madman, animal fat, medicine, an enemy, one with matted hair, a wandering ascetic, grass, a physician, a naked person, one smeared with oil, one with loose hair, one intoxicated or unconscious, a person with a missing limb, a hungry person, blood, menstrual discharge, a chameleon or lizard, seeing one's own house on fire, cats fighting, or a sneeze. Also: one wearing ochre robes, jaggery <note>unrefined sugar</note>, buttermilk, mud, a widow, a hunchback, a quarrel within the family, the accidental dropping of clothes or an umbrella, buffaloes fighting, black grains (such as black gram), cotton, vomiting, the braying of a donkey on the right side, intense anger, a pregnant woman, a person with a shaven head, one wearing wet clothes, wicked speech, a blind person, a deaf person, or a menstruating woman. These things are inauspicious for a traveler at the time of departure. || 99 || 100 ||

# Chapter 11: Including the Vernacular Commentary

-> (In the *Shardulavikridita* meter) <-
Speaking the names of the monitor lizard, the hedgehog <note>original: "jahaka"</note>, the boar, the snake, and the rabbit is auspicious at the start of a journey. However, hearing their sounds or seeing them is not. For monkeys and bears, the opposite is true <note>their names are inauspicious, but seeing them is lucky</note>. In matters of crossing rivers, fearful situations, entering a home, battle, or searching for lost items, these omens are reversed. When going to see a King, the omens described for travel should be considered auspicious or inauspicious accordingly. || 101 ||

To speak the names of the monitor lizard, the hedgehog (a creature that curls its body), the boar, the snake, or the rabbit during the time of departure is auspicious, but hearing their cries or seeing them is inauspicious. Conversely, for monkeys and owls, speaking their names is inauspicious, while seeing them or hearing their sounds is auspicious. In the context of crossing rivers, situations involving fear, fleeing, entering a house, war, or searching for lost property, the aforementioned lucky omens become unlucky and the unlucky ones become lucky. For the purpose of visiting a King, the travel omens should be applied in the same way. || 101 ||

-> (In the *Anushtubh* meter) <-
The cuckoo, the lizard, the *potaki* bird, the sow, the *rala* bird, the spotted owl, the shrew, and the jackal—these male-designated creatures are auspicious when appearing on the left side. || 102 ||

The cuckoo, the female pigeon, the sow, the myna bird, the *ratna* bird, the *bhairavi* (spotted owl), the lizard, the shrew, the jackal, and male-designated birds like the pigeon, wagtail, partridge, and swan are auspicious on the left side during travel. || 102 ||

-> (In the *Anushtubh* meter) <-
The *chikkara* deer, the *pikka* bird, the vulture <note>original: "bhasa"</note>, the *shrikantha* bird, the monkey, and the *ruru* deer—these female-designated creatures, along with the crow, the bear, and the dog, are auspicious on the right side. || 103 ||

The *chikkara* deer, the *pikka* bird, the vulture, the monkey, the *shrikantha* bird, and the *ruru* deer (which are female-designated), as well as the crow, the bear, and the dog, are auspicious on the right side of the traveler. || 103 ||

-> (In the *Anushtubh* meter) <-
Deer and birds that move from left to right are excellent during a journey. An odd number of deer is very blessed, as is the sound of a donkey on the left. || 104 ||

If deer and birds circle clockwise <note>circumambulation</note> during a journey, it is auspicious. Seeing an odd number of deer is extremely lucky. Similarly, the braying of a donkey on the left side is considered a blessing. || 104 ||

-> (In the *Anushtubh* meter) <-
Upon the first bad omen, one should stop for eleven breaths; upon the second, sixteen breaths; after a third, one should not go at all. || 105 ||

If a first bad omen occurs during a journey, one should wait for the duration of 11 breaths <term>Prana: the time taken for one inhalation and exhalation</term> before proceeding after seeing a good omen. If a second bad omen occurs, wait for 16 breaths. If a third occurs, one should cancel the trip entirely. || 105 ||

<summary>This page details specific animal omens for travelers, distinguishing between the luck brought by hearing an animal's name versus seeing it, and specifying which sides (left or right) different animals should appear on for success. It also provides a protocol for pausing or canceling a journey based on the frequency of bad omens.</summary>

--- Page 155 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion on Muhurta (electional astrology), specifically concluding the section on travel (Yatra) and the auspicious timing for returning home or entering a new residence.</meta>

<page-num>138</page-num>
->### Muhurta Chintamani (The Jewel of Auspicious Moments)<-

<note>Original Sanskrit verse in Jagati-Upajati meter</note>
Upon returning from a journey, entry into the home is auspicious under "soft" <term>Mridu (gentle constellations)</term> and "fixed" <term>Dhruva (steady constellations)</term> stars. If one enters under "swift" <term>Kshipra</term> or "mobile" <term>Chara</term> stars, they will soon have to depart again. Entry under Vishakha, Krittika, "dreadful" <term>Daruna</term>, or "terrible" <term>Ugra</term> stars leads to the destruction of the wife, the home, the sons, or oneself, respectively. || 106 ||

There are four types of "Entry" <term>Pravesha</term>: bringing home a new bride, returning after a long absence, entering a brand new house, and entering after a short trip. Here, the focus is on returning after a journey. This should be done in soft or fixed stars. If done in swift or mobile stars, one travels again. Specifically: in Vishakha, the wife is lost; in Krittika, the house is destroyed (by fire, etc.); in "dreadful" stars, the son is lost; and in "terrible" stars, the traveler themselves meets destruction. || 106 ||

<note>Original Sanskrit verse in Manjubhashini meter</note>
The solar transit stars, the "needle" of the direction <term>Dik-Shula</term>, the day of the week, the position of Venus and Mercury in front of the traveler, the "skull" of the direction <term>Dik-Kapala</term>, the retrograde motion of Venus, the "iron bar" <term>Parigha</term> and "staff" <term>Danda</term> yogas, the wife’s menstrual cycle, impurity <term>Ashaucha (mourning periods)</term>, and festivals—all these must be considered. || 107 ||

The "dead fortnight" <term>Mruta-paksha (the dark half of the month)</term>, the "empty" <term>Rikta</term> lunar days (4th, 9th, 14th), the 12th, 6th, 15th, and 30th lunar days, and the days of Saturn, the Sun, and Mars are unfavorable. Likewise, if the Moon is to the left or behind the traveler, or if the "Great Obstacle" <term>Mahadala</term> stars or the five stars starting from Dhanishtha <term>Panchaka</term> or the Abhijit moment are to the south, it is forbidden. || 108 ||

<note>Original Sanskrit verse in Sragdhara meter</note>
An ascendant <term>Lagna</term> that is the 8th house from one's birth sign or birth ascendant, or the 6th house from an enemy's sign, or the lords of these houses; the signs of Aquarius and Pisces or their sub-divisions <term>Navamsha</term>; signs that rise from the back <term>Prishthodaya</term>; Saturn in the 10th house; Venus in the 7th house; or retrograde planets in the cardinal houses <term>Kendras</term> or on their respective weekdays—all these faults mentioned in the marriage section are also avoided here. || 109 ||

### Thus ends the Chapter on Travel in the Illustrious Muhurta Chintamani. || 11 ||

**Summary of Faults (The Collection of Defects):**
The "Solar Needle" <term>Ayana-Shula</term> occurs when the sun is in the northern transit, etc. There are two types of "Month Needles" <term>Masa-Shula</term>: 1. Based on groups of three signs starting from Taurus (affecting the East, etc.), and 2. The "Skull-Thorn" <term>Kapala-Kantaka</term> based on groups of three months starting from Kartika. 

Other prohibitions include:
*   **Directional Faults:** Traveling toward the "Needle" <term>Shula</term> based on the star or day; traveling while Venus or Mercury are directly in front.
*   **Planetary States:** Venus being retrograde, set, or in its sign of debilitation.
*   **Yogas:** The "Iron Bar" <term>Parigha</term> and "Staff" <term>Danda</term> formations in the four directions.
*   **Personal/Social:** The wife's menses, ritual impurity (death in the family), or ongoing festivals.
*   **Lunar/Calendar:** The dark fortnight, "empty" days (4, 9, 14), the 12th, 6th, 15th (Full Moon), and 30th (New Moon) lunar days.
*   **Days:** Saturday, Sunday, and Tuesday.
*   **Moon Position:** The Moon being to the left or behind the traveler is generally avoided <note>though sometimes considered good for returning, here it is listed among faults for departure</note>.
*   **Specific Stars:** The "Great Obstacle" <term>Mahadala</term> stars, the five stars from Dhanishtha <term>Panchaka</term>, and the Abhijit Muhurta when traveling South.
*   **Astrological Houses:** Using an ascendant that is the 8th from the birth sign, or the 6th from an enemy's sign.
*   **Specific Placements:** Aquarius or Pisces ascendants/sub-divisions; signs that rise "back-first"; Saturn in the 10th house; Venus in the 7th house; or any retrograde planet in the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses. All faults previously described for marriage ceremonies are also applicable and must be avoided for travel.

<summary>This page concludes the chapter on travel (Yatra), detailing the specific astrological conditions, planetary positions, and lunar days that must be avoided to ensure a safe journey and a successful return home.</summary>

"Regarding portents and the burning of the path, etc." and "The moon with malefic planets, etc." These previously mentioned defects are to be avoided. Among these, the defects of the month (such as the Sun being in Sagittarius) or the defect of the seventh house (being devoid of Venus) are not the only considerations. ॥ 107 ॥ 108 ॥ 109 ॥

Thus ends the Chapter on Travel in the commentary on the *Muhurta Chintamani* composed by the illustrious Mahidhara. ॥ 11 ॥

# Now Begins the Chapter on Architecture (Vastu).

A householder should perform all <term>Shrauta-Smarta</term> <note>Rituals derived from the Vedas (Shrauta) and those derived from traditional law codes or Smritis (Smarta).</note> rites within their own home. If performed in another's house, the owner of that land takes the merit of those actions. As stated in the <term>Bhavishya Purana</term> <note>One of the eighteen major Hindu Puranas, often dealing with future prophecies and duties.</note>—"All auspicious Shrauta and Smarta rites performed in another's house become fruitless, because the lord of the land enjoys their fruit." Therefore, the <term>Vastu Shastra</term> <note>The traditional Indian science of architecture and placement.</note> is explained.

> (Shardulavikridita Meter) If the village sign is the 2nd, 9th, 5th, 10th, or 11th from one's birth sign, it is auspicious. Multiply the value of one's own phonetic group by two, add the value of the other's group, and divide by eight to find the remainder. ॥
> These remainders are the <term>Kakinis</term>; in the difference between these two, the one whose value is higher grants wealth. Furthermore, the door is beneficial in the East for Brahmins, Vaishyas, Shudras, and Kings according to their respective zodiac signs. 1 ॥

According to the <term>Avakahada Chakra</term> <note>A traditional grid used to determine astrological compatibility based on the letters of a name.</note>, if the town or village sign is the 2nd, 9th, 5th, 10th, or 11th from the name-sign, it is auspicious for residence; otherwise, it is not. To calculate the <term>Garuda-varga</term> <note>Phonetic groupings of the alphabet used in calculations; 'Garuda' is the first group.</note>, take the number of the group corresponding to the first letter of the person's name, double it, add the village's group number, and divide by 8. The remainder is the person's <term>Kakini</term> <note>A unit of measurement or point-value used to determine the strength of a location versus an individual.</note>. Similarly, double the village's group number, add the person's group number, and divide by 8; the remainder is the village's Kakini. The one with the higher Kakini is the provider of wealth; thus, it is better for the village's Kakini to be higher than the person's. "Regarding the door": Those with signs 4, 8, 12 (Cancer, Scorpio, Pisces) should face the East; Vaishyas with 2, 6, 10 (Taurus, Virgo, Capricorn) the South; Shudras with 3, 7, 11 (Gemini, Libra, Aquarius) the West; and Kings/Kshatriyas with 1, 5, 9 (Aries, Leo, Sagittarius) the North. ॥ 1 ॥

> (Vasantatilaka Meter) Taurus, Leo, Capricorn, and Gemini should reside in the center of the village. In the East: Aquarius, Scorpio, Pisces, and Virgo. In the West: Cancer, Sagittarius, Libra, Aries, and Aquarius. Similarly, the fifth groups from one's own are strong in the directions starting from the East. ॥ 2 ॥

In the consideration of settling a new village, the entire boundary is divided into 9 parts like a cloth. In the center: 2, 5, 10, 3. In the East: 8. In the Southeast: 12. In the South: 6. In the Southwest: 4.

<summary>This page concludes the chapter on travel and introduces the principles of Vastu (architecture), emphasizing the spiritual necessity of owning a home and providing astrological calculations for choosing a village and door orientation.</summary>
<keywords>Vastu Shastra, Muhurta Chintamani, Kakini calculation, zodiac signs, house orientation, Shrauta-Smarta rituals, Garuda-varga</keywords>

--- Page 157 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Vastu Shastra (architectural astrology) from the Muhurta Chintamani, focusing on the directional compatibility of house owners and the mathematical calculation of the 'Pinda' (building area/volume).</meta>

<page-num>140</page-num>
# ->The Muhurta Chintamani<-

In the West 9, in the Northwest 7, in the North 1, and in the Northeast 11—one should not reside there. There are 8 groups of letters starting with 'A' <note>The Sanskrit alphabet is divided into groups (Vargas) assigned to directions</note> which are powerful in their respective directions. For example: 'A' group in the East, 'Ka' group in the Southeast, 'Cha' group in the South, 'Ta' group in the Southwest, 'Ta' (dental) group in the West, 'Pa' group in the Northwest, 'Ya' group in the North, and 'Sha' group in the Northeast. The fifth group from one's own is the enemy; for instance, the fifth from the East (Garuda/Eagle) is the West (Serpent), which is an enemy. He whose letter-group is powerful in the East should not reside in a house with a West-facing door. || 2 ||

> (Indravajra Meter) Multiply the desired Nakshatra (minus one) by 152; add the desired Aya (minus one) multiplied by 81; add 17 and divide the total by 216. The remainder is the **Pinda** (the building's dimensional constant). || 3 ||
> (Indravajra Meter, First Half) From the desired Nakshatra comes the length; dividing the Pinda by the length gives the width, and dividing by the width gives the length.

The <term>Bhumi-Griha-Upayogi</term> <note>Land suitable for housing</note> can be of many types: even, odd, <term>Tryasra</term> <note>triangular</note>, <term>Chaturasra</term> <note>quadrangular/square</note>, etc. One must examine the compatibility between the owner's name and the house's <term>Nakshatra</term> <note>lunar mansion/star sign</note> just as one examines the <term>Melapaka</term> <note>astrological matchmaking</note> between a bride and groom. To calculate: take the number of the <term>Nakshatra</term> assigned to the name, subtract 1, and multiply by 152. Then take the desired <term>Vastu</term> <note>architectural type</note> (such as Dhvaja, etc.), subtract 1, multiply by 81, and add this to the previous sum. Add 17 more and divide by 216. The remainder is the <term>Pinda</term>. This should be done according to the householder's desired income/benefit. (By dividing the Pinda by the length, the width is found; by dividing by the width, the length is found). 

**Example:** For a person named Neelakantha, his <term>Nakshatra</term> is <term>Anuradha</term>. To check the <term>Melapaka</term> with the star Rohini: the desired star Rohini is 4. The <term>Vastu</term> is the third (Lion/Simha), which is odd. Subtract 1 from the star number (4-1=3), multiply by 152 to get 456. For the desired Vastu (3), subtract 1 and multiply by 81 to get 162. Adding these gives 635 <note>The text says 635, though 456+162+17 is 635</note>. Dividing this by 216, the remainder is 203. Now, if the chosen length is 29, dividing 203 by 29 gives a width of 7. If the width is 7, the length is 29. For a "Great House" (Mahagriha), the area including the desired Vastu is 216; by adding the desired 1, 2, or 3, the area of the Great House is determined. || 3 ||

> (Indravajra Meter, Second Half) The Aya (income/type) are Dhvaja, Dhuma, Hari (Lion), Shvan (Dog), Gau (Bull), Khara (Donkey), Ibha (Elephant), and Dhvanksha (Crow), determined by the remainder when the Pinda is divided by eight. || 4 ||
> (Upajati Meter) Dhvaja and others are in all directions; the face of the Dhvaja should be made in the East, the Lion in the South and North... or the Bull in the East, the Elephant in the East and South... for Brahmins and other castes. || 5 ||

When the Pinda is divided by eight, the remainder determines the <term>Dhvajadi Vastu</term> (the eight architectural archetypes): 
1. <term>Dhvaja</term> (Flag) 
2. <term>Dhuma</term> (Smoke) 
3. <term>Simha</term> (Lion) 
4. <term>Shvan</term> (Dog/Crow) 
5. <term>Vrisha</term> (Bull) 
6. <term>Gardabha</term> (Donkey) 
7. <term>Gaja</term> (Elephant) 
8. <term>Kaka</term> (Crow) 
These are the names of the Vastu types. In Dhvaja...

<page-num>141</page-num>
<header>With Commentary—Chapter 12.</header>

In a *Simha* <note>Lion-type</note> house, doors may be placed in all directions. In a *Vrishabha* <note>Bull-type</note> house, doors should be in the East, South, and North. In a *Gaja* <note>Elephant-type</note> house, doors should be in the East and South. Even-numbered architectural layouts are forbidden; odd-numbered ones are auspicious. || 4 || 5 ||

(Upajati Meter) **If the Sun, Moon, Jupiter, or Venus are weak, set, or in their debilitation relative to the house owner's birth sign, destruction follows for the owner, his wife, happiness, and wealth respectively. If the day's constellation or the house's constellation is directly in front or behind the builder, there will be no stability and a risk of theft.** || 6 ||

If the Sun is weak relative to the house owner's birth sign, the owner is destroyed; if the Moon is weak, his wife; if Jupiter, his happiness; and if Venus, his wealth is destroyed. One should not reside in a house if the day's constellation <term>Nakshatra</term> or the house's constellation is directly facing the person. If these constellations are at the back, it is also not suitable, as it brings fear of theft <note>original: "kummal"</note> or housebreaking. This means the directional divisions of the constellations should be at the sides, as previously described. The 7 constellations starting from Krittika are East; the 7 from Magha are South; the 7 from Anuradha are West; and the 7 from Dhanishtha are North. || 6 ||

(Upajati Meter) **Take the remainder after dividing the house constellation by 8; this is the "Expenditure." Add the numerical value of the letters of the house name (Dhruva, etc.) to this sum. Divide this total by 3; the remainders 1, 2, and 3 represent the portions of the Moon, Yama, and the King. Among these, the portion of Yama is not auspicious.** || 7 ||

If the house constellation is 8 (Rohini), and you divide by 8, the remainder is 8 <note>interpreted here as 4 in the commentary's specific example</note>, which is the "Expenditure." To this, add the numerical value of the letters of the hall's name. Divide the total by 3. A remainder of 1 signifies the Moon, 2 signifies Yama <note>the god of death</note>, and 3 signifies the King. Of these, the Yama portion is inauspicious. || 7 ||

(Anushtubh Meter) **The fixed values (Dhruva) for the halls in the directions starting from the East are 1, 2, 4, and 8. The sum of these hall values plus one determines the "Dhruva" status of the house.** || 8 ||

Method for calculating the Dhruva value: For an East door, the value is 1; South is 2; West is 4; and North is 8. Add the values for as many directions as there are doors, then add 1. This identifies the specific type of house (Dhruva, etc.). || 8 ||

(Pathyavaktra Meter) **If the resulting Dhruva values are 15, 12, 8, 16, 9, 11, or 14, the house name should have three syllables. If the values are 1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 10, 3, or 13, use a two-syllable name. For a value of 7, use a four-syllable name.** || 9 ||

From the previously mentioned directions, if the calculated Dhruva value (after adding 1) results in the numbers 15, 12, 8, 16, 9, 11, or 14, the house name must consist of three letters. If the value is 1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 10, 3, or 13, it should be a two-letter name. For the number 7, it should be a four-letter name. This Dhruva method is used for counting letters related to grain and other prosperity factors. || 9 ||

# Muhurta Chintamani
<note>The "Jewel of Thought on Auspicious Moments," a major 16th-century Sanskrit text on astrology.</note>

->*(Arya Giti Meter)* 1. Dhruva, 2. Dhanya, 3. Jaya, 4. Nanda, 5. Khara, 6. Kanta, 7. Manorama, 8. Sumukha, 9. Durmukha, 10. Ugra,<-
->11. Ripuda, 12. Vittada, 13. Nasha, 14. Akranda, 15. Vipula, and 16. Vijaya. || 10 ||<-

**Names of the Halls (Shalas):** 1. Dhruva (Constant), 2. Dhanya (Wealthy), 3. Jaya (Victorious), 4. Nanda (Joyful), 5. Khara (Sharp/Hard), 6. Kanta (Beloved), 7. Manorama (Beautiful), 8. Sumukha (Pleasant-faced), 9. Durmukha (Ugly-faced), 10. Ugra (Fierce), 11. Ripuda (Enemy-giving), 12. Vittada (Wealth-giving), 13. Nasha (Destruction), 14. Akranda (Crying/Lamentation), 15. Vipula (Abundant), 16. Vijaya (Triumphant). The results of these halls correspond to their names; for auspicious purposes, one should choose those like Dhruva and avoid those starting from Akranda. || 10 ||

->*(Upajati and Pathyavaktra Meters)* Multiply the area (Pinda) respectively by 9, 9, 6, 8, 3, 8, 8, 4, and 8.<-
->Divide these by 8, 7, 9, 12, 8, 27, 15, 27, and 12 respectively. || 11 ||<-

->*(Anushtubh Meter)* These yield the Income (Aya), Weekday, Part (Anshaka), Wealth, Debt, Constellation (Nakshatra), Lunar Day (Tithi), Yoga,<-
->and Lifespan. Furthermore, if the house's constellation and the owner's constellation are the same, it causes death. || 12 ||<-

| Aya etc. | Income | Day | Part | Wealth | Debt | Star | Tithi | Yoga | Life |
| :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| Multiplier | 9 | 9 | 6 | 8 | 3 | 8 | 8 | 4 | 8 |
| Divisor | 8 | 7 | 9 | 12 | 8 | 27 | 15 | 27 | 12 |

Multiply the area (Pinda) by 9 and divide by 8; the remainder is the <term>Aya</term><note>The 'Income' or orientation of the building</note>. Multiply by 8 and divide by 7 for the Weekday; multiply by 6 and divide by 9 for the Part; multiply by 8 and divide by 12 for Wealth; multiply by 3 and divide by 8 for Debt; multiply by 8 and divide by 27 for the Constellation; multiply by 8 and divide by 15 for the Lunar Day; multiply by 4 and divide by 27 for the Yoga; and multiply by 8 and divide by 12 for the Lifespan. 

An odd-numbered Aya is auspicious, while even is inauspicious. Friday is auspicious; malefic days are inauspicious. A malefic zodiac sign is censurable. If Wealth is greater than Debt, it is auspicious; if Debt is greater, it is inauspicious. The 3rd, 5th, and 7th <term>Tara</term><note>Astrological star-groupings</note> are inauspicious. If the house and the house-owner share the same constellation, it causes death. One should calculate the zodiac compatibility similar to marriage calculations: Ashvini and the next two are Aries; Magha and the next two are Leo; Mula and the next two are Sagittarius; other constellations are taken 2.25 each per zodiac sign. In house construction, the "Master-Servant" or "Friend-Friend" relationship and having the same <term>Nadi</term><note>Pulse/Channel in astrology</note> is auspicious. The "Empty" (Rikta) lunar days and the New Moon (Amavasya) are inauspicious. If the area is multiplied by 14 and divided by 30, the remainder is the Tithi. Malefic Yogas like Vyatipata are inauspicious. If the auspicious qualities are not met using standard hand-measurements, one should adjust the area by adding finger-breadths (Angulas); the method for this should be learned from the <term>Lilavati</term><note>A famous 12th-century mathematical treatise by Bhaskara II</note>. || 11 || 12 ||

### (Shalini Meter) <note>A Sanskrit poetic meter consisting of 11 syllables per line.</note>
> When beginning a house or other structure, counting from the Sun's constellation: three in the Bull's head cause burning; four in the front feet result in emptiness; four in the hind feet bring stability; three in the back bring wealth; four in the right flank bring gain; thirteen.
> Three in the tail cause the destruction of the master; four in the left flank cause poverty; three in the mouth cause constant pain. Counting from the Sun's constellation, seven are bad, eleven are good, and ten are bad. fourteen.

At the start of building a house, palace, or village, count from the constellation where the Sun is located to the constellation of the current day. In the "Bull-Vastu-Diagram" <term>Vrishavastu-chakra: a symbolic diagram shaped like a bull used to determine auspicious spots for construction</term>:
*   3 constellations in the Head: Result in fire/burning.
*   4 in the Front Feet: Result in "emptiness" or lack of fruit.
*   4 in the Hind Feet: Result in stability.
*   3 in the Back: Result in <note>Shri</note> prosperity/wealth.
*   4 in the Right Flank: Result in profit.
*   3 in the Tail: Result in the death or ruin of the owner.
*   4 in the Left Flank: Result in poverty.
*   3 in the Mouth: Result in constant suffering.
This is the Bull-Vastu-Diagram. In another method: from the Sun's constellation to the day's constellation, 7 are inauspicious, 11 are auspicious, and 10 are inauspicious. 13. 14.

---

### (Sragdhara Meter) <note>A long poetic meter of 21 syllables, often used for complex descriptions.</note>
> With the Sun in Aquarius during the month of Phalguna, east or west-facing houses are favored; similarly in Shravana when the Sun is in Leo or Cancer, and in Pausha when in Capricorn. In Vaishakha with the Sun in Aries or Taurus, and in Margashirsha with the Sun in Libra or Scorpio, north or south-facing houses are good. Using fixed, soft, Shatabhisha, Swati, Dhanishtha, Hasta, and Pushya constellations is auspicious for starting a house; however, for a maternity room, beginning in Punarvasu and entering in Shravana or Abhijit is recommended. 15.

When the Sun is in Aquarius (February/March), east-west doors are auspicious. When the Sun is in Leo or Cancer (July/August) or Capricorn (December/January), east-west doors are also auspicious. When the Sun is in Aries or Taurus (April/May) or Libra or Scorpio (November/December), north-south doors are auspicious. The "fixed" <note>Rohini and the three Uttaras</note> and "soft" <note>Chitra, Anuradha, Mrigashirsha, Revati</note> constellations, along with Shatabhisha, Swati, Dhanishtha, Hasta, and Pushya, are good for starting construction. But for a delivery room <note>Sutika-griha</note>, it is said to start in Punarvasu and enter during Shravana or Abhijit. 15.

---

### (Shardulavikridita Meter) <note>A "tiger-play" meter of 19 syllables.</note>
> Some say a house is auspicious in Aries (Chaitra), Taurus (Jyeshtha), Cancer (Ashadha), Leo (Bhadrapada), Libra (Ashvin), and Capricorn (Pausha). It is said to be auspicious in Aquarius (Magha), but not in Gemini, Virgo, or Sagittarius, according to the lunar months. 16.

### (Upajati Meter) <note>A common 11-syllable meter.</note>
> From the full moon, east-facing; from the ninth day, north-facing; from the new moon, west-facing; and from the ninth of the bright fortnight, they say south-facing is not auspicious. 17.

If a house is built from the Full Moon until the eighth day of the dark fortnight, do not make it north-facing.

<header>The Jewel of Auspicious Moments (Muhurta Chintamani)</header>

Until the eighth day of the bright fortnight <note>the waxing moon</note>, facing West is not auspicious. From the ninth day of the bright fortnight until the fourteenth, one should not face South. The placement of the door should be determined using the Vastu Chakra <note>architectural energy grid</note> consisting of 81 squares; it is said that placement in a "benefic" section brings good results, while an "malefic" section brings misfortune. || 16 || 17 ||

### (Verse) Construction should avoid Tuesday, Sunday, the "Empty" dates (4th, 9th, 14th), the New Moon, the 1st, and the 8th. Avoid the five constellations starting from Dhanishta and avoid movable signs. ||
### Benefic planets should not be in the 12th or 8th houses; malefic planets are auspicious in the 3rd, 6th, and 11th houses. || 18 ||

Construction of a house should be started by avoiding Tuesday and Sunday, the *Rikta* <term>Empty dates: 4th, 9th, and 14th of the lunar month</term> tithis, the New Moon, the 1st, and the 8th. One must also avoid the *Panchak* <note>a period of five specific constellations considered inauspicious for new beginnings</note> starting from the Dhanishta constellation, and avoid movable ascendants. Furthermore, in the birth chart of the project, benefic planets are auspicious if they are not in the 12th or 8th houses, while malefic planets are best situated in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses. || 18 ||

### (Verse) For temples, houses, and water reservoirs, the mouth of Rahu moves counter-clockwise from the Northeast. ||
### From the Sun's entry into Pisces, Leo, and Capricorn, in groups of three signs, the digging is auspicious at the back, away from the mouth. || 19 ||

->The Cycle of Rahu's Mouth<-

| Direction | Northeast | Northwest | Southwest | Southeast |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **For Temples** | 12, 1, 2<br>Sun in Pisces, Aries, Taurus<br>Rahu's Mouth | 3, 4, 5<br>Sun in Gemini, Cancer, Leo<br>Rahu's Mouth | 6, 7, 8<br>Sun in Virgo, Libra, Scorpio<br>Rahu's Mouth | 9, 10, 11<br>Sun in Sagitt., Capric., Aquar.<br>Rahu's Mouth |
| **For Houses** | 5, 6, 7<br>Sun in Leo, Virgo, Libra<br>Rahu's Mouth | 8, 9, 10<br>Sun in Scorpio, Sagitt., Capric.<br>Rahu's Mouth | 11, 12, 1<br>Sun in Aquar., Pisces, Aries<br>Rahu's Mouth | 2, 3, 4<br>Sun in Taurus, Gemini, Cancer<br>Rahu's Mouth |
| **For Reservoirs** | 10, 11, 12<br>Sun in Capric., Aquar., Pisces<br>Rahu's Mouth | 1, 2, 3<br>Sun in Aries, Taurus, Gemini<br>Rahu's Mouth | 4, 5, 6<br>Sun in Cancer, Leo, Virgo<br>Rahu's Mouth | 7, 8, 9<br>Sun in Libra, Scorpio, Sagitt.<br>Rahu's Mouth |

When beginning temple construction, one should know that Rahu's mouth stays in the intermediate directions (Northeast, etc.) in a reverse order for three zodiac signs each, starting from the Sun in Pisces. For starting a house, this begins from the Sun in Leo, and for water reservoirs, from the Sun in Capricorn, as shown in the table. The purpose of this is that the *Khata* <note>groundbreaking or purification of the earth</note> should not be done in the direction where Rahu's mouth is facing. The direction opposite the mouth is Rahu's tail; the "back" lies between the mouth and the tail <note>and is considered the safest place to begin digging</note>.

Excavating from the "back" <note>the side opposite the head of the Vastu Purusha or Rahu</note> is auspicious. For example, in the excavation for a temple, during the months of Chaitra, Vaishakha, and Jyeshtha <note>approximately March to June</note>, the mouth of Rahu <note>the celestial serpent</note> is in the Northeast and the tail is in the Southwest; therefore, in reverse order, the "back" is in the Southeast. Thus, excavation should begin there. || 19 ||

->**Shalini Meter**<-
> In a well or a dwelling, placement in the center causes loss of wealth. From the Northeast onwards, the results are: nourishment, increase in lordship, loss of a son, loss of a wife, death, wealth, pain from enemies, and happiness. || 20 ||

**Well:** A well in the center of the house causes loss of wealth. Following the path of creation starting from the Northeast, the results are as follows: in the Northeast, nourishment; in the East, increase in prosperity; in the Southeast, loss of a son; in the South, loss of a wife; in the Southwest, death of the master of the house; in the West, auspiciousness; in the Northwest, trouble from enemies; and in the North, happiness <note>The text uses 'mukha' or face, implying a positive outcome or source of joy</note>. || 20 ||

->**Vasantatilaka Meter**<-
> The rooms for bathing, cooking, sleeping, weapons, eating, grain storage, wealth, and worship should be placed starting from the East. In the intermediate spaces between them: churning, ghee storage, latrine, study, pleasure, weeping, medicine, and general storage. || 21 ||

In a square house, one should establish the place for bathing in the East, the kitchen in the Southeast, the bedroom in the South, the armory <note>shastra</note> in the Southwest, the dining room in the West, the granary in the Northwest, and the treasury in the North. A stable for animals is also auspicious in the Northwest. Regarding the intermediate points between the cardinal and ordinal directions: between East and Southeast, the place for churning curd; between Southeast and South, the storage for ghee <note>clarified butter</note>; between South and Southwest, the latrine; between Southwest and West, the schoolroom or study; between West and Northwest, the room for "weeping" <note>a space designated for mourning or expressing grief/heat</note>; between North and Northwest, the room for intimacy; between North and Northeast, the place for medicines; and between Northeast and East, the place for all other miscellaneous items. || 21 ||

->**Upajati Meter**<-
> If Jupiter is in the Ascendant, the Sun in the sixth, Mercury in the seventh, Venus in the fourth, and Saturn in the third, the house shall stand for a hundred years. If the Sun is in the third, Mars in the sixth, and Jupiter in the fifth from the Ascendant, the life of the house is two hundred years. || 22 ||

**Longevity of the House:** If at the time of beginning construction, Jupiter is in the first house (Ascendant), the Sun is in the sixth, Mercury is in the seventh, Venus is in the fourth, and Saturn is in the third, the house will have a lifespan of 100 years. Furthermore, if the Sun is in the third, Mars is in the sixth, and Jupiter is in the fifth, the house's lifespan will be 200 years. These are the planetary combinations for longevity. || 22 ||

# Muhurta Chintamani
<page-num>146</page-num>

->If Venus is in the Ascendant <note>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</note>, Mercury is in the tenth house, and the Sun is in the eleventh, with Jupiter in any of the angles <note>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note>, the house will last for one hundred years. If Jupiter is in the fourth house, the Moon in the tenth, and Mars and Saturn are in the eleventh house, the house will last for eighty years. || 23 ||<-

If Venus is in the Ascendant, Mercury in the tenth, and the Sun in the eleventh, with Jupiter in a central house (excluding the Ascendant), the house's lifespan is 100 years. If Jupiter is in the fourth, the Moon in the tenth, and Mars and Saturn are in the eleventh, the house's lifespan is 80 years. || 23 ||

->If Venus is in its sign of exaltation in the Ascendant, or if Jupiter is exalted in the fourth house, or if Saturn is exalted in the eleventh house, that house will be filled with wealth <note>Lakshmi: the goddess of wealth and prosperity</note> and remain stable for a very long time. || 24 ||<-

If exalted Venus is in the 1st house, or exalted Jupiter is in the 4th, or exalted Saturn is in the 11th house, the home will be blessed with prosperity and endure for ages. || 24 ||

->If any planet is in an enemy's division <note>Navamsha: a 1/9th divisional chart used for fine-tuning strength</note> in the seventh or tenth house from the Ascendant of the house-starting ceremony, the house will pass into another's hands within a year, provided the Lord of the Caste <note>Varnesha: a specific planetary ruler assigned to the owner's social class</note> is weak. || 25 ||<-

If even one planet is in an enemy's Navamsha in the 7th or 10th house at the start of construction, the house will be sold or lost within a year. However, if the Lord of the Caste is strong, this negative result does not occur. || 25 ||

->Construction done during the constellations of Pushya, the Fixed stars <note>Dhruva: refers to Rohini and the three Uttaras</note>, Mrigashira, Shravana, Ashlesha, or Purvashadha, when joined by Jupiter or on a Thursday, brings sons and political power. Construction during Vishakha, Ashwini, Chitra, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, or Ardra, when joined by Venus or on a Friday, brings abundant food and wealth. || 26 ||<-

If a house is built when Jupiter is in Pushya, the Fixed stars, Mrigashira, Shravana, Ashlesha, or Purvashadha, or on a Thursday, it grants the family sons and status. If Venus is in Vishakha, Ashwini, Chitra, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, or Ardra, or on a Friday, the house will be full of grain and money. || 26 ||

->If the constellations Hasta, Pushya, Magha, Revati, Purvashadha, or Mula are joined by Mars, or if it is a Tuesday, it brings distress from fire and suffering to sons. If Mercury is in Rohini, Ashwini, or the Uttara stars... || 27 ||<-

If Mars is in Hasta, Pushya, Magha, Revati, Purvashadha, or Mula, or if construction begins on a Tuesday, the house will suffer from fire and the sons will face hardships. If Mercury is in Rohini, Ashwini, or the Uttara stars on a Wednesday, it brings happiness and sons. || 27 ||

<summary>This page details astrological combinations that determine the lifespan of a house, its financial prosperity, and the potential for losing the property to others. It also lists specific auspicious and inauspicious constellations (Nakshatras) and weekdays for beginning construction.</summary>
<keywords>Vastu Vidya, Muhurta, house longevity, planetary exaltation, Varnesha, Nakshatras, architectural astrology</keywords>

--- Page 164 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for house construction and positioning from the previous section, concluding the chapter on Architecture (Vastu) and beginning the chapter on House Entry (Grihapravesha).</meta>

<page-num>147</page-num>
<header>With Commentary—Chapter 13.</header>

If Mercury is situated in the constellations of Phalguni, Chitra, or Hasta, and it is also a Wednesday, then the house shall grant happiness and sons. || 27 ||

> (Verse in Anushtubh meter) If Saturn is in Purva Bhadrapada, Uttara Bhadrapada, Jyeshtha, Anuradha, Revati, Swati, or Bharani, and the day is also Saturday, the house will be possessed by demons and ghosts. || 28 ||

Purva Bhadrapada, Uttara Bhadrapada, Jyeshtha, Anuradha, Revati, Swati, or Bharani—if Saturn is in any of these constellations and the day is Saturday, that house remains inhabited by demons, spirits, and the like. || 28 ||

> (Verse in Shardulavikridita meter) Counting from the Sun's constellation, four constellations are in the head, yielding wealth; then eight in the corners, causing displacement; then eight in the door-frames, bringing happiness; three in the threshold, causing the death of the master; and four in the center, bringing happiness. Having observed this wheel, the wise should place the auspicious door. || 29 ||

->Thus ends the Chapter on Architecture (Vastu) in the Muhurta Chintamani,<-
->composed by Rama, son of the illustrious astrologer Ananta. || 12 ||<-

According to some opinions, the "Door-Wheel" <note>A diagram used to determine the auspicious placement of a door based on the stars</note> is as follows: counting from the Sun’s constellation to the Moon’s constellation, the first 4 constellations are in the "head" and bring the attainment of wealth (Lakshmi). The next 8 are in the four corners (Uddhasana), meaning no one can remain in the house. Then, 8 in the door-frames (shakhas) bring happiness. 3 in the threshold (dehali) cause the death of the house owner. Finally, 4 in the center bring happiness. Other texts also mention the five limbs of the calendar (Panchang): Ashwini, Chitra, Uttara, Swati, Revati, and Rohini are auspicious for the door-frames and threshold. The 5th, 7th, 9th, and 8th lunar days are auspicious; the 11th, 12th, 13th, and 14th are middling; other dates are inauspicious. Auspicious day-alignments should also be chosen. || 29 ||

->Thus ends the Chapter on Architecture in the vernacular commentary on the Muhurta Chintamani by the illustrious Mahidhara. || 12 ||<-

### Now Begins the Chapter on Entering the House.

> (Verse in Indravajra meter) During the Northern Transit of the Sun, in the months of Jyeshtha, Magha, or Phalguna, upon a King's return from a journey, or for a new house; it should be done on Nanda dates, under soft or fixed constellations, in the birth-constellation, or in an Upachaya sign, or a fixed Ascendant. || 1 ||

Regarding the auspicious timing for a King's return from a journey (Sapurva) or for the first entry into a new house (Apurva): it should be free from the combustion of Venus and Jupiter. In the faultless Northern Transit (Uttarayana)... <note>The text cuts off here, continuing the requirements for the timing of a house-warming ceremony</note>

# Muhurta Chintamani
<note>The "Jewel of Auspicious Time," a classic 16th-century Sanskrit text on Hindu astrology.</note>

( 148 )

Entering a house is auspicious in the months of Jyeshtha <note>May/June</note>, Magha <note>January/February</note>, Phalguna <note>February/March</note>, and Vaishakha <note>April/May</note>. The months of Kartika <note>October/November</note> and Margashirsha <note>November/December</note> are considered of medium quality. Regarding the "Doorway Constellations" mentioned in the verse beginning with "Place the stars in the four directions," one should choose the Soft or Fixed constellations <note>Nakshatras</note> corresponding to the direction the house faces. Entry is auspicious when the ascendant <note>Lagna</note> is in the 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th house <term>Upachaya: houses of growth and increase</term> from the birth sign or birth ascendant, and in fixed signs. In this matter, the twenty-one major flaws <note>Mahadosha</note> mentioned in the marriage rites must also be avoided. || 1 ||

*(Indravajra Meter)*
In an old house, or a new one rebuilt due to fear of fire or similar disasters, entry is also auspicious in the months of Margashirsha, Jyeshtha, and Shravana <note>July/August</note> because of their inherent goodness. The constellations Shatabhisha, Pushya, Swati, and Dhanishtha are also favorable. In such cases <note>re-entry or emergency rebuilding</note>, there is no necessity to consider the combustion <note>astadi: the period when a planet is too close to the sun to be seen</note> of Venus or Jupiter. || 2 ||

*(Upajati Meter)*
One should perform the worship of the Vastu <note>the spirit of the dwelling</note> and the offering to the spirits <term>Bhuta-bali: a ritual offering to elemental beings</term> on the days of Soft, Fixed, Swift, Movable, or Moola constellations. Regarding the purity of the ascendant: auspicious planets should be in the trines (5th, 9th), centers (1st, 4th, 7th, 10th), the 2nd, or the 11th house, while malefic planets should be in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses. || 3 ||

*(Indravajra Meter)*
Furthermore, the 4th and 8th houses should be free of planets. The ascendant at the time of entry should not be the 8th sign from one's birth sign or birth ascendant. One should avoid Sundays, Tuesdays, the "Empty" lunar dates <note>Rikta: 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar cycle</note>, the New Moon <note>Darsha</note>, and the months of Chaitra <note>March/April</note> and Ashadha <note>June/July</note>. When entering, one should proceed with a water-filled pitcher and Brahmins <note>priests</note> leading the way. The day must also be pure according to the "Bhakuta" <note>astrological compatibility</note> rules used in marriage. || 4 ||

*(Indravajra Meter)*
The Sun is auspicious in the 1st, 8th, 4th, 5th, 2nd, or 11th positions <note>relative to the house direction</note>. For an east-facing house, the "Full" lunar dates <note>Purna: 5th, 10th, 15th</note> are auspicious. For houses facing South, West, or North, the "Joyful" dates <note>Nanda: 1st, 6th, 11th</note> and others are favorable. || 5 ||

<summary>This page details the astrological requirements for Griha Pravesh (house-warming), specifying auspicious months, lunar dates, and planetary positions for both new and renovated homes. It emphasizes ritual purity, including Vastu worship and the symbolic entry with a water pitcher and priests.</summary>

<keywords>Griha Pravesh, Muhurta, Vastu Shastra, Nakshatra, Hindu Calendar, Astrological Houses, Ritual Offerings, Bhuta-bali</keywords>

### Chapter 13: Including the Commentary

| East Entrance | South Entrance | West Entrance | North Entrance |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Sun 8 | Sun 5 | Sun 2 | Sun 11 |
| Sun 9 | Sun 6 | Sun 3 | Sun 12 |
| Sun 10 | Sun 7 | Sun 4 | Sun 1 |
| Sun 11 | Sun 8 | Sun 5 | Sun 2 |
| Sun 12 | Sun 9 | Sun 6 | Sun 3 |

If the Sun is positioned in the five houses starting from the 5th house relative to the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>Lagna</note> of entry, it is considered <term>Vama Surya</term> <note>literally "Left Sun," a specific astrological alignment</note> for entering a south-facing house. If it is in the five houses starting from the 8th, it is Vama Surya for an east-facing house. If in the five houses starting from the 2nd, it is for a west-facing house, and if in the five houses starting from the 11th, it is for a north-facing house. Furthermore, for entering an east-facing house, the 5th, 10th, and 15th lunar days are auspicious; for a south-facing house, the <term>Nanda</term> dates (1st, 6th, 11th); for a west-facing house, the <term>Bhadra</term> dates (2nd, 7th, 12th); and for a north-facing house, the <term>Jaya</term> dates (3rd, 8th, 13th) are auspicious. || 5 ||

> (Verse in Shardulavikridita meter) If the Sun's constellation at the time of entry is in the mouth of the Jar, there is a risk of fire; four in the East causes displacement; four in the South brings gain; four in the West brings profit; four in the North brings wealth; four in the belly causes destruction; three in the base <note>literally "anus"</note> bring stability; and three in the throat bring permanent stability. || 6 ||

<term>Kalasha Vastu Chakra</term> <note>The "Water-Jar Architecture Diagram," used to predict the fate of a house based on the stars</note>—Counting from the Sun’s constellation to the Moon’s constellation: 1 in the mouth of the jar means fire; 4 in the East means the house will become empty; 4 in the South means profit; 4 in the West means financial gain; 4 in the North means strife; 4 in the belly means destruction of the inhabitants; 3 in the base means stability; and 3 in the throat means permanent stability. This chart must be considered during the entry ceremony. || 6 ||

> (Verse in Upajati meter) Thus, having entered one's own home during an auspicious Ascendant, adorned with canopies, flowers, and the sound of Vedic chants, the King <note>or householder</note> should honor the architects, astrologers, ritual experts, and citizens with land, gold, and clothes. || 7 ||

->Thus ends the <term>Chapter on House Entry</term> <note>Grihapravesha-prakaranam</note> in the <term>Muhurta Chintamani</term> <note>The "Thought-Gem of Auspicious Moments," a famous 16th-century astrological text</note>. || 13 ||<-

In the manner described, during a faultless Ascendant, the King—accompanied by auspicious signs, Vedic echoes, and a home decorated with canopies and flowers—having entered, should honor the (Architects) royal...

<summary>This page concludes the chapter on house entry rituals, detailing the Kalasha Vastu Chakra for predicting a home's future and prescribing the honorable treatment of the craftsmen and scholars involved in the building's creation.</summary>
<keywords>Grihapravesha, Muhurta Chintamani, Kalasha Vastu Chakra, Vama Surya, auspicious dates, architects, Vedic chanting, housewarming rituals</keywords>

<page-num>150</page-num>
<header>The Jewel of Auspicious Moments</header>

The patron should worship the carpenter and others, as well as the astrologer (who calculates the auspicious moments), the <term>Vidhighna</term><note>One who knows the sacred rules of construction</note>, those expert in house building and the rituals of offering to spirits <insert>Bhuta-bali</insert>, and the priest and other city dwellers, by giving them land, gold, and clothing according to their merit. || 7 ||

->Thus ends the Chapter on House Entry in the commentary written by Mahidhara<-

# The Concluding Chapter

> <note>Original verse in Shardulavikridita meter</note> In the city of Dharmapura, which is adorned by Brahmanas who study the six limbs of the Vedas, there lived a man named Chintamani. He was the crown jewel of astrologers and had exerted great effort in mastering the Great Commentary <note>The Mahabhashya</note> composed by the Lord of Serpents <note>Patanjali</note>. He was the author of various works on natal astrology, omens, and mathematics, and was honored by great kings. His intellect shone through his mastery of Logic, Rhetoric, and the interpretation of Vedic sentences. || 1 ||

The city named Dharmapura is located in the Vidarbha region near the Narmada river; it is the residence of Brahmanas who study and teach the <term>Shadanga</term><note>The six limbs of Vedic knowledge: Phonetics, Ritual, Grammar, Etymology, Meter, and Astronomy</note>. In that city lived the astrologer named Chintamani. He was the "Tilaka" (the most eminent) among those who know the stars. He labored intensely in the study of the *Mahabhashya* (the foundational work on grammar). He revealed through his writings the three branches of astrology: <term>Hora</term><note>Natal astrology</note>, <term>Ganita</term><note>Mathematical astronomy</note>, and <term>Samhita</term><note>Omens and natural phenomena</note>. He was highly respected by great kings, and his intellect was characterized by a playful mastery over Logic, Poetics, and the Mimamsa and Vedanta schools of philosophy. || 1 ||

> <note>Original verse in Shardulavikridita meter</note> His son was the virtuous Ananta, whose lotus-feet were saluted by groups of astrologers. He attained great fame and was like a sun upon the earth. For the pleasure of the virtuous, he composed the beautiful *Jani-Paddhati* (Manual of Birth), which destroys the doubts of the wicked-minded, and he wrote a commentary on the excellent *Kamadhenu-Ganita*. || 2 ||

The son of the aforementioned astrologer Chintamani became famous in the world by the name Ananta. His lotus-feet were bowed to by throngs of astrologers, meaning he was the supreme teacher of the science of light in his time. He was like a sun in illuminating the science of astrology across the earth and was highly skilled and expert in the composition of many texts.

<summary>This page marks the transition from the practical rules of house entry to the genealogical conclusion of the work, detailing the scholarly lineage of the author's family in Dharmapura.</summary>
<keywords>House Entry rituals, Chintamani, Ananta, Dharmapura, Vidarbha, Vedic sciences, astrology lineage, Mahabhashya, mathematical astronomy</keywords>

--- Page 168 ---
<meta>This page concludes the introductory or concluding remarks regarding the lineage of the author and the publication details of the Muhurta Chintamani.</meta>

<page-num>151</page-num>
<header>With Commentary in the Vernacular—Chapter 14.</header>

He who created the beautiful <term>Janma-Paddhati</term><note>A manual for casting horoscopes</note>, which teaches the calculation of planetary periods and sub-periods <gloss>Bhava-Dasha-Antardasha</gloss> and their auspicious or inauspicious results; he who established the proper method for constructing a birth chart <gloss>Janmapatri</gloss> and destroyed the wicked intentions of those ignorant of the true path of astrology; and he who also wrote the commentary on the <term>Kamadhenu-Ganita</term><note>A mathematical treatise for astronomical calculations</note> which facilitates the creation of almanacs according to the school of Aryabhata—all these works were performed for the pleasure of virtuous men, that is, for the sake of helping others. ॥ 2 ॥

<note>Written in the Prithvi Meter</note>
Then his son, the noble-minded Rama—the younger brother of the learned scholar Nilakantha—placing the lotus feet of Lord Ganesha within his heart, composed this very *Muhurta Chintamani* in the excellent city of Shiva <note>Varanasi/Kashi</note> in the Shaka year measured by the arms (2), the arms (2), the arrows (5), and the moon (1) <note>The numerical code for the year 1522 Shaka, approximately 1600 CE</note>. ॥ 3 ॥

The son of the aforementioned astrologer named Ananta, the noble Rama—who possessed a mind dedicated to gifting knowledge to his disciples—was the brother of the astrologer Nilakantha, a scholar of astrology, grammar, and many other sciences. Having enshrined the lotus feet of his family deity, Lord Ganesha, in his heart, he composed this book titled *Muhurta Chintamani* in the year 1522 of the Shalivahana Shaka era in the city of Kashi, the giver of liberation. A commentary on this work, titled *Piyushadhara* <note>The Stream of Nectar</note>, was composed in the Shaka year 1525 by the astrologer named Govinda, who was the son of Rama’s brother, Nilakantha. ॥ 3 ॥

->॥ Thus ends the description of the author’s lineage ॥<-

---

->Khemraj Shrikrishnadas,<-
->Director of the "Shri Venkateshwar" (Steam) Press—Bombay.<-

# Auspiciousness!

## Dedication by the Author of the Commentary

---

> ->Having placed the eternal lotus feet of the Guru within the heart,<-
> ->In the year measured by the oceans (4), the nines (9), and the earth (1) <note>original: "nava-abdhi-nav-bhumi" - a numerical code for the year 1949</note> of King Vikramaditya,<-
> ->Mahidhar Sharma, residing in the town known as Tehri,<-
> ->On the beautiful banks of the celestial river that followed Bhagiratha's chariot. || 1 ||<-

This is the introduction by the author of the commentary: In the capital city named <term>Tehri</term> <note>A former princely state in the Himalayas, now in Uttarakhand, India</note>, situated on the banks of the holy Ganges (Bhagirathi), <term>Mahidhar Sharma</term>—having meditated upon the lotus feet of the Holy Guru (the indestructible Supreme Soul) within the lotus of his heart—composed this <term>vernacular commentary</term> <note>original: "Bhashatika" - a translation and explanation in the local language, likely Hindi, to make the Sanskrit text accessible</note> of the <term>Muhurta Chintamani</term> <note>A classic 16th-century Sanskrit text on electional astrology, used to find auspicious times for rituals</note> in the year **1949** of the Vikrama Era <note>equivalent to approximately 1892-1893 CE</note>. || 1 ||

> ->At the request of Khemraj Shrikrishnadas,<-
> ->The jewel of the Vaishya lineage and son of Shrikrishnadas,<-
> ->This commentary on the Chintamani was produced in the pure common tongue;<-
> ->May those who are free from envy and understand the labor involved take it to heart. || 2 ||<-

He says further: I have created this commentary (in the simple language of the country) according to the command of the virtuous and all-knowing <term>Khemraj Shrikrishnadas</term> <note>A prominent 19th-century publisher based in Bombay (Mumbai) known for the Venkateshwar Press</note>. I have made it with a simple spirit for the benefit of the general public so they may understand it. May everyone embrace this with a pure mind, free from pride and jealousy, so that whenever they read it, they may gain the power to comprehend the <term>Muhurta Chintamani</term> (which is not easily understood by everyone). || 2 ||

->|| Auspiciousness ||<-

<summary>This page contains the formal dedication of a Hindi commentary on the astrological classic Muhurta Chintamani, written by Mahidhar Sharma in 1892 under the patronage of the publisher Khemraj Shrikrishnadas. It highlights the effort to make complex Sanskrit astrological knowledge accessible to the general public through vernacular translation.</summary>
<keywords>Mahidhar Sharma, Muhurta Chintamani, Tehri, Khemraj Shrikrishnadas, Vikrama Samvat 1949, astrology, Hindi commentary, patronage, Ganges river</keywords>

# Physical Description of the Artifact

The current image does not contain legible historical text or script. It displays a piece of coarse, woven fabric—likely **burlap** or **linen**—which appears to function as a protective cover, endpaper, or binding fragment for the manuscript.

The material shows significant signs of age, including:
*   Heavy dark staining and discolorations.
